  `tests/hooks/test_driver_lib.py`. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5584→5636.

### v3.132.0 (2026-08-05)
- **The transport model and the one-successor fence, built and tested but NOT YET WIRED (#927
  part 1, epic #871).** Read that scope line literally: this ships `hooks/` machinery with 96
  tests and **no caller** — `_cmd_handoff` is unchanged, so the boundary still behaves exactly as
  it did, and the fence does not execute until part 2 wires it. An earlier draft of this entry
  claimed the boundary "finally has" a fence; the pre-PR cross-model review caught that as a
  Critical, because the code and the claim disagreed. The one piece that IS live is the
  `session_mode` projection, which is enforced inside `_locked_state_update` and therefore active
  from this commit. `session_mode` was a hand-authored, permanent
  answer to a question whose answer changes: herdr can be present at campaign creation and gone
  by boundary four. It becomes `preferred_transport` (`pane_chain` | `inline`, durable) plus a
  per-transition EFFECT that is never stored as a field — it lives in an append-only two-event
  log, so a stale effect cannot become the next boundary's default. `transport_probe` reports its
  two tiers **separately** rather than as one verdict, and that is not cosmetic: campaign creation
  legitimately has no pane reference, so a collapsed verdict would report `inline` there and
  silently preserve the very default this issue exists to invert — an AC-1 regression the design
  gate caught before any code existed. Tier 1 (`herdr pane list`, no pane reference needed —
  `--current` is exactly what fails in a pane-less session) reuses the shape `_pane_inventory`
  already hardens; tier 2 requires the answer to BE about the requested pane, since an rc 0
  response describing a different one is not evidence about this one. **The #845 fence is BUILT
  here and wired in part 2**: the boundary and mid-child paths already shared the
  generation/claim/lease/ack machinery and differed only in a precondition, so only the
  precondition was added — no
  `kind` discriminator, because `_refuse_foreign_kind` documents that this entry point serves the
  handoff "which carries no kind at all" and would have made the boundary refuse its own record
  (D232). The riskiest branch is reconciliation, where `successor_pane: null` must NEVER be read
  as "nothing was created": with `split_attempted` recorded BEFORE the split, a null under a true
  marker is INDETERMINATE and is resolved by diffing a fresh inventory against the recorded
  `panes_before`, never by trusting the null — the Critical the cross-model design review raised
  against an earlier draft that had documented the double-launch window and called it acceptable.
  A pane is also not a running agent, so a recorded successor with no agent entry is
  `start_failed` rather than acked as alive. `session_mode` survives as a write-only projection
  enforced at the single locked writer (`_locked_state_update`), with a guard pinning that
  `_atomic_write` has exactly one call site — a second writer would bypass the projection
  silently. 96 tests added across `tests/hooks/test_launcher_lib.py` and `test_driver_lib.py`,
  including the AC-1 creation test, the indeterminate-relaunch refusal, and the
  unreadable-inventory park. The pre-PR cross-model review returned 1 Critical + 8 High + 1
  Medium, all confirmed against the code and all FIXED in this commit: a missing
  `split_attempted` marker no longer authorises a relaunch (only an explicit `False` does), an
  unreadable agent inventory parks instead of being coerced to "no agent", a duplicate
  `resolution_id` is refused, `_terminal_for` reports the LATEST outcome so an unpark cannot be
  repeated, an unrecognised transport now REMOVES a stale projection rather than leaving a
  rollback to run the opposite one, the guard fails closed on unreadable state, rc 2 is no
  longer presented as a missing pane, and the probe's default runner bounds the stream in the
  reader instead of checking length after buffering. Part 1 changes NO prose surface; the
  epic-run skill and `docs/multi-issue-driver.md` rewrite is part 2. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5488→5584.

### v3.131.1 (2026-08-05)
- **Three supervision CLI tests carried a dated time bomb and broke `main` an hour after
  merging green (follow-up to #948, epic #871).** `tests/hooks/test_supervision_lib.py` and
  `test_supervision_admin.py` freeze `NOW = 2026-08-05T21:00:00Z` and derive `LATER = NOW + 2h`.
  That is correct for the in-process tests, because every one of them injects that clock. Three
  tests handed `LATER` to a CLI **subprocess** instead, and a subprocess cannot take an injected
  clock — `supervision_admin` deliberately has no `--now` override, since it gates outward
  installs — so it read the real clock. At 2026-08-05T23:00:00Z `LATER` became the past and all
  three failed permanently, not intermittently: `declare` refused with "`until` is in the past",
  `effective` reported `attended-overdue` where the test expected `away`, and `nobody-to-ask`
  inverted its exit code. CI was green at merge only because it ran before that instant. Fixed at
  the boundary rather than at the constant: `_real_future()` / `_real_past()` build every
  timestamp that crosses into `_cli` from the real clock, while the frozen `NOW` stays for the
  injected in-process calls that want determinism. A sensitivity check confirms the assertions
  still bite — inverting the helper reproduces exactly the three original failures, so the tests
  were repaired rather than hollowed out. One guard added
  (`test_no_cli_test_hands_a_frozen_future_timestamp_to_a_subprocess`), narrow by design per
  CLAUDE.md §4 mistake #6: one exact pattern, in the CLI section of the two named files. A
  repo-wide sweep for the same frozen-date-plus-subprocess shape found only
  `tests/hooks/test_registry_prune.py` and `test_step_state.py`, and both are safe — the former
  passes `--now` INTO its CLI, the latter injects into a pure function. No workflow-spine change
  → no diagram REV. Suite 5487→5488.

### v3.131.0 (2026-08-05)
- **Supervision is declared, not guessed — `RAWGENTIC_HEADLESS` is retired (#943 Part A, epic #871).**
  Three code paths asked "is anybody watching?" by reading one environment variable that could
  say only present-or-absent: it could not distinguish the owner stepping out (reachable by phone)
  from the owner asleep, and a session could not clear it, because a process cannot un-export its
  parent's environment. Replaced by a declared workspace state
  (`claude_docs/.supervision.json`) written by three new commands — `/rawgentic:away [until]`,
  `/rawgentic:sleeping <wake time>` (a wake time is required; "unreachable forever" is not a state
  to leave a run in), and `/rawgentic:back`, the only thing that lifts the guards. Split across two
  modules for a measured reason: `supervision_lib` is read-only and **stdlib-imports-only**
  (test-enforced) because `context_meter` consumes it on a hook that runs every tool call, while
  `supervision_admin` holds `plan_lib.file_lock` across the whole read-validate-increment-write
  cycle and lands via `atomic_write_text(fsync=True)`, fenced by `--expected-revision` so a write
  computed against a stale read aborts instead of clobbering a fresher declaration. **Two
  predicates rather than one flag**, because the consumers need opposite safe defaults:
  `nobody-to-ask` relaxes when a declaration expires (it only picks which advice a
  context-pressure nag prints), while `installs-forbidden` does not — installing packages is an
  outward act, and a clock passing a stated wake time is not evidence anybody returned. For the
  same reason present-but-invalid is **not** absence: `ENOENT` under a valid root is the only file
  failure read as absent, so a corrupt file — or a supplied-but-unresolvable workspace root —
  keeps refusing installs instead of a config bug inverting the guard. Also adds the additive
  top-level driver-state `campaign_wait` object (`waiting_for_owner` / `waiting_for_reset`, with
  `clears_when` required) and its `build_goal_text` campaign clause, so a Stop-hook goal loop reads
  an honest pause rather than nagging one — deliberately NOT new `issues[].status` values, since
  that vocabulary is closed and enforced twice with three further closed sets keyed off it, and
  `additionalProperties: true` permits new fields, never new values. The retirement is kept honest
  in both directions: the bare token joins `RETIRED_VOCABULARY` **and** the exact-set assertion is
  retained with an empty allowed set, so any re-introduction fails loudly. Skills 21 → 24. Blocker
  routing while unsupervised — texting, consulting, action claims, `authority_permits`, the
  departure preflight, and any behavioural consumer of `campaign_wait` — is split out to #947 and
  gated off, so an absent owner currently gets an honest park rather than an autonomous decision;
  `docs/supervision.md` says so plainly. 121 tests added across
  `tests/hooks/test_supervision_lib.py`, `test_supervision_admin.py` and
  `test_supervision_campaign_wait.py`, including the asymmetry test that fails if the two
  predicates are ever collapsed and the inert-feature regression proving a bare `/rawgentic:away`
  still guards the hook sites. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5366→5487.

### v3.130.0 (2026-08-05)
- **Run-records now land exactly once (#888, epic #871).** Closes both measured halves of the
  telemetry-loss problem this issue absorbed. Dropped (#588): WF2 Step 14 gains the merge-grant
  sub-step — assemble, `usage_capture`, `summarize`, commit the JSONL line as `chore(telemetry)`,
  re-verify CI per-sha on the new head, prove it landed with `work_summary.py find --issue`, and
  only then merge — because Step 16 ran after the merge and orphaned the record when anything
  interrupted the gap (saystory epic #204 persisted 1 of 6 children). Duplicated (#355):
  `persist_record` blind-appended, so a Step-16 re-run double-recorded; it is now idempotent on a
  run fingerprint (`run_id`, else a digest excluding `generated_at`/`schema_version`/`timing` —
  `timing` because `_auto_embed_timing` recomputes it on a recovery re-run), no-ops with a
  one-line stderr notice at rc 0, and falls open to appending on an unreadable store since losing
  a record beats duplicating one — and the check plus the append now run under one `flock`, the
  shape `decision_log.append_record` already uses, so two writers cannot both miss and both
  append. Only a line that VALIDATES as a record can shadow a record, after review showed a
  `{"run_id": …}` stub could otherwise suppress the real one. Step 16 gains the `--no-persist`
  render-only path, and Step 14 proves this run's line landed with `git show --numstat` rather
  than the issue-scoped `find`, which an earlier run's record satisfies. 26 tests added in
  `tests/hooks/test_work_summary.py` + `tests/test_wf2_clarity.py`; the existing
  `test_each_line_is_independent_json` was repaired, as the guard would have left it passing on a
  single line while testing nothing it names; `references/step-14.md`'s prose ceiling was raised
  trim-first (4_975 + 256) for the genuinely new gated sub-step. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5340→5366.

### v3.129.1 (2026-08-05)
- **Ad-hoc pane handoff failures now name their cause (#731, epic #906).** `perform_handoff`
  derives `failure_detail` for every `failed_step` (`record()` auto-notes failed steps from the
  herdr error body; poll and pre-split exits carry inline causes), captures the tentative pane's
  output into the report as `pane_capture` before cleanup closes it, preflights the agent name
  via `herdr agent list` and refuses `name_taken` without splitting a pane (start-time race maps
  to the same name-specific step, never retried); the pane-handoff skill's failure table routes
  operators to `failure_detail` and a fresh `--name`. Tests: injected-runner coverage for the
  preflight, the race, capture-before-close ordering, tail truncation, and every note-less
  failure exit. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5303→5340.

### v3.129.0 (2026-08-05)
- **Behavioural selection gate for skill evals (#928, epic #906).** `hooks/skill_evals.py` turns
  eleven `evals.json` files from inert data into a runnable gate: `discover_eval_files` finds both
  sanctioned locations plus the skill-root spelling, `load_cases` accepts both on-disk schemas
  (`skill_name`+`evals` and the `peer-consult` stub's `skill`+`cases`), `classify_intent` separates
  the three intents already in the corpus, `skills_selected` parses a transcript for `Skill`
  `tool_use` blocks, and `judge` scores a case — with a **refuse** case counting the skill *firing*
  as the failure. The live `claude -p` spawn is an INJECTED seam, so all 49 new tests
  (`tests/hooks/test_skill_evals.py`) run in the ordinary lane; the transcript shape is measured
  against a real 1.5 MB session transcript, not guessed. Two classifier defects were found by
  running the real 38-case corpus rather than fixtures: a whole-text "does not" match called
  `pane-handoff` cases 1/4/5 refusals (inverting the gate for three dominant real phrasings — those
  negations are about what the skill does not do *internally*), and reading `slash` from
  `expected_output` matched any path-shaped token, mislabelling natural-language cases across five
  skills; `slash` now comes from the prompt, keyed on the skill's own name. Adds
  `skills/epic-run/evals/evals.json` — six cases covering the 403 description characters #909
  restored, which had never been exercised for selection, including both of that description's
  NOT-for-this-skill boundaries. Evals fraction 10/21 → 11/21. `docs/skill-evals.md` records the
  fourth AC's decision: a live verdict is a documented MANUAL gate, not a CI lane, because
  selection runs from the installed plugin cache and reinstalling while hook-using sessions live is
  prohibited (§7, mistake #5) — the same wall that stopped #909. The live end-to-end run is
  explicitly deferred, with the reason and the follow-up recorded in that doc. The Step-11
  cross-model pass (gpt-5.6-sol) returned 7 findings and all 7 were fixed, not deferred: `discover`
  now FAILS on an empty corpus instead of reporting `total cases: 0` and exiting 0; `load_cases`
  refuses a file with neither case key (truncation used to read as a deliberate stub); skill
  matching is namespace-aware, so `other-plugin:pane-handoff` can no longer satisfy a gate on
  `rawgentic:pane-handoff`; a refusal no longer passes on a dead session, and one that names the
  correct route requires that route to fire; the refusal regex is bound to clause order, so
  "Invokes pane-handoff but does not invoke clear-prep" is a trigger; and the manual procedure
  gained real containment (disposable workspace-less cwd + `--disallowed-tools`) because the corpus
  prompts are live requests and this CLI has no `--max-turns`.
  No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5254→5303.

### v3.128.2 (2026-08-05)
- **The last two hand-pinned skill-count strings are now computed, finishing the #271 conversion
  (#910, epic #875).** `CLAUDE.md` §4 mistake #2 named "All 7 config-driven skills" and "6 workspace
  management" as the residue the #271 guards never reached. Nothing read those lines, so both had
  already rotted — the real counts are 9 and 9 — and the #528 run record booked exactly that drift
  as a follow-up on 2026-07-20, where it sat for fifteen days. Correcting the numbers is a forced
  consequence of guarding them, not a separate cleanup. The fix extends the checker that already
  exists rather than adding a parallel guard beside it (`hooks/skill_registration_check.py`, #528):
  `CLAUDE.md` joins `SWEEP_GLOBS` — it is not "docs" in the excluded sense but the operating manual
  a model reads as instruction — and `pin:config-driven` is promoted into `COMPUTED_FAMILIES`, so it
  is checked against the tree instead of against its own copies. That promotion is the substantive
  half: consensus had failed in the only way consensus can, with README and its own test literal
  agreeing on 8 while the corpus carried 9, every copy consistent and every copy wrong. A lone pin
  trivially agrees with itself, which is why one occurrence reported OK for months. The two counts are NOT guarded equally, and both the manual and this entry now
  say so rather than implying parity: the config-driven count is computed, while the
  workspace-management count stays **consensus-checked, not computed** — no per-skill category
  metadata exists to derive it from, and inventing a taxonomy would add a hand-maintained surface
  while claiming to remove one. Cross-model review was right that `breakdown-sum` cannot close that
  gap, since a category running low still passes when another absorbs the difference (`[9, 8, 2, 2]`
  sums to 21 exactly as `[9, 9, 1, 2]` does), so the honest description replaced the overstated one. The checker already names the stale surface with file:line, so the failure message
  requirement needed no new machinery. Verified by reverting the prose: `pin:config-driven` reports
  `expected 9, stale at: ... README.md:460 (8), CLAUDE.md:221 (7)` and `pin:workspace` names
  `CLAUDE.md:222 (6)` against six surfaces at 9. Five new tests cover the promotion, the widened sweep, the
  fail-closed path that must never silently demote a computed family back to consensus, AC3's claim
  itself, and — at the reviewers' request — the config-loading **membership** rather than only the
  count, so an omission and a compensating addition can no longer cancel out. The change also exposed a latent bug in the checker's
  own fixture, which claimed two config-driven skills over a tree containing one — the single-copy
  blind spot reproducing itself inside the test suite. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV.
  Suite 5249→5254.

### v3.128.1 (2026-08-05)
- **Skill frontmatter descriptions brought under Anthropic's 1,024-char cap, two descriptions
  un-truncated, and `incident/SKILL.md` split into `references/` (#909, epic #875).** The headline
  find was not the cap: an unquoted YAML plain scalar treats ` #` as a comment, so `epic-run`'s
  description loaded as 131 of 534 chars — losing every trigger phrase after "cycle through all
  issues in epic" — and `pane-handoff` silently lost `Requires HERDR_ENV=1.`; both are now
  single-quoted and load whole. `pane-handoff` 1,174 → 864 chars keeping every dictated variant
  (#732) and the unprompted-run directive; backend install notes moved from `adversarial-review`
  and `peer-consult` descriptions into their bodies. `incident/SKILL.md` 537 → 216 lines, with the
  SEV-1/SEV-2 verification gate and the destructive-action approval rule deliberately kept in the
  always-loaded body rather than a lazily-read reference. New `tests/test_skill_description_budget.py`
  (present/string/non-empty, the 1,024 cap, and a truncation check that a length-only guard cannot
  make) and `tests/test_skill_reference_structure.py` (flat references, walked recursively; no
  plugin-root token); both `incident` ordering pins rebuilt on a new provenance-preserving
  `corpus.skill_files()` because the joined corpus string cannot express "same file", and verified
  by mutation. Follow-up #928 filed for the behavioural selection gate this repo still lacks.
  No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5208→5249.

### v3.128.0 (2026-08-05)
- **A task class (`disposable` | `internal` | `production`) is now resolved once from the issue body,
  snapshotted write-once, and rendered into every cross-model review and consult prompt (#761,
  epic #875).** New `hooks/task_class_lib.py` resolves the canonical `**Task class:**` line by
  prefix-then-validate (a line with trailing junk is *malformed*, never silently the config default)
  behind a CommonMark line-state walk, so an issue that merely documents the field — as #761 does —
  cannot set it; `os.link` plus a containing-DIRECTORY fsync makes the snapshot atomic AND durable,
  since atomic visibility is not persistence. `build_prompt` / `build_consult_prompt` gained a
  `task_class` argument that always renders and defaults to the strictest class, and
  `hooks/review_runner.py` gained `--task-class` / `--issue` on all three verbs. The load-bearing
  refusal: `--issue` WITHOUT `--task-class` exits 2 before any egress, because an issue-scoped review
  that quietly fell back to the project default would show the reviewer a class the issue never set
  with no failure and no diagnostic. Prose wired at four surfaces (WF1 draft contract, WF2 Step 1
  resolve+snapshot, WF5 and WF13 read-then-pass) and `defaultTaskClass` documented in
  `docs/config-reference.md`. The class is **inert**: reviewers are told it and told explicitly to
  apply the same rubric regardless — no demand scales, no gate relaxes. Shipped under owner override
  **D204**, which closed the Step-4 design gate after two escalations and turned pass-6's 13 findings
  into implementation constraints C1–C13; all 13 are folded into the design doc (Revision 7) with a
  constraint→location map, and the 7 High ones carry terminal ADOPTED dispositions. Scope is the
  owner's rescope (`issuecomment-5187270371`): AC1 + AC5 + AC2's injection half; AC4 and AC6 are
  #923's. Tests: `tests/hooks/test_task_class_lib.py`,
  `test_task_class_prompt_injection.py`, `test_task_class_integration.py` (the integration file drives
  a seeded snapshot through the runner's real prompt path with the snapshot's class and the config
  default deliberately DIFFERENT, so a silent fallback fails rather than passes, and additionally pins
  the four prose handoffs no Python test can execute), plus `TestTaskClassFlags` in
  `test_review_runner.py`; `tests/test_wf2_prose_budget.py` ceilings recalibrated to
  actual + allowed_headroom for `step-01.md` and the corpus total. The Step-8a wave over the three
  high-risk tasks ran two independent passes and found five real defects, all fixed rather than
  deferred: the runner trusted a caller-supplied class without checking the snapshot (resolved under
  owner decision **D207** as verify-if-present, since the reviewer's `design-flaw` classification
  collided with D204's no-further-design-passes override); Step 1's task-class instruction re-fetched
  the issue body with no exit-status gate, so a failed fetch could resolve an EMPTY body and snapshot
  that permanently; a non-string `defaultTaskClass` was silent where a bad string diagnosed; the
  issue-less WF5/WF13 path resolved the project default and then discarded it, so a configured
  `internal` could never reach a prompt; and `resolve`'s failure surface was a raw traceback rather
  than the `task-class: FAILED — <reason>` line its own docs promise. Step 11 then ran two passes plus
  the mandated adversarial diff layer and returned twelve more findings (0 dropped by band, 10 unique):
  nine applied, one declined on the merits, and one — the argument that the runner should refuse
  outright when no snapshot exists — left closed because D207 had already ruled on exactly that
  question. Two are worth naming because they were regressions in the Step-8a fixes themselves: the
  corrected WF5/WF13 instruction was contradicted by its own completion checklist, and gating the
  issue fetch left the `jq` body extraction unguarded, so the empty-body hole had moved rather than
  closed. Also fixed: a newly-created snapshot directory's own entry was never fsynced (durable
  contents inside a directory a crash could still lose); `os.path.exists` followed symlinks, so a
  dangling link read as "absent" and took the trust-the-caller branch; an excluded-candidate notice
  was suppressed whenever the config default was also invalid; and block-quote exclusion missed
  CommonMark lazy continuation, so quoted documentation of the field could set it.
  No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5080→5208.

### v3.127.1 (2026-08-05)
- **herdr re-pinned to v0.8.0 against the migrated `herdrdev/herdr` org, with every value 0.8.0
  changed re-measured from a probe rather than from the release notes (#886, epic #875).**
  `hooks/herdr-pin.json` moves all upstream coordinates plus the asset size and digest, verified
  bit-for-bit against the release API *before* any install. `api_schema.protocol` 17→19 — the release
  notes never mention a protocol bump and it skips 18 — read by running the digest-verified asset from
  a temp path, since `api schema` is served by the binary and not the server, so the new value was
  readable while the live server was still 0.7.5/17. `settings_footprint.reformats_whole_file` and
  `drops_trailing_newline` both true→false (upstream #2066), re-measured by running the shipped 0.8.0
  installer against a sandboxed `$HOME` seeded with a byte copy of the live `settings.json`: key order
  unchanged, trailing newline preserved, a 13-line diff where 0.7.5 produced ~300, and the live file's
  sha256 identical before and after. Two value asserts in `tests/hooks/test_herdr_pin.py` move in the
  same commit — the org/version pair, and the two footprint booleans the issue reported as unpinned
  when a test did pin them. Probed and deliberately unchanged: the claude integration is still
  current (v7) with a byte-identical hook script, and all seven pane/tab commands `launcher_lib`
  drives exist in 0.8.0. Dated Apache-2.0 addenda (relicense verified three ways: API `spdx_id`,
  `LICENSE` at the tag, `Cargo.toml`) on the supply-chain vet artifact and the console plan; no dated
  artifact body rewritten. A Step-11 review finding is adopted rather than waved through: subcommand
  *existence* is not qualification across a protocol jump, so every `launcher_lib`-driven command was
  re-run against the installed 0.8.0 and checked for the argv shape, parsed response fields and exit
  status the code actually consumes — recorded at `pin.cli_surface_qualified` with an explicit
  `not_qualified` note, and guarded by a new test, because `test_launcher_lib.py` runs on
  0.7.5-captured fixtures and so cannot detect a live shape change.
  No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5079→5080.

### v3.127.0 (2026-08-05)
- **A budget-exhausted design-gate close now requires a terminal disposition on every surviving
  Critical/High finding (#903, epic #875).** #798 let WF2's Step-4 gate self-close on budget
  exhaustion alone. Observed live on #874: the design source cap was reached with the breaker
  clear, so the close was available while a High design finding was unresolved and an AC was known
  unmet — the run refused it by hand and escalated. A second instance is on record (epic #667 child
  #665: budget exhausted with two findings still open, run proceeded, recorded as a deviation).
  New `plan_lib.severe_findings_are_disposed` makes "exhaustion over RESOLVED ground" checkable —
  each Critical/High finding in `--findings-file` declares `terminal_disposition`
  (`applied|refuted|deferred`), and `refuted`/`deferred` each need a non-empty
  `disposition_reason`, because a bare token would be a property asserted and never checked, the
  exact defect `findings_are_unambiguous` exists to fix. It is wired into `close-design-gate`
  AFTER the ambiguity check deliberately: an ambiguous finding must keep refusing for AMBIGUITY,
  or that older guard silently stops proving what it claims. The refusal is **self-repairing** —
  it names the field and its accepted values and is explicitly NOT an escalation condition —
  because a merely-correct refusal would have escalated every caller still emitting the old
  findings-file shape, reintroducing the six-consecutive-escalations problem #798 removed (AC4).
  Medium/Low need no disposition. The field is `terminal_disposition`, not `disposition`, because
  the ledger entry already carries its own top-level `disposition: "adopted"` — which means
  adopted INTO THE CLOSE RECORD, not that the recommendation was implemented; `adopted`
  deliberately does not auto-dissolve at the #393 join, so a refuted finding stays re-raisable.
  Bounded limitation stated rather than assumed: this boundary validates the findings it is GIVEN
  and cannot detect a Critical/High the caller omitted, nor verify an `applied` was truly applied.
  WF2 diagram REV 3.127.0 (station 04 delta). Suite 5031→5079.

### v3.126.0 (2026-08-04)
- **Reviewer confidence is now a native number the severity band filter can consume mechanically
  (#902, epic #875).** Ten consecutive cross-model review rounds returned `confidence` as a word
  ("high"/"medium") because the runner's OWN contract demanded words — the `FINDINGS_SCHEMA`
  declared an enum, `validate_finding` enforced it, and the prompt instructed it; reviewers were
  complying, and every round needed a manual word→float mapping before
  `plan_lib.SEVERITY_BANDED_CONFIDENCE` could run. The schema now demands `{"type": "number"}`
  (live-probed on the exact `codex exec --output-schema` invocation before design: native floats,
  rc 0 — external probe evidence recorded in session notes, not CI-reproducible; the checked-in
  strict-keyword ban test pins the schema discipline; range checked in `validate_finding` since
  `minimum`/`maximum` are strict-mode-rejected), the prompt asks
  for 0.0–1.0, and `adversarial_review_lib.coerce_confidence` handles the fallback: legacy words and
  numeric strings map through the ONE existing map `ADV_CONFIDENCE_TO_FLOAT` (AC1's second-map
  triple deliberately dropped per the issue's authority comment) with per-finding
  `confidence_source: native|mapped` and top-level `confidence_mapped` provenance — flagged, never
  silently native; garbage (bool, out-of-range, non-finite) refuses the whole review. The runner
  gives a word round ONE bounded retry (mirrors the #793 truncation retry) before accepting the
  mapped fallback — the GLM backend has no strict schema mode, which is exactly the path the
  fallback exists for. WF2 Step 11 prose now verifies provenance instead of mapping by hand; the
  golden prompt fixture was re-captured (and renamed to drop its stale `pre393` label). Tests: the
  full coerce/validate/normalize matrix, schema pins, runner retry/provenance CLI black-box, and a
  consumer test applying the banded filter mechanically to a mapped result. No workflow-spine
  change → no diagram REV. Suite 4959→5031.

### v3.125.8 (2026-08-04)
- **A closing keyword next to an issue number is now caught before the PR is opened (#901, epic
  #875).** GitHub's closing-keyword parser does not understand negation: a PR body reading "this PR
  does not close #N" matches `close #N` and shuts the issue on merge anyway. It has fired twice for
  real — #568 on the #573 merge (2026-07-21) and #874 on the #898 merge (2026-08-04) — both times on
  a PR that was deliberately `Part of`, with no closing keyword in any commit; the body prose alone
  did it. `hooks/plan_lib.py` gains `find_closing_refs` (a deliberately NAIVE scanner mirroring
  GitHub: keyword, optional colon, at most one line break, then `#N` / `owner/repo#N` / an issues URL
  / `GH-N`), `assert_pr_body_closing_refs`, and a `check-pr-refs` CLI wired into WF2 Step 12 item 4b
  and WF3's PR step 3b — both placed BEFORE `gh pr create`, since a check that runs after the PR
  exists cannot prevent the closure. Two fail-closed choices are load-bearing and pinned: omitting
  `--closes` means the PR closes nothing, so every closing reference flags (fail toward asking), and
  an empty `--pr-body-file` is rc 2 rather than a vacuous rc 0 — the same class as the #796 finding
  on `assert-pr-body`. Code spans are deliberately NOT exempted even though GitHub ignores a keyword
  inside backticks (verified live 2026-07-28), because a markdown span parser would be a new bug
  surface inside the guard that has to be the trustworthy one, and a keyword in backticks is a trap
  in the other direction too — an INTENDED closure silently does not fire. WF3's prose explicitly
  preserves its own `(closes #<issue>)` linkage, which Step 14 relies on, and a pin asserts that
  carve-out so the rule cannot drift into contradicting it. Both incident sentences are pinned
  verbatim; the prose pins anchor to `step-12.md`, never a `references/steps.md` path that #874
  deleted. The Step-8a cross-model pass then closed two fail-open gaps in the guard itself:
  commit MESSAGES were unscanned while the prose claimed the rule bound them (now `--commit-range`),
  and a bare `--closes 901` authorized `other/repo#901` because only the integer was kept (refs now
  carry their `owner/name`, and an unqualified declaration authorizes this repo only). Two further
  fail-closed fixes: a `None`/blank body no longer returns a vacuous pass, and invalid UTF-8 is
  rc 2 rather than a traceback whose rc 1 was indistinguishable from a real finding
  (`UnicodeDecodeError` is a `ValueError`, not an `OSError`). Step 11 then caught a **Critical**
  that made the whole thing inert: both workflows prescribed a `/tmp/...` body file, which the
  gate's own containment check refuses before opening, so the documented command returned rc 2
  every time — reproduced, then fixed by drafting to `./.rawgentic/<wf>-pr-body.md` and publishing
  that same file, since WF2 pointed `gh pr create` at the /tmp path and WF3 rebuilt its body from
  an inline heredoc (either way the gate could read one body while another was published).
  `--commit-range` also became required rather than bracketed — documented-optional is a
  documented opt-out of the fail-open — and the `\x1e`-delimited commit reader, which silently
  skipped segments a crafted message could hide a reference in, became a two-stage read where an
  unparseable commit id is rc 2 rather than skipped. The adversarial layer then caught the WF3
  ordering bug introduced by that very fix (item 3b checked a body item 4 had not written yet) and
  an over-claim in the code comment: a DECLARED reference inside backticks is skipped regardless of
  markdown context, so an intended closure silently does not fire — now named as a known gap and
  pinned by a characterization test rather than only in prose. `references/step-12.md`'s prose
  ceiling rises 13,287→14,999 bytes (actual + the 5% allowed headroom, per the #874 AC7
  convention) because item 4b is new prose; no other row moved. WF2 diagram REV 3.125.8 (station 12
  delta). Suite 4887→4959.

### v3.125.7 (2026-08-04)
- **A `gates[]` row for step 11.5 is now rejected at write time (#904, epic #875).** The run-record
  schema forbids it — Step 11.5's result lives in the `security_scan` section, and
  `CANONICAL_GATE_NAMES` deliberately carries no `11.5` key — but the rule was documented at
  `hooks/work_summary.py:76-77` and never enforced, so Step-16 assembly kept re-adding the row: **28
  of the 205 records** in the committed store carry one, and each renders a DUPLICATE Security-Scan
  line. `validate_record` now rejects such a row in STRICT (write-time) mode only, naming the
  offending index and pointing at `security_scan`, mirroring the `SCANNER_KINDS` vocabulary check.
  Strict-only is the load-bearing choice: `load_store` validates leniently, so a non-strict guard
  would have evicted all 28 historical records — verified against the real store, which still loads
  205 records with 0 excluded. Every write path is covered, because `persist_record` has exactly one
  caller and it is reachable only past the strict validation that returns 1 without persisting. This
  issue's originally filed symptom (a renderer that reported "Security Scan: not run" for a valid
  passing block) was **REFUTED** during queue revalidation and the issue rescoped by owner decision
  D187; the renderer is deliberately unchanged, and the 28 historical records are deliberately not
  rewritten (the store is append-only), so those summaries keep their duplicate line as a stated
  consequence. Tests: 7 new — strict rejection, index naming, the actionable `security_scan` pointer,
  the same record passing once the row is removed, the CLI write path exiting 1 without persisting,
  and two lenient-read pins that go red if the guard is ever moved out of strict mode — one on
  `validate_record`, one on `load_store` itself (the read path that actually consumes the store),
  the latter added from the Step-11 cross-model review. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV.
  Suite 4880→4887.

### v3.125.6 (2026-08-04)
- **The `project_switched` handoff gate compares `project_path` path-equivalently, and says which
  field disagreed when it refuses (#800, epic #875).** `registry_has_session` matched the registry
  row's `project_path` against the caller's expectation with a bare `!=`, but that value is written
  by a MODEL following the switch skill and it does not reliably pick one representation. Two
  consecutive `ad-hoc-handoff` runs with identical inputs (2026-08-01, both passing
  `--project-path ./projects/claude-skills`) had one successor write the relative form and pass
  while the next wrote the absolute form and FAILED — despite its own transcript saying "Bound to:
  claude-skills" — so a correct bind was torn down. No caller-side value fixes it: the variance is
  on the producer's side, which is why the fix is in the consumer. `project_paths_equivalent`
  compares `(is_absolute, normalized components)` and then resolves relative sides against a
  caller-supplied base, mirroring the convention two siblings already use for this exact value
  (`context_meter.bound_project`, `wal-lib.sh`); with no base the comparison stays exact, so the fix
  is opt-in per caller and fail-closed. A component-suffix rule — the obvious base-free alternative
  — was **refuted by probe** and is deliberately not used: `projects/rawgentic` is a suffix of
  `/other/ws/projects/rawgentic`, and `rawgentic` of `/home/x/rawgentic/projects/rawgentic`, both
  false ACCEPTS on a gate whose job is refusing the wrong project. The project LABEL comparison is
  untouched and stays exact, so #665's wrong-project protection is unchanged, and an equivalent
  false accept found mid-implementation (bare component tuples made `/projects/rawgentic` equal
  `./projects/rawgentic`) is pinned by its own test. Both call sites are fixed: `perform_handoff`
  passes its own `project_root`, and `retire_predecessor` — which holds none — derives one from the
  registry path, but ONLY when that path ends in `claude_docs/session_registry.jsonl`, because
  deriving unconditionally would turn `/x/registry.jsonl` into the base `/` and manufacture exactly
  that false accept. `registry_match_diagnosis` reports whether no row carried the session id, the
  label disagreed, or the path was not equivalent (naming the base), recorded over the same
  post-baseline tail the gate polls: previously a comparison that could never match was
  byte-identical in its output to a 120 s poll timeout, and this campaign published a wrong
  diagnosis off that ambiguity. Tests: 68 new — helper/gate/diagnosis units including both
  probe-derived false-accept counter-examples, the workspace-root derivation guard, and behavioural
  call-site tests at BOTH gates; the primary red reproduced the reported symptom through the
  reporter's own entry path. **The Step-8a cross-model review then found three more, all reproduced
  by execution before being fixed:** lexical normalization equated different directories when `..`
  follows a symlink (a `..` component is now refused outright, and a relative or `..`-bearing base is
  not used); the derived workspace root trusted ANY contract-shaped registry path, so a foreign
  workspace's registry could have authorized a teardown (the base is now cross-checked against the
  already-validated `position["repo_root"]` and dropped when they disagree); and a malformed registry
  line was silently skipped and then reported as "the successor never wrote its bind" — the very
  wrong-diagnosis class this change exists to remove — so corruption is now named as corruption.
  Fixing the first of those also exposed that every pre-#800 registry fixture wrote the SAME
  representation on both sides and left `repo_root` unrelated to `project_path`, which is part of how
  the defect stayed hidden; the shared retire fixtures now model production's shape. **Step 11 then
  had BOTH cross-model passes independently raise the same High**, and it closes a hole OLDER than
  #800: `retire_predecessor` used the derived workspace base when it was available and fell back to
  the exact comparison when it was not — but that fallback refuses nothing, because a row from a
  FOREIGN registry that spells `project_path` relatively (`./projects/rawgentic`) compares equal to
  the expectation before any base is consulted, so the gate passed and could authorize closing the
  predecessor pane. Verified by execution on a symlinked fixture, including that the pre-#800 exact
  comparison accepted it too. A trusted base is now REQUIRED at that gate: the registry must resolve
  to the recorded `project_path` inside the workspace holding the validated `repo_root`, the
  derivation follows `realpath` instead of `abspath` (so a symlinked `claude_docs` cannot vouch for
  the workspace it merely looks like it is in), and a `..` component in the registry path is refused.
  `--registry` is passed through unvalidated at all four CLI call sites, so provenance had to be
  established at the gate. No workflow-spine
  change (a launcher gate's comparison, not a station, gate, loop-back or lane) → no diagram REV.
  Suite 4812→4880.

### v3.125.5 (2026-08-04)

  `python3 hooks/driver_lib.py <anything>` imported and exited 0 with no output — a
  success-shaped nothing that was read live (2026-08-04) as a passing gate while the real gate

### v3.125.4 (2026-08-04)
- **WF2 prose budgets gained the symmetric STALE CEILING check, and the characterization-pin sweep landed (#874, epic #875).** Closes #874, the sixth M1 child, whose split shipped separately in #898. PATCH: guards and test-scope only, no behaviour change. **AC7:** the byte-budget guard was one-directional — it caught a file growing past its ceiling but never a ceiling left far ABOVE actual, so after the split shrank every file the provisional ceilings left paste-sized slack a ~30 KB regression would have fitted inside while the guard stayed green. `STALE CEILING` now fires when `ceiling - actual > allowed_headroom(actual)`, with `STALE TOTAL` as the aggregate analog. Margins are hybrid on purpose — per-file `max(256 bytes, 5%)`, aggregate `max(1 KiB, 2%)`: a percentage alone punishes small files (5% of 400 bytes is 20, which no real ceiling fits inside) and an absolute alone becomes disproportionately strict on large ones, while the tighter aggregate stops many individually-legal gaps accumulating into corpus-wide slack. Arithmetic is integer-only (no float drift), `OVER CEILING` and `STALE CEILING` are mutually exclusive by construction with a test pinning that, and the diagnostic names path, actual, ceiling, allowed and excess. Implementing it turned the live-corpus check red with **22 stale ceilings** — the guard forcing **AC5**'s recalibration rather than a regression — so all 24 rows are now `actual + allowed` (total 245,497 against 240,683 actual). **AC3 (owner decision 2026-08-04: conservative sweep):** deleted **7 tests / 8 assertions** that were pure prose wording with no structural half — the #344 design-artifact style sentence, the #330 DISPATCH capture example (a mechanism M0b **retired**, so no live run emits it), two `<probe-before-design>` sentences, one `<early-smoke-install>` sentence, the runFeedback wiring sentence, and a doc-heading presence check. **AC3's premise did not survive per-assertion classification, and that is the finding:** of ~154 assertions outside the six contract areas only 8 were pure wording; the rest carry structural or negative halves that must not go (the lane predicate mirroring `plan_lib.lane_decision`, the append-only rule that exists because a headless run lost 6 of 7 commits' audit trail, a zero-count negative guard against an observed multi-session race, the marker slot table, a constant-versus-prose drift guard). Classification was per **assertion**, never per test function, exactly as two review passes warned. New `TestSixContractAreasStillGuarded` is a completeness FLOOR proving the sweep removed wording and not a contract; it names guard **classes** so rewording cannot break it, asserts the executor-vocabulary area as a separately-counted **negative scan** rather than a prose pin, and names the sibling file that pins reviewer≠author instead of assuming silence means coverage. **AC1 is recorded as deliberately UNMET by owner decision** — research (Exa, Context7, Claude Code skills docs) settled that 500 lines is a soft performance guideline, Anthropic's own docs saying "it's fine to exceed 500 lines" for genuinely complex instructions; the stronger signal is words, where this SKILL.md is 6,292 against a 5,000 guideline, filed as **#899**. Both AC7 and the meta-check sabotage-verified; two mis-targeted sabotage attempts are recorded in the commits because a sabotage that does not create the condition proves nothing. **Step-11 cross-model review then found FIVE High fail-opens in these very guards, all fixed under an owner-authorized round** (the issue's loop-back budget was spent, so the review was diagnostic and could not self-authorize — it escalated, which is the gate working). Two were misclassifications in the AC3 sweep itself: the `<probe-before-design>` exact-invocation rule and the `<early-smoke-install>` run-the-check instruction pinned RULES, not wording, and deleting them left only a marker-presence test — so a proxy probe could have been presented as feasibility evidence, and the smoke check could have vanished, with every guard green. Both restored. The other three were fail-opens in the new code: the six-areas floor checked `class X:` SOURCE TEXT (an emptied class passed) and sibling-file EXISTENCE (an emptied file passed), and the budget guard SKIPPED its aggregate check on an empty mapping (an absent or mis-resolved directory measured as zero files and read as clean). The floor now parses the **AST** and requires real assert statements per area, reads sibling **content**, and refuses an implausible parse; the budget guard emits **EMPTY CORPUS** and returns immediately. Each fix sabotage-verified, and two mis-targeted sabotage attempts are recorded in the commits because a sabotage that does not create the condition proves nothing. No workflow-spine change (guards and test scope only) → no diagram REV. Suite 4802→4809.

### v3.125.3 (2026-08-04)
- **WF2's `references/steps.md` monolith split into one reference file per step (#874, epic #875).** Sixth M1 child, shipped as `Part of #874` — **AC1 (`SKILL.md` <500 lines) is NOT delivered and is not closed by this PR**; it is blocked on an owner decision recorded on the issue, so this PR carries the mechanical split only. PATCH: restructure with no behaviour change. `references/steps.md` (1,938 lines / 157,313 bytes, 18 `## Step` sections behind a 236-line preamble) became **19 flat files** — `step-00-preamble.md` plus `step-01 … step-16` including `step-01b` and `step-11_5`. **There is no `step-08a.md`: Step 8a is a level-3 heading INSIDE Step 8, so it lives in `step-08.md`** — the issue's framing implied 20 files, and the partition measured 19. Flat, not a subdirectory, because `tests/corpus.py:22` reads the FLAT glob `sorted(refs.glob("*.md"))` and a subdirectory would silently escape the corpus and un-pin every content guard (AC2's named hazard). **Correctness is mechanical, not asserted: the split refuses to write unless `concat(preamble, step files in glob order)` equals the original byte-for-byte, and that equality was re-asserted AFTER `sync_shared_blocks.py` against the `origin/main` blob** (157,313 = 157,313) because the sync writes into `step-12.md` and could otherwise invalidate an assertion that had already passed. Probed live beforehand with the exact `sorted(refs.glob("*.md"))` invocation: the split files occupy precisely the slot `steps.md` held, **in step order**, so corpus content pins AND slice pins survive and only direct-path location pins break — loudly, with `FileNotFoundError` at collection. `scripts/sync_shared_blocks.py` MANIFEST retargeted to `implement-feature/references/step-12.md` (the `render-addon` call site was at `steps.md:1427–1555`, inside Step 12) and the sync confirmed a content no-op. Location pins redirected across 7 test files, each to the ONE step file it owns (`test_wf2_error_and_ci` → `step-13`, `test_render_addon_block` → `step-12`, `test_wf_review_sites` → `step-04`/`step-11`, `test_bundled_agents` → `step-08`/`step-11`, `test_wf2_clarity`'s Step-8/8a/9/5 pins → their own files); two module-level constants use a LOCAL step-order concatenation, labelled for removal by AC3's sweep and deliberately not shared via `tests/corpus.py`, since a shared concatenator would outlive the sweep and re-monolith the pins. One negative guard was strengthened rather than moved: the "no `feature/<issue` instruction" scan now covers the SKILL plus EVERY step file, so a forbidden instruction cannot hide in a step the old single-file read missed. 19 provisional byte-budget rows replace the single `references/steps.md` ceiling (AC5), sized at actual + max(1 KiB, 10%) and explicitly recalibrated by the follow-up content commit. NOT in this PR: AC1 (owner-blocked), AC3's pin sweep, AC7's stale-ceiling class, and the block moves. Step-11 cross-model review returned 3 findings: one Critical **refuted with evidence** (it reported the file creations as missing, but the review base was the mechanical commit — the files are created in the preceding commit of the same PR, and 12/12 recent main commits are single-parent so the intermediate never lands alone), and two Medium **accepted and fixed** — the concatenation helpers and the negative scan accepted any matching set, so an extra, stale or renamed `step-*.md` could have altered a slice or let a pin match from the wrong step. Both now assert the EXACT ordered 19-name set, and the §4b ordering guard this PR adds also asserts the split files form one **contiguous interval in the FULL unfiltered corpus glob** — a `step-*`-filtered check would still pass with a non-step markdown file sorted between two step files, which is the exact residual that contaminates a slice. All three fixes sabotage-verified (a stray step file and an intruder sorted mid-sequence each fail loudly). One earlier full-suite run showed a single failure in `test_notes_size_handler.py`, a wall-clock timing flake untouched by this branch that passes 3/3 in isolation and did not recur. No workflow-spine change (prose relocated verbatim; no station, gate, loop-back or lane delta) → no diagram REV. Suite 4798→4802.

### v3.125.2 (2026-08-04)
- **All three version surfaces and both mandatory changelog tail tokens are now checked in one command (#822, epic #875).** Fifth M1 child; PATCH because it adds guards only. Folded into the existing version-pin test rather than adding a CLI, per this epic's note, so the one command is `pytest tests/hooks/test_adversarial_review_registration.py`. **THREE surfaces, not the four the issue body lists** — #866 M0d retired the `phase_executor` canary and `CLAUDE.md` already reads "The version lives in THREE surfaces that must match"; the body's claim was corrected on the issue during queue revalidation. **The gap was real and measured: desyncing `plugins/rawgentic/.codex-plugin/plugin.json` to 9.9.9 left the whole file GREEN at 36 passed**, because `test_plugin_version_bumped` read only `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` — the copy `CLAUDE.md` §4 mistake #1 says everyone forgets was pinned by nothing. It now compares both JSON files against this file's own `EXPECTED_PLUGIN_VERSION` literal (the third surface) and names each stale surface with its value. **AC3 is the criterion that killed #796 rev 4, so the tail-token regexes are validated against the LIVE corpus by a test rather than by assertion — and measuring the corpus first changed the design twice.** 130 of 227 `Suite` tokens are not plain `<int>→<int>` (there are `+1skip` forms, trailing parentheticals, and **45 entries using ASCII `->` instead of `→`**), so a naive pattern rejects the majority; and 112 of 338 entries carry no `Suite` token at all with 114 carrying no diagram token, all predating the convention, so the newest-entry gate is deliberately scoped to the newest entry rather than failing on a third of the corpus. The rev-4 boundary `(?![\w.])` is now quantified in a comment — it misses **198 of 224** token-bearing entries because `no diagram REV.` ends a sentence — and an explicit trailing-period test pins that it must keep matching. Two entries word their token differently (v3.31.1 `Suite unchanged 2556+1skip.`, v3.121.0 `the diagram REV is DEFERRED to M0d`) and are named individually in `LEGACY_*_EXCEPTIONS` with their wording, so a NEW miss fails loudly instead of being absorbed by loosening a regex. Fail mode is **fail-CLOSED** per `CLAUDE.md` §3 and stated in the docstrings: a missing Changelog heading, an unparseable entry, or a section yielding zero entries all raise rather than pass vacuously, since a heading-shape change would otherwise silently disable these guards. All three guards sabotage-verified before commit. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4793→4798.

### v3.125.1 (2026-08-04)
- **The `switch`/`new-project` session-registry append wrote to a RELATIVE path, so a bind from a drifted cwd silently created a registry in the wrong tree (#885, epic #875).** Fourth M1 child; PATCH because it is a fix with no behavior change beyond the corrected write target. Both bind templates already passed the ABSOLUTE workspace root as a `printf` argument for the line's own `cwd` field (`switch`'s `<root>`, `new-project`'s `<WORKSPACE_ROOT>`) and then redirected to a bare `>> claude_docs/session_registry.jsonl`. Step 5 assumes cwd is the workspace root, but nothing enforced that at the write and the Bash tool's cwd persists across calls, so a session left at `projects/sysop` created `projects/sysop/claude_docs/session_registry.jsonl` — exit 0, `tail -1` echoing the line back, indistinguishable from the documented "create it if absent" first bind. Reads fail loudly; that one write did not, and binding is the input to WFn resume, wal-guard and driver state. Each redirect now reuses the literal the command already carried, double-quoted so a root containing a space stays correct; no `$(...)` is introduced, so the expansion-free bind constraint holds structurally. **Two fixes the gates forced beyond the one-token redirect:** `<root>` had NO definition anywhere in `skills/switch/SKILL.md`, which would have made the fix vacuous (a reader substituting the cwd yields `>> "./claude_docs/…"`), so Step 5 now defines it at the point of use (Step-4 High); and `new-project` was violating its own Step-1 rule that `WORKSPACE_ROOT` is "used for all subsequent file operations" rather than lacking one, so its sibling `claude_docs/session_notes/` creation is absolute too. **The Step 8a cross-model review then caught the guard itself being fail-open** (High): the first version asserted `not target.startswith("claude_docs/")`, which `./claude_docs/…`, `../claude_docs/…`, `sub/claude_docs/…` and a single-quoted bare target all satisfied — the defect could have been reintroduced with the guard green, and `./` is exactly what a cwd substitution produces. Replaced with an exact per-skill allowlist plus a double-quoting assertion, sabotage-verified by injecting `./` and watching it go red. It also caught `why.md` overclaiming that a bad absolute root "fails loudly": only a nonexistent root or a missing `claude_docs/` parent does — an existing but wrong root containing one still misfiles silently. Measured precondition, recorded so the defect is not mistaken for unreachable: `>>` creates a missing file but never a missing parent directory, so the silent misfile needs the drifted cwd to already have `claude_docs/` — **18 of 49 project directories here do**, including the `projects/sysop` of the live report. Tests: `test_bind_append_target_is_absolute` (red-before-green) and `test_guard_rejects_every_bad_target_form` in `tests/test_bind_command_expansion_free.py`, plus a four-arm scripted verification for AC (i) proving a drifted-cwd bind lands in the workspace registry while the old form misfiles. `skills/switch/references/why.md` gains the cwd failure mode, the three rejected alternatives, and the guard's honest limit (it pins template shape; no mechanical check can prove a runtime-substituted root is absolute). **Upgrade note: already-misfiled records are NOT migrated** — the fix is prospective only. A stray `claude_docs/session_registry.jsonl` under a project directory means those sessions were never visible to anything reading the workspace registry; re-run the bind for any session that still matters and delete the stray file. No automatic migration by choice: moving session records between trees on upgrade is riskier than re-binding, and the one observed occurrence was remediated by hand at report time. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4791→4793.

### v3.125.0 (2026-08-04)
- **Seven friction fixes from the first two post-retreat WF2 runs (#880, epic #875).** Third M1 child; MINOR because Defect G changes WF2 behavior for every project. The Step 9 P15 coverage gate stops passing vacuously and stops reading the wrong file: `plan_lib.assert_review_coverage` raises the new fail-closed `ReviewLogError` on a missing log with high-risk tasks or on a wrong file (>0 non-blank lines, 0 schema-valid entries — the session-notes-as-log_path case measured on the #879 run: 8,878 warnings AND `ok=False`); a missing log with no high-risk tasks passes only WITH a stderr diagnostic naming the path; a "parsed entry" now requires the identity triple (task_id + sha + vocab verdict, one `_valid_review_entry` shared with the writer), so `{}` or a foreign object cannot dodge wrong-file detection and a valid-JSON non-dict line no longer crashes the gate; warnings are capped at 10 plausible lines plus an always-printed final count. The new `append-review-log` CLI is the sanctioned Step 8a writer (validates, delegates, fail-closed exit 2, path containment), Step 8a item 8 finally prescribes the append with the entry shape inline, and Step 9 names `claude_docs/.wf2-state/<issue>/review_log.jsonl` explicitly — before this, no step ever wrote the file the mandatory gate reads. `parse_tasks` accepts shell-safe token ids (`T1`, `1a`, `2.3`) and fails LOUD with a line number on every malformed `### Task ` heading and riskLevel attempt (wrapped reasons forbidden) — a typo can no longer silently parse to a zero-task plan that skips Step 8a. `classify_branch_protection` accepts the probe body as raw JSON text instead of silently returning `unknown`, the less-safe answer. The switch skill's staleness nudge invokes `post_update_reconcile.py` via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` so it can fire for the first time (it had exited 2 on every bind since it shipped). `launcher_lib` gains `_retires`: the satisfied-goal evaluation row (sentinel-less `met: true`, byte-equal to a trusted condition) retires cleanly at teardown — the ordinary end state of every successful run had permanently blocked the retire path — with the #758 forgery posture preserved, the refusal REMEDY made conditional (no more prescribing a provably-inert `/goal clear`), and the STILL-ARMED note asserting only checked armedness. `docs/config-reference.md` stops naming the #807-deleted `render_artifact.py` (negative guard). `BRANCH_PREFIX_FEATURE` becomes `feat` with the Step 5 prose pinned to it, and `step_state_post.py`'s issue-rebind regex learns `feat/` — the rename would otherwise have silently broken the statusline rebind. 69 red-first/new tests; 2 launcher tests rewritten with their reversal rationale in the docstrings; `docs/consolidation.md` deliberately untouched (quarantined historical, #270). WF2 diagram REV 3.125.0 (stations 5, 8a, 9 delta). Suite 4722→4791.

### v3.124.0 (2026-08-04)
- **`adversarialReview.diffReviewMode` — force the WF2 Step-11 cross-model diff review on every diff (#879, epic #875).** Second M1 child. `plan_lib.should_run_diff_review` elected the Step-11 1a diff review only on a security-relevant changed path or a `riskLevel: high` plan task, so a prose diff in a repo whose *product* is markdown scored `no security surface` and left the author model reviewing its own work as the only Step-11 pass (#856 run, friction 1; epic #875 D180 mandates cross-model review of every M1 diff). Owner ruling 2026-08-04: config wins. The `adversarialReview` block gains an optional `diffReviewMode` of `"auto"` (default, and what an absent field resolves to — byte-identical for every existing project) or `"always"` (elect on every **non-empty** diff; an empty diff still skips). The field mirrors the #403 `backend` field's shape exactly — sentinel-default read so an explicit `null` refuses, an `"invalid"` sentinel preserving the rejected value as a repr, a stderr warning naming it, and a new `diff-review-mode` CLI verb with the same 0/2 exit contract — so a present-but-invalid value makes the 1a sub-step abort with `failed (invalid diffReviewMode config)` and is **never** coerced to `auto`, which would silently restore the heuristic the operator opted out of. In the gate, `always` sits after the empty-diff guard and before the path/task heuristic so the returned reason names the mode; an unrecognized mode raises rather than falling back. The mode vocabulary is mirrored into `plan_lib` with a drift guard, and two new WF2 clarity pins assert the prose actually *passes* the resolved mode to the gate and that the invalid path is a loud marker. Activation for this repo is a separate hand edit of `.rawgentic_workspace.json` (AC5), deliberately not in this PR. The Step-11 review round added three fixes: the 1a prose now defines a marker for **every** non-zero exit of the mode resolver (not just 0 and 2), states that under `always` an unestablishable base ref means the mandated every-diff review did not run and must be said in the PR body, and both resolver verbs stop double-quoting the rejected value (the error field already holds a `repr`, so `!r` hid an int's type). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4666→4722.

### v3.120.0 (2026-08-03)
- **The M0a review runner lands, unused (#866, epic #756).** First of the four M0 retreat PRs (roadmap 2026-08-03, rulings D174/D179): `hooks/review_runner.py` is the ONE small cross-model review entry point that will serve WF2 Steps 4/8a/11, WF5 and WF13 after the M0b cutover — nothing invokes it yet. Three verbs (`review-code --base/--brief`, `review-artifact --type`, `consult`), gpt + glm backends, REUSING the proven pure pieces of `adversarial_review_lib` (schema, prompt builders, validators, GLM transport) rather than duplicating them — M0d deletes the old engine around those kept pieces. It closes the extraction's known holes by contract: reviewer identity is PINNED (an explicit `-m` always rides the codex argv; author==reviewer or an unresolvable reviewer refuses pre-egress; an egressed result never records an empty model string — the `adversarial_review_lib.py:1352` config-inherit hole); the #855 choke point ships as `plan_lib review-reopen` (mints a token file and debits the atomic loop-back budget AT MINT TIME — transport retries reuse the token and never re-debit; tokenless runner runs stamp `diagnostic: true`; a spent or malformed token refuses); oversize input REFUSES instead of truncate-and-continue (#834), with composition time recorded in the result's `timing`; error classes replace the old blanket nonzero-exit retry (#857): transport blip → one bounded retry, org-wide 429/spend limit → terminal never retried, per-account 429 → one permitted backend switch with identity re-checked, anything else → recorded-unclassified terminal; dead process / empty output is a terminal FAILURE (#766); truncated non-JSON output gets exactly one retry (#793); the noise-strip list is fixed and VISIBLE with deliberately no generic docs-only skip (markdown is executable behavior in this plugin); results bind freshness via `input_sha256`/`base_sha`/`head_sha` so the orchestrator can reject a result whose HEAD or artifact moved before disposition; invocation is `codex exec -m <reviewer> --sandbox read-only --output-schema … -o <file>` (flags verified against the installed codex-cli 0.146.0). A pre-PR cross-model review (direct `codex exec`, gpt-5.6-sol, per D180) returned 0 Critical / 4 High / 3 Medium / 1 Low; 7 were fixed in-PR — an actionable result now requires a successful EXCLUSIVE token stamp (a concurrently-spent or unstampable token downgrades to diagnostic, never authorizes); the default GLM transport became a true SINGLE provider attempt (arl.glm_complete blanket-retries internally, which would have multiplied quota attempts underneath the classification); a rename into a noise path no longer hides executable code (both old AND new paths must be noise); schema-invalid/vacuous outputs (`{"findings":[]}` with no summary, an all-empty consult proposal) are invalid_output, never a vacuous pass; the brief became bound input (combined byte cap with the diff, secret-scanned, `brief_sha256` in the result, and fenced as DATA with its own nonce); `--out` is validated and stale receipts invalidated BEFORE egress, and a receipt-write failure is a terminal failure, never exit 0; stdout joins stderr in error classification — and 1 was declined with reason (a mint-registry to make tokens unforgeable is the enforcement state machine roadmap §7 bans; within a single-actor trust boundary the token is a drift tripwire, and the result echoes the nonce for post-hoc audit). Classifier quota-marker shapes were live-verified (exa): Codex's weekly/session-cap sentences carry neither "usage limit" nor "rate limit" and now classify. 74 tests added (66 runner tests, black-box via a PATH-stubbed codex + injected GLM fakes, plus 8 review-reopen CLI tests). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 7031→7105.

### v3.119.1 (2026-08-03)
- **A cost-cap trip is no longer mistaken for a crash, and no longer burns the whole fallback chain (#852, epic #756).** When a Claude-lane dispatch hit `--max-budget-usd`, `resolve_parse_status` returned `nonzero_exit` at its exit-code branch (`adapters/base.py`) **before** ever consulting the envelope — so a terminal cost condition read as an ordinary crash. Two consequences, both measured on the ten preserved `analysis` receipts of the #762 run: the fallback chain advanced opus → fable → sonnet into the identical wall, because the cap is a per-attempt DOLLAR bound and model-independent; and the cause was unreadable from the receipt, since `terminal_reason`/`subtype`/`errors` lived only in `transport.stdout.txt` — which is why a retrospective attributed the failures to "the brief or the harness, not any one model" when it was a $2 cap against a ~$2.1 task. New `budget_exhausted` parse_status, deliberately **absent** from `AVAILABILITY_FAILURES` so `engine.run_with_fallback` (`engine.py:163`) stops instead of advancing, plus an optional-additive `terminal` object on the Observation carrying the provider's own verdict. **Corrects the issue's AC1 on evidence:** the budget-exhausted envelope has **no `result` key at all** (a success carries 11,303 chars), so there is no model output to preserve — the CLI discards the text itself and rawgentic never receives it; 17,434 output tokens were generated and billed and never handed over. What is preserved is the evidence. Observation schema bumped **v2 → v3** with `observation-2.schema.json` frozen, because widening a closed enum in place would break any vendored v2 copy — and the bump had to ship in the same commit, since `build_observation` validates fail-loud on the write path, so the new status without the schema would have turned a diagnosable failure into a crash. Fixtures are the real envelopes, per AC4's ban on stubs. A code review then caught that the distinct classification was **undone one layer down** — `observation_process_failure` tested the positive exit code first, so every downstream consumer read the trip back as `nonzero_exit`; and that `hooks/seat_outcomes_lib.PARSE_STATUS_ENUM` omitted the new status, silently redacting it to null, while its own comment claimed a drift test pinned it against the live schema and **no such test existed**. Both fixed, and the drift guard now exists. 22 tests added. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 7009→7031.
### v3.119.0 (2026-08-03)
- **The review-admission foundation lands: an issue-scoped journal whose lock spans the run-ledger append (#855, epic #756).** #855 found that the three review-loop controls — the loop-back budget, the severity-banded confidence filter and High-deferral discipline — have **zero production call sites**: `plan_lib.py` exposes only `assert-pr-body` and `close-design-gate`, `executor_routing_lib.py` never mentions loopback/deferral/disposition, and every `skills/**` reference is prose telling an LLM to call Python with no runner. So nothing debits under any run-id, and the hand-minted run-id in the issue title is a red herring. Two candidate mechanisms were refuted on evidence across three design-review passes before the third was adopted: gating the `build` seat (all three build call sites are initial plan-task builds — review fixes are applied inline by the orchestrator, so the barrier would never fire on the real path) and a serializing wave barrier on the `review` seat (serializing rounds never *caps* them; a wave finding nothing actionable debits nothing, so #840's thirteen rounds still run one at a time). The selected mechanism is **review admission**: a gate gets exactly one free initial review wave, and every later wave for that issue and gate is a reopening that debits — so a clean review stays free while a clean *re-read* costs budget, bounding wave count with the shipped budgets and no new counter. This release is the foundation only, inert for every existing caller: `ExpectedCallLedger.append_expected` gains an optional keyword-only `review_admission` object whose omission leaves the record **byte-identical** (the key is left out, not written as null, because `_locked_append` serializes with `sort_keys=True`); the new `phase_executor/src/phase_executor/admission_journal.py` provides the durable issue-keyed log whose `precheck` runs under the held `flock` and whose `transaction()` keeps that lock across the caller's run-ledger append; a wired POSIX platform gate refuses rather than degrading to a non-atomic path; and a downgrade pin executes the real `origin/main` reader to establish that an unknown *field* is tolerated while an unknown *kind* fails closed, which is the actual rollback constraint. 60 tests added across five files, including a Step-8a review round in which two independent reviewers converged on the same three defects: the platform gate REQUIRED `dir_fd` support while the journal still opened by pathname (so a symlinked parent escaped containment), durability stopped at the file and never reached its directory entry (so the first reservation was unreachable after a crash), and the size/record caps were checked only against the pre-append state (so one legal append could leave a journal every later read refuses). All fixed, plus short-read/short-write handling, a closed per-kind key set, and lock-errno discrimination so an EIO is not reported as a timeout. One High is deferred with rationale to PR 1b — the admission check is still caller-selectable, which PR 1a cannot close because the gate registry is hook-owned and `phase_executor` must never import `hooks/`. A second review round at Step 11 then caught a fail-OPEN the first fix round had INTRODUCED — `read()` decided "nothing written yet" with a pathname `.exists()` check, which follows symlinks, so a dangling issue-directory symlink read as absent and returned empty state, meaning every slot looked free. Fixed, along with a FIFO leaf that could park a blocking open before the refusal was reachable, an unsupported `schema_version` parsed as though supported, unbounded admission strings, and the ledger's own unchecked single `os.write` with no fsync. Two of the previous round's TESTS were also too weak to prove what they claimed and were replaced rather than kept as coverage that looks like proof. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 6938→7012.

### v3.118.2 (2026-08-02)
- **The notes trimmer no longer destroys decision logs (#847, epic #756).** `hooks/notes-size-handler.py` deleted everything outside the last 200 lines with no archive, and its only preservation path — a POST to `localhost:9077/ingest` (`:47-65`) — had never served, swallowed every exception, and had its result assigned at `:105` and never read. Six epic decision logs were destroyed, most recently `epic-46-autorun-log.md` (960 lines) and `epic-756-autorun-log.md` (848). Reproduced by the reporter's entry path before any fix: an 852-line fixture holding 140 decision entries came back with **0 of 140 surviving**, `"ingested": false` and `"trimmed": true` in the same result, and no archive anywhere on disk. Three defects, each separately sufficient. **(1) Destruction was the primary action** — the cut content now goes to `.notes-archive/<file>.<ts>.archive.md` first, created `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` so a same-second second trim cannot clobber the first archive (`atomic_write_text` ends in `os.replace`, which would), in a dot-directory outside the `session_notes/*.md` glob so archives are never re-trimmed. **(2) It failed OPEN** — the trim is now fail-CLOSED: if the archive does not land, the file is left byte-identical and the refusal is loud on stderr. **(3) Lines were an inverted proxy** for context cost — measured on the old handler, a 100-line 2.0 MB file was spared while an 801-line 2.4 KB file was cut, an 833x inversion; the threshold is now 64,000 characters with a 200-line / 16,000-character kept tail. Decision logs are additionally never trimmed at all, matched by name rather than by the accident that had been sparing them (a dot in the filename stem failed `PROJECT_NAME_RE`, which is the only reason `sysop.handoff.md` survived at 1,670 lines). New `hooks/decision_log.py` gives decisions a durable home at `claude_docs/decisions/<project>.jsonl`, a sibling of `session_notes/` and outside the glob by construction; `session-start` injects the newest 15. Appends use `flock` + `O_APPEND`, a deliberate deviation from AC8's `atomic_write_text`: sabotaging it to read-modify-write lost **31 of 40** concurrent records. Two sabotage runs shaped this change — the first showed the fail-closed test passing with the guard removed (a read-only parent blocked the notes write too, so the OS was doing the work), which is why the archive got its own directory. The integration test also caught the handler destroying a `decisions/*.jsonl` file when handed one directly; it now refuses anything that is not a `.md` notes file. `docs/wal-guide.md` also documented the 800/200 behaviour and was not in the issue's scope list — it would have gone stale silently. Round-1 cross-model review (two independent `gpt-5.6-sol` seats against `anthropic` authorship) returned 0 Critical / 3 High / 8 Medium / 4 Low and materially changed the fix: `os.write` short returns were being ignored so a truncated decision reported `"appended": true`; universal-newline reading silently ate every `\r`, so the archive did not byte-preserve CRLF content and `char_count` under-counted it; a single-line oversized file reported a successful trim while growing the file and writing an empty archive; a digits-only check accepted `RAWGENTIC_DECISION_INJECT_COUNT=999999999`, which is the whole store; and the store rejected dotted project names that `session-start` itself accepts. Four of the reviewers' findings were false-greens in this change's OWN tests — the "exactly at threshold" case used a 7 KB fixture, and the never-trimmed fixtures sat under the char threshold, so deleting the exclusion logic would have left them green. Those tests now fail when the guard is removed. Deferred with rationale: the pre-existing `flock` + `os.replace` inode race (a plain `>>` appender can still lose writes to the replaced inode — unchanged by this fix, and what was formerly lost outright is now archived), and surfacing refusals past session-start's `/dev/null` redirects. Round 2 (same two neutral briefs, fresh diff) found four more real defects, all now fixed: a truncated final JSONL record POISONED the next append, because `O_APPEND` concatenated onto the unterminated line and destroyed both while reporting success; a 40-digit `RAWGENTIC_DECISION_INJECT_COUNT` made bash's own `[ -gt ]` fail with "integer expression expected", so the numeric clamp silently did not clamp (it now bounds the string length first); newly created archive DIRECTORY entries were not fsynced, so a crash could keep the truncation and lose the archive; and the no-progress guard measured only the kept tail, so a tail that fit under the threshold but crossed back over once the header was prepended still "trimmed" successfully and churned a fresh archive every session start. Round 2 also caught four more tests that could not fail — including `test_invalid_project_name_rejected`, whose fixture stem `passwd` is a perfectly valid project name and which asserted only `rc == 0`. **Owner decision 2026-08-02:** the decision WRITERS were pointed at the new store in this PR rather than deferred — `skills/epic-run/SKILL.md` now records forks and substitutions via `decision_log.py append` (with the mandatory `--overturnable`) instead of hand-appending markdown, and `driver_lib`'s fresh-session resume prompt sends the successor to the store rather than the notes log; a drift guard pins both. ONE High remains deferred with owner acknowledgement: the pre-existing `flock` + `os.replace` inode race, where a writer holding the old inode loses its append when the pathname is replaced (`plan_lib.file_lock()` already models the stable-sidecar-lock remedy, and fixing it means changing the shared atomic-write discipline across nine #285 call sites). Also deferred: an archive retention bound, and surfacing refusals past session-start's `/dev/null` redirects. Per the run's two-round review cap, the round-2 fixes themselves were not re-reviewed by a third party. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 6886→6938.

### v3.118.1 (2026-08-02)
- **Severity now has a definition, and review briefs stop accumulating history (#853, epic #756).** Severity was the ONLY trigger for loop-backs (`steps.md:644`, `steps.md:1143`) and had **no definition anywhere in the repo** — a grep of `skills/` and `shared/` returned nothing, so every reviewer invented a scale. Two measured consequences on #840: Highs were treated as hard blockers for thirteen rounds although `steps.md:1144` makes a High deferrable via `plan_lib.append_deferral` (none ever was), and `SEVERITY_BANDED_CONFIDENCE` could not run at all because the briefs never asked for a confidence score.
