New single-sourced `shared/blocks/review-severity.md`, synced into WF2 and WF3, bands on **impact AND preconditions** rather than vibes, states plainly that a High is deferrable, requires `severity` + `confidence` + a precondition rationale so the existing filter can fire, and carries a worked calibration: #840's `observe_head` stale-ref defect is **High, not Critical** — catastrophic impact, but a non-default git configuration and no observed incidence (the peer consult reached the same calibration independently). **The brief-hygiene rules are measured, not stylistic.** Round 13 was run as a controlled experiment — three adversarial briefs (19.5-19.8 KB, carrying 13 rounds of accumulated failure history) against three neutral ones (3.6 KB), same commit, seat and lane. All six FAILed on the same real defects, so the adversarial framing inflated nothing; but the NEUTRAL arm found a Critical the adversarial arm missed, because the bloated brief had told reviewers that clause was "stable for eight rounds". Accumulated history SUPPRESSES findings — it had grown those briefs 6.5KB→19.8KB for a strictly worse review at 5.4x the tokens. So: never prescribe a verdict, never say which areas are settled, never carry round history, and keep a brief under ~8KB — if it will not fit, the change is too large to review in one pass. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 6886→6886.

Entries are one line per released version (most recent first), derived from the
merged PR. Dates are the merge dates; `#N` links the PR.

### v3.118.0 (2026-08-02)
- **The revalidation gate goes live: no next child while the queue is stale (#840, epic #756).** PR 1 (v3.117.0) shipped the machinery and enforced NOTHING by design. This is the enforcement half, atomically. **The gate:** `next_ready_issue(..., observed_head=)` raises `QueueRevalidationRequired` when the campaign receipt's `validated_head` differs from a freshly observed `origin/main`, when any eligible child's `validated_against` differs from it, and — until it was CUT, see below — when any durably-undisposed child carried a `pending_disposition`. It RAISES rather than returning `None` because `None` already means "nothing ready" and `resume_prompt_for_state` reported it as *the epic finished* — announcing completion over a stale queue is strictly worse than refusing, so `resume_prompt_for_state` now returns a result object and `launcher_lib handoff` exits **6** for a refusal and **5** when the head cannot be observed. **The head must be FRESHLY OBSERVED, not merely supplied** — both pass-3 reviewers found independently that requiring the argument without binding it to a live observation is no guard, since a caller could pass a cached SHA (or `validated_head` itself) and satisfy both clauses after `main` had moved, which would also defeat the abrupt-death recovery because a crashed predecessor's stale head compares equal to itself. So `launcher_lib.observe_head(repo_root)` fetches with an EXPLICIT refspec (`git -C <root> fetch <remote> +refs/heads/<branch>:refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch>`) then `rev-parse`s the tracking ref, checks BOTH return codes, and validates a full 40-char SHA — the refspec is load-bearing, see round 13 below. **It lives in `launcher_lib`, correcting the design**, which named a `driver_state.observe_head`: no such module exists, and `driver_lib` cannot host it because `tests/hooks/test_driver_state_write_back.py:295-301` greps that module's source for `subprocess`. **The `queue_revalidated` rung ships WITH its producer, and the existing suite proved why that is not optional** — adding the rung alone turned 4 tests red, one on the real `perform_handoff` path, because `perform_handoff` gates on `_predecessor_steps(ladder)`, `retire_predecessor` gates on the same prefix, and `evaluate_verifications` treats an unreported step as FAILED. It is the FIRST rung (the queue must be revalidated before a successor is spawned to inherit it), produced at BOTH gate sites by the launcher reading the durable receipt — never by a caller-supplied verification result, which was the peer consult's sharpest point — and in `perform_handoff` it runs after every caller-mismatch validation but before the first effect, so a bad argument still raises and a refusal never leaves an orphan pane. **`handoff_pending.queue`** carries the ordered child list plus `validated_head`, and `handoff_claim` validates the COMPLETE ORDERED payload against durable state; r2's "head and membership" was refuted because membership admits a reordered queue, and order decides which child runs next. Its discriminator is the state's own receipt, NOT the disposition's `kind` — `fresh_session_handoff`'s ready disposition carries no `kind` yet IS a campaign producer, so keying on `kind` would have broken the two tests pinning exactly three persisted keys on that path. **The mandatory correction consumer** closes the last hole: posting a correction comment does not repair a body, and `grep -c correction hooks/driver_lib.py` was **0**, so an agent could pass the gate and then build from the exact stale claim the revalidation had caught. Both prompt builders now render the evidence — the claim verbatim, what it was checked against, what the check found — not merely a URL an unattended successor may never open. New `/rawgentic:revalidate-children` skill mechanises the 2026-08-02 audit method (name the claim, check it, quote the proof; an empty `claims` list is refused; corrections are COMMENTS and an obsolete child is marked for an owner decision, never closed), registered across all surfaces via the checker (whitelist between `peer-consult`/`run-feedback`, codex mirror symlink, counts 20→21, workspace management 8→9, codex `longDescription`, and two stragglers the checker named that hand inspection missed: `.rawgentic.json`'s description and a hand-pinned assert). **An absent `campaign_context` REFUSES** rather than skipping the check — verified at source that no path uses the mid-child ladder without campaign state (the ad-hoc handoff passes no `steps=` and gets the three-rung launch ladder), so "absent" there can only be a caller that forgot the argument, and passing the rung for it would silently skip the whole gate. **The Step-11 cross-model review (`gpt-5.6-sol`) returned FAIL with 3 Critical, 1 High and 2 Medium, every one of the first four reproduced by execution before acceptance, and the owner closed all three Criticals rather than deferring them.** (1) Enforcement was **opt-in theatre**: a campaign with no receipt was not gated at all — the observation was skipped, selection saw neither an observation nor a receipt and proceeded, and the producer returned True without touching git, while nothing in the code would ever have created that first receipt. This was the PR's own most-doubted claim and the reviewer's refutation was decisive: a refusal is recoverable by one command, silent selection is the one direction forbidden. The head is now observed unconditionally and an un-armed campaign is refused, naming its remedy; the migration consequence (an existing campaign refuses until the skill runs once) is documented rather than discovered. (2) **`single-session` was a third bypass** — the mode check was the FIRST thing in `fresh_session_handoff`, so an armed campaign with a stale receipt advanced even with a fresh head supplied, and that mode is the driver's DEFAULT. The gate moved above the mode branch, and because a pure function cannot fetch, a new `launcher_lib next-child` command is the gated selection path the in-session loop must use (rc 0/3/5/6 matching `handoff`). (3) **The no-eligible-children shortcut returned before receipt validation AND before the head comparison**, so with one in_progress child the ladder rung reported PASSED on a malformed or stale receipt — on the path that gates an irreversible teardown; both are now unconditional. (4) An invalidated payload was **illegible**, not permanent: exact equality is kept, and `handoff_queue_is_current` lets `retire_predecessor` report `queue_changed` instead of "a foreign or live claim", which sent the operator after a session that does not exist. A first attempt cancelled the record from `record_child_outcome` and was **reverted** — an AST guard asserts `open_handoff` is the only writer of `handoff_pending` in `driver_lib`, and that single-writer rule is worth more than the convenience. (5) The mid-child command bumped the generation before the first-rung check, so an abrupt death left an uncancelled generation for an unchecked queue; the check moved ahead of the durable write, ordered after the plausibility check so a bad position keeps its own exit code and outside the lock so a `git fetch` never blocks readers. (6) **My own call-site source tests were mutation-insensitive** — `campaign_context=None` still satisfied `assert "campaign_context=" in src` — and are replaced by runtime tests that drive a genuinely stale campaign through each entry point, including one asserting no `herdr pane close` is ever issued on a refused teardown. Making the gate universal forced a real test-infrastructure change worth naming: `test_launcher_lib.py`'s handoff CLI tests moved off `--project-root <repo root>` onto a throwaway repo with a LOCAL origin, because "always observe" would otherwise have made the suite perform a live `git fetch` per test and depend on the network. **A SECOND review round then found four more High defects in the fix round itself, and this is recorded rather than smoothed over: fixing three Criticals introduced a new jam.** The universal gate was **unrecoverable** for a schema-valid legacy campaign — `base_default_branch_sha` is optional and nullable, and `revalidation_worklist` RAISED for an unstamped child without it, so the gate refused the campaign and the clearing skill could not build its first worklist; re-running it changed nothing. The first arm now falls back to `from_sha == to_sha == observed_head` and forces `deep`, failing toward MORE scrutiny, with a round-trip test proving refused→arm→selectable. `next-child` folded `no_project` into rc 3 ("nothing ready"), so a fresh, ready campaign lacking an optional `project` field reported as complete-or-blocked and the loop could stop for good — it now has its own rc. `handoff_queue_is_current` returned True for the migration shape (a pre-#840 handoff in flight has no queue payload; arming the campaign then requires one), so the refusal was reported as a foreign claim. And the documented fresh-session caller in `epic-run` and `docs/multi-issue-driver.md` was never migrated to observe the head at all, while `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` still documented the deleted receipt-less pass — my own prose from the previous round contradicting production, which is the exact defect class this epic exists to remove. **Twenty guards were sabotage-tested individually — nine in the build, six in the first review round, five in the second — and each confirmed red: the per-child clause, the head comparison (tested in ISOLATION, with children stamped at the observed head and only the receipt behind, because the two clauses both refuse the naive stale state and a test built the obvious way proves only that one of them fired), the obsolete check (built otherwise fully stamped and current, differing ONLY by `pending_disposition`), the producer, the rung, both `observe_head` checks, the claim-time comparison, and the mandatory-`queue` branch. **One sabotage initially read as a vacuous guard and that was the harness's error, not the code's** — the patch had landed at the first textual match of a line that occurs twice, in a different function — recorded because "the sabotage missed" and "the guard is vacuous" produce an identical green. PR 1's inertness guard and the driver doc's "the machinery above is INERT" pin are INVERTED rather than left passing; a guard that no longer describes the code is worse than none. `docs/multi-issue-driver.md` and `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` document the live three-layer gate and the seven-rung ladder; `skills/epic-run/SKILL.md` handles the new disposition. **Three cross-model review rounds, each finding defects introduced by the previous round's fixes** — round 1 3 Critical, round 2 4 High, round 3 3 High + 4 Medium, all closed: claim precedence now outranks queue-payload diagnostics (a live foreign claimant could be reported as "no claim was ever created", inviting a competing successor), malformed OR unresolvable baselines fall back to an equal-endpoint forced-`deep` range instead of jamming the migration, the driver doc's own primary loop and advance rule no longer prescribe the gate-bypassing direct selector, and a stamped child whose receipt carries a `pending_disposition` is refused. 180 tests added, measured by AST across the changed test files rather than counted by hand (round-9 Low 2 caught the hand-maintained figure stale). **Diagram decision: no workflow-spine change → no diagram REV** — the gate is on the epic driver's selection and the pane-handoff verification ladder, neither of which is a WF1/WF2/WF3/WF5 station, gate or loop-back (`grep` for the ladder and driver symbols in `docs/workflow-diagram.html` returns 0). **Round 8 changed the METHOD, by owner decision, because seven rounds had each fixed the shape a reviewer named and shipped its twin** — rounds 6, 7 and 8 every one found a fresh variant of a jam an earlier round had already ‘closed’ (round 6 fixed absent receipts, round 8 found corrupt ones; round 7 fixed the merged-child remedy for UNSTAMPED children, round 8 found the stamped twin its fixture omitted). Round 8's own five findings were all reproduced before acceptance: the rung's producer called `next_ready_issue` **without the issue-state probe**, so the rung and the selection it gates disagreed about which children were open and a probe-merged sibling jammed a handoff on a child nobody could revalidate (the probe is now DERIVED inside the producer, since #695's own lesson is that an optional corroboration no caller threads in ships dead); `validate_queue_revalidation` **preempted** the probe-aware owner-gate remedy with the unsafe `deferred|abandoned` wording for the stamped shape, now swallowed at the gate with a `preempted` re-raise that makes the superset argument falsifiable rather than trusted; `_receipt_covers_child` equated **key presence with attestation**, so a corrupt record made selection die while the worklist had nothing to rebuild; and a stale receipt head with a pending-only outstanding set printed ‘re-running it will change nothing’ when the stale head was exactly what re-running fixes (the combined remedy now names both, owner write-back FIRST because disposing a child changes what is left to revalidate). **The durable fix is a MECHANICAL SWEEP, not another round:** `tests/hooks/test_revalidation_jam_matrix.py` drives all **3840** reachable gate states — status × stamp (including a malformed one) × receipt record × receipt head × probe verdict — through their OWN printed instructions, executing each remedy via the production entry points and asserting the gate opens within a bounded chain. It immediately found four defect classes **no review round had reported**: 280 states whose refusal named nothing an operator could run (every one a bare `DriverStateError` from the receipt validator, of which an earlier round-8 fix had patched precisely one — the very mistake this method exists to stop), 39 where a corrupt record belonging to a NON-eligible child could never be rebuilt because the worklist is eligible-only, 810 where the prescribed remedy **crashed** on a malformed `validated_against`, and 24 where the gate hard-errored on a value `revalidation_worklist` had been taught to tolerate at round-3 High 1 — two halves of one feature disagreeing about what is recoverable. All four are fixed **at the boundary rather than per-site**: a wrapper on `validate_queue_revalidation` attaches the remedy to every refusal it raises today and every one added later, an unusable stamp is reported as stale provenance instead of raising, and the new pure `driver_lib.rebuild_receipt` makes SKILL.md step 7 executable — it REBUILDS the receipt from evidence (a record that no longer validates or attests another head is dropped; a stamp whose evidence went with it is cleared; a `pending_disposition` child is recorded but never stamped; an empty audit still advances the head, because a campaign whose children are all merged or in flight must still be armable or the gate is shut for good on the mid-child handoff it serves) and validates its own output before returning. Step 7 was prose until now, and prose is where three of the eight rounds found defects. Four contract surfaces still carrying the old eligible-only pending scope were corrected together (`driver_lib` and `launcher_lib` docstrings, `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` and its rendered `.html`), along with the runbook's false claim that every rung refusal is cleared by revalidation. **Round 9 replaced the single sequential reviewer with THREE parallel ones on distinct lenses** (recoverability, blast radius, prose-vs-code) and returned 5 High + 5 Medium + 2 Low against round 8's 3 High + 2 Medium — with the most serious defect found INDEPENDENTLY by two lenses via different routes. Three of its findings were in code written during the round-8 fix pass, and two guards reported as sound were vacuous; that is recorded rather than smoothed over, because it is the measurement that justified the method change. **The worst of them: `rebuild_receipt` could retire a `pending_disposition` on a durably-undisposed child** — via a dropped malformed record, a dropped stale-head record, or an audited record simply overwriting the marker — so the campaign armed, the gate opened, and a dependent was handed out with no owner having decided anything. **The 3840-state sweep scored all three as PASSES, and the reason is the generalisable lesson:** its only question was *does the gate OPEN*, which is exactly what laundering an obligation achieves. A recoverability property with no safety counterpart rewards laundering. `TestTheGateNeverOpensOverALiveOwnerDecision` is that counterpart — while a durably-undisposed child carries a marker, nothing an operator can run may make the queue selectable, and only a durable owner outcome retires it. The root cause was structural: an owner decision was stored inside a HEAD-SCOPED audit record, so every rule that rebuilds records against a head could destroy it; `rebuild_receipt` now REFUSES while an obligation is live, naming the owner command. Also closed: receipt keys must be canonical decimal ASCII (`"01"`, `"001"` and the Unicode digit `"١"` all validated, all mapped to `int` 1, and all were invisible to `children.get(str(n))` — so a marker filed under one hid the owner gate completely); the remedy is now carried as a STRUCTURAL `remedy` attribute rather than appended-unless-the-text-already-mentions-it, after a receipt whose `version` field held the literal string `revalidate-children` suppressed its own remedy AND fooled the harness, which shared the identical substring blind spot; the sweep now checks that the FIRST refusal DISCLOSED every action the chain takes, which immediately exposed 225 further states where a structural receipt error prescribed only revalidation while an owner decision was pending; a new `launcher_lib rebuild-receipt` command performs the rebuild INSIDE `_locked_state_update` against a freshly observed head and re-reads from disk to validate what landed, closing both the gap where the documented remedy never persisted anything and the lost update where rebuilding from a pre-audit snapshot erased a concurrent `record-child-outcome`; `build_revalidation_record` and `normalize_issue_body` make the eight-field record shape and its previously-undefined `body_hash` derivation executable instead of prose; `retire-predecessor` now surfaces the actionable revalidation reason instead of filing it where the CLI never projects it; and the #840 design doc and its rendered HTML are marked SUPERSEDED rather than left prescribing `driver_state.observe_head`, a module that does not exist. **Round 10 then returned FAIL on all three lenses, and the owner CUT SCOPE rather than running an eleventh round.** The measurement that decided it: four of round 10's six findings lived entirely inside the `pending_disposition` OWNER GATE — a clause added in round 6 that then broke in rounds 7, 8, 9 and 10 — while the head-freshness and per-child-stamp clauses had been stable since round 5. Round 10's sharpest finding is worth recording verbatim because it shipped in round 7 and nine review rounds walked past it: the printed remedy `record-child-outcome --issue N --status deferred|abandoned` is a SHELL PIPELINE. Pasted into bash it WRITES `deferred`, prints `abandoned: command not found` and a `BrokenPipeError`, and exits 127 — so the operator sees a failure, concludes nothing happened, and the child is silently in a terminal status that `record_child_outcome`'s own guard then refuses to correct to `merged`. Verified by execution. Round 10 also found that round 9's own fix had reintroduced round 7's defect at a second, PROBE-BLIND site, which is the pattern in one sentence: several places each computing their own remedy from information some of them did not have. **So this PR now ships the two stable clauses only.** The owner gate, its remedies, the stamped-plus-pending receipt invariant and the rebuild's live-obligation refusal are all removed; `pending_disposition` remains a validated FIELD that gates nothing, exactly as v3.117.0 shipped the rest of this machinery inert, and `TestTheOwnerGateIsInert` pins that — INVERTED by #848 rather than deleted. Cutting it exposed one more jam the sweep caught immediately: the "an obsolete child stays UNSTAMPED" rule existed only to keep such a child un-selectable, so with nothing gating on the marker it stopped protecting anything and instead left the child permanently unstamped, refusing the provenance clause for ever — removed with the gate it served. **Knowingly left open and pinned by a test rather than discovered later:** an obsolete child can still satisfy a dependent's dependency under `deps_satisfied_by: "pr_open"`. That is PRE-EXISTING — nothing enforced it before #840 — so the cut returns to the status quo rather than regressing past it. #848 carries the rebuild, with the acceptance criterion that ONE function owns "what is wrong and what clears it", which is the structural answer to ten rounds of per-site remedy fixes. **Round 11 (three lenses again) returned FAIL on all three, 0 Critical — and the attribution diagnostic, not another fix round, decided what happened next.** Attributing each finding to a sub-feature showed the largest cluster was not a new defect class but MY OWN scope cut left half-applied: the removed owner gate still spoke through the generated successor prompt and four doc/skill surfaces. The worst instance is a High rather than cosmetic — a fully stamped, attested child carrying a marker is now correctly SELECTED, while the prompt still told the successor an owner decision was required “before any work starts”, so an unattended successor stalls on a gate that no longer exists (found independently by two lenses). Finished the cut across the prompt, the skill's frontmatter no-op claim, its candidate rule, `docs/multi-issue-driver.md` and the runbook. Two small bugs fixed rather than filed because both are ≤5 lines and fail in forbidden directions: an UNATTESTED stamp was trusted as a diff baseline and bought a `quick` audit — suppressing exactly the citation checks `deep` runs, which is failing toward LESS scrutiny — now scoped so the legacy no-receipt first-arm path keeps its narrow range (the first attempt at this fix was too broad and its own negative twin caught it); and the `rebuild-receipt` CLI mapped `"1"` and `"01"` to one integer, silently discarding one record and returning rc 0, which is audit evidence lost while reporting success. Two genuinely separate concerns were FILED rather than fixed, per the owner's rule that non-convergence means split: the handoff queue payload omits `claims`, `depends_on` and dependency policy so durable state can move under a live handoff (#849), and the jam matrix drives the pure helper rather than the CLI — a reviewer no-op'd `_cmd_rebuild_receipt` and all 3840 states still passed (#850). That overclaim is corrected in the matrix's own docstring here; the coverage work is #850. **Round 12 (three lenses) then found the same class one level deeper, in the half this PR still ships, and two lenses found it independently:** `rebuild_receipt` DELETED the active `in_progress` child's `body_corrected` record — the worklist is `queued`-only, so nothing re-supplied it — and `produce_queue_revalidated` then reported the rung PASSED. Verified by execution: the mid-child prompt carried the correction before the rebuild and not after. The rung therefore authorised the handoff and eventually the predecessor teardown while the successor lost the mandatory correction and resumed from the stale body — this issue's own thesis defeated on the exact one-`in_progress` path the feature exists to serve. Fixed with BOTH halves, because refusing alone would have been another unrecoverable jam: `carries_successor_evidence` names what a successor still needs (a broken claim, a correction comment, a pending disposition), `rebuild_receipt` REFUSES to drop it for an undisposed child without a replacement, and the worklist now offers that child as a candidate even when it is not `queued` so the refusal is clearable. Ordinary and corrupt records are still dropped freely — that dropping is what makes a corrupt entry recoverable, and a negative twin pins it. Two more from the same round: an OLDER audit could replace a NEWER same-head correction (the state lock serialises WRITES, it does not order EVIDENCE), now refused; and the round-11 canonical-key fix did not catch LITERAL duplicate JSON properties — `json.load` keeps the last silently, before any check sees either — now rejected at decode time via `object_pairs_hook`, tested with duplicate JSON TEXT because a Python dict cannot express the shape that defeated the round-11 test. **Round 13 ran the three adversarial lenses AND, as a control, three NEUTRAL WF2-style briefs against the same commit, same seat, same lane — and the control is the finding.** All six FAILed and converged on the same defects by executed reproduction, so prompt framing was NOT inflating severity. But the neutral arm, at one fifth the prompt size, found a defect the adversarial arm missed: `observe_head` ran `git fetch origin` with NO refspec, so a narrow or filtered `remote.origin.fetch` returns rc 0 WITHOUT advancing the tracking ref and the rev-parse hands back a STALE sha that passes as freshly observed — defeating the freshness clause this feature exists to enforce. The adversarial prompt had told reviewers that clause was "stable for eight rounds"; all three duly cleared it. Accumulated round-history in a review brief SUPPRESSES findings rather than sharpening them, and it had grown 6.5KB→19.8KB across rounds. Three more, each found by all six reviewers: the round-12 evidence-ordering guard rejected only `now < was`, but `validated_at` is an integer epoch SECOND so two audits inside one second TIE and the incoming record won unconditionally — reinstating exactly the loss round 12 closed, now refused as unordered unless the record is IDENTICAL (which keeps an interrupted rebuild re-runnable); the duplicate-property hook guarded ONE decode path of five, leaving `_locked_state_read`, `_locked_state_update`, `next-child` and `handoff` on plain `json.load` — so the durable receipt that actually gates selection and teardown still collapsed to the LAST of two identical properties and opened at rc 0, now one `_load_state_strict` used everywhere, which also refuses a non-object document (a valid `[]` previously reached `fresh_session_handoff` and raised an uncaught AttributeError where the contract is rc 2); and the revalidate-children SKILL still derived candidates as eligible/`queued` only while the round-12 worklist also requires undisposed non-queued children carrying successor evidence, so following the printed remedy literally raised `no extraction supplied for child #N` — the refusal could not clear itself, the seventh instance of this issue's dominant defect class. The new tests EXECUTE the documented procedure rather than hand-feeding the maps, which is how the round-12 test missed it; five guards sabotage-tested individually, each confirmed red with the sabotage verified as valid Python first. Suite 6644→6886.

### v3.117.0 (2026-08-02)
- **Revalidation machinery for epic children — pure, tested, and deliberately INERT (#840, epic #756).** Every merge moves `main` underneath every child that has not started, so their file:line citations, root-cause claims and acceptance criteria rot silently and nothing notices. The 2026-08-02 audit of epic #756 measured the tax: **14 of 23** open children carrying claims that no longer held, three of which had already caused real wasted work (#838's design written against an unmerged branch, #839's recorded fix shape invalidated by a `credential_ref` fact nobody re-checked, #835's body naming a root cause that turned out to be wrong). This PR ships the mechanism only — `cited_paths`, `parse_changed_paths`, `revalidation_worklist`, the `queue_revalidation` state shape and its fail-closed validators — and **refuses nothing**: `QueueRevalidationRequired` is defined but never raised and `next_ready_issue` is byte-identically unchanged, asserted by a test. That split is a design-gate finding, not caution: an earlier plan put the refusal in this PR and its only clearing mechanism in the next, which would have jammed a live campaign between the two. **The central decision came from the owner after both independent cross-model reviewers refuted the original premise**: the cited-paths ∩ changed-files intersection now decides **how hard** to look, never **whether** — a child whose cited files a merge did not touch is still looked at, merely `quick` rather than `deep`. Intersection is an incomplete dependency model, and #835 is the standing proof: its body was wrong about the *cause*, not a filename, so a path filter would have cleared it. A stamp additionally requires evidence — `claims` must be non-empty and each record names the claim, what it was checked against, and the quote from `main` that settles it — because without that an agent could mark every remaining child valid having checked nothing, and the gate would report a fully validated queue. `issue_obsolete` is deliberately not an `outcome`: a stamped child is selectable, so an obsolete child stays unstamped under `pending_disposition` until an owner moves it. Validators RAISE rather than accumulate errors, because `validate_driver_state` is permissive and `queue.schema.json` sets `additionalProperties: true`, so a malformed stamp would otherwise pass every existing check in silence; both new keys are additive, so no `schema_version` bump and every pre-#840 campaign file validates unchanged. Fail direction is uniform: a missing injected extraction or changed-set RAISES rather than defaulting, since an absent value would silently produce `quick` and fail toward *less* scrutiny. Regex over issue bodies is treated as untrusted input — every quantifier bounded (≤8 segments of ≤64 chars), with a ReDoS timing test and a structural guard asserting no `+`/`*` outside a character class. **Four platform behaviours were probed live rather than assumed**, after a reviewer correctly refused evidence that cited skill documentation: `git -C <root> fetch origin` + `rev-parse` (rc 0), `cat-file -e <ref>:<path>` (rc 0 present / rc 128 absent, cleanly separable), `show <ref>:<path>`, and `diff --name-status -M`. That last probe corrected the design twice: the `R` row appears with *and without* `-M` (git detects renames by default), and the load-bearing fact is that a rename row carries **three** tab-separated fields where `M`/`A`/`D` carry two — a two-field parser takes `old_name.py` as the status and loses the old path, sabotage-confirmed. Test fixtures are five REAL epic-#756 bodies with recorded hashes, and every expected classification was MEASURED against `origin/main`, not assumed: that measurement disproved the design's own claim that #797 cites no repository paths (it cites `hooks/context_meter.py:84-85`, which exists), found that #835 now cites two resolvable paths because a prior session corrected it, and identified #775 as the genuinely citation-free body. **Three vacuous passes were caught and fixed during this work** — a trigger keyed on a marker a code path bypasses, a parallel-group declaration where every task had its own group so the validator proved nothing, and a ReDoS guard that bounded quantifiers passed trivially — each rewritten and then deliberately sabotaged to confirm it fails. The generalisable rule is now in the design doc: a test asserting that another test exists, or asserting a value some other rule already forces, is not mutation-sensitive. The Step-8a and Step-11 review waves (four cross-model reviewers plus an adversarial diff pass) then found **13 further High/Medium defects in this very PR**, every one confirmed by execution before it was accepted: the extraction regex matched extension PREFIXES (`web/app.tsx` extracted as `web/app.ts`, `hooks/a.pyc` as `hooks/a.py`) yielding a WRONG path set whose empty intersection produces `quick`; URL scrubbing both left a >2048-char tail to be rescanned as a citation AND swallowed a real citation that followed a URL; returning `paths` as soon as ONE candidate resolved let a resolving decoy manufacture the low-scrutiny verdict; root-level files and dot-directories were invisible; the 'fail-closed' diff parser accepted `MALFORMED` as `M` and silently dropped a third field; injected facts were presence-checked but not value-checked, so `None` became an empty set and produced `quick`; the receipt validator accepted a contradictory record (`still_valid` alongside `broken` claims, an obsolete disposition on a stamped child, a null `body_hash`); enum checks leaked raw `TypeError`; and an unstamped child with no campaign base emitted `from_sha: None`. All fixed, plus a new `validate_queue_revalidation` tying the receipt to the per-child stamps. `cited_paths` was rewritten TOKEN-BASED rather than patched, and self-testing that rewrite caught two more of my own bugs before commit (`strip('.')` ate the leading dot of `.github`; `_` as a token delimiter shattered every `test_*.py`). **Seven vacuous tests were identified and rewritten** — including two the reviewers proved green under their own sabotage — and the ReDoS structural guard was corrected twice, ending as a nested-quantifier check that is itself sabotage-tested. 133 tests added across four new files. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV (no WFn station, gate or loop-back added; the enforcement rung on the pane-handoff ladder lands in the follow-up PR and will carry its own REV decision). Suite 6511→6644.
### v3.116.2 (2026-08-01)
- **A review seat can no longer be dispatched with nothing attached — via a typed flag, not by reading the brief's prose (#826, epic #756).** A review dispatch that needs an external artifact and carries none is a quality gate that passes while reviewing nothing: the reviewer returns whatever it can infer from the brief, and the gate reads that verdict as a pass. Measured over this repo's own run tree at `3a017dd9`: **167 recorded review-seat observations, 117 with empty `context_hashes`**; a first pass flagged 10 as attachment-referencing and the Step-11 review showed **five were FALSE POSITIVES**, so the real defect class is **~5**. Root cause was a missing required-input contract: `--context-file` appeared exactly ONCE in the whole skill corpus as an optional bracketed flag, and Steps 4, 8a and 11 never mentioned it, while the `build` seat has enforced its own required inputs since #464. **The requirement is now carried OUT OF BAND as a typed `--requires-context` dispatch flag** (owner decision 2026-08-01): declaring it without a usable `--context-file` is refused `review_context_required` (exit 2) at the `_do_dispatch` choke point — pre-receipt, pre-ledger, covering the ordinary, resume AND supervised routes — with the `dispatch_seat` check retained as defense in depth for direct library callers. **Three prior rounds of PROSE INFERENCE were refuted and are deleted outright**, each for a different reason: a phrase list was over-inclusive (5 of 10 flagged dispatches INLINED their artifact, and a false positive refuses live work), a narrowed list was under-inclusive ("the provided spec", "uploaded document"), and an HTML-comment marker was FORGEABLE — an unrestricted substring, so artifact data quoting it sets a control signal, the same control/data confusion #829 existed to close. `CONTEXT_REQUIRED_MARKER`, `prompt_references_attachment` and `prompt_requires_context` are removed rather than left importable, with a test asserting they stay gone so a fourth heuristic cannot quietly reappear. The counter-argument is answered rather than waved away — a caller can forget `--requires-context` just as easily as `--context-file`, but that is a STATIC property of the call sites, so `tests/hooks/test_wf_review_sites.py` reads all four WF review sites and fails if any stops mandating both flags; a wrong prose guess was only ever discoverable in production. That guard was itself verified non-vacuous by sabotaging the WF3 site and confirming it fails with the precise reason. `has_usable_context` survives the redesign unchanged: an empty or whitespace-only context file is cardinality, not content, and still refuses. Ten tests replace twenty-two: the behavioural INVERSION (a brief saying "the attached design" with no flag now PROCEEDS — wording is no longer a control signal), the flag refusing with no receipt minted, the with-context pass, whitespace-only context, a non-review seat proving scope, the CLI surface black-box via `--help`, plus three static site tests. Fail-CLOSED per the §3 decision guide: this is a correctness boundary, not convenience routing. Correction to the originating report, recorded because the citation was wrong while the finding was right: the attempt it named as the dead contentless one carries a context hash and returned real findings; the genuinely dead attempt in that run is `0-a8d3122b`, `.incomplete` with no observation — the receipt-with-no-observation half is #766/#733 work and is deliberately out of scope. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV (no station, gate or loop-back added; an existing dispatch gained a required flag). Step-11 review then found the fix ASSERTING something it did not deliver: `--requires-context` is a positive claim that the artifact reached the provider, but the resume and supervised routes drop `--context-file` entirely (#832, open), so the flag would have been true-looking and false. That combination now fails closed with its own `review_context_unsupported_route` code until #832 lands; a resume/supervised dispatch making no such claim is untouched. Two more review findings fixed: the new tests exercised only the defense-in-depth `dispatch_seat` copy — the SAME defect round 3 was rejected for — so a choke-point test now asserts refusal happens BEFORE the expected-call ledger append, which is the only signal that distinguishes the two guards; and the static site test now DISCOVERS sites rather than pinning hand-written sentences. A re-review of that fix round then caught the discovery being **incomplete in the same way it claimed to fix**: keying on the `model-routing: role=review` marker still returned four while a real fifth review dispatch — Step 4's spec-tightening incremental verifier — carried no marker, so the changelog's own claim of protection was false. Discovery now keys on the DISPATCH INSTRUCTION itself, and every review dispatch must be either marker-annotated (flags checked) or named with a reason as needing no external artifact; the verifier is the latter, because `role=review` and "needs an external artifact" are different properties. The same re-review gated the new unsupported-route guard on `role == "review"` for contract consistency. **Every guard added here was verified non-vacuous by sabotage** — and two of them failed that check first: a choke-point test that passed with its guard disabled, and this discovery test. Suite 6497->6511.
### v3.116.1 (2026-08-01)
- **`--context-file` reached the audit trail but never the provider (#829, epic #756).** `engine.py:147` stored the attached context on the `AdapterRequest`, but all three adapters sent `req.prompt` ALONE — `codex_cli.py:175`, `claude_cli.py:168`, `zhipuai_sdk.py:94` — and `capture.hash_context` (via `base.py:150`) was its only consumer. So a dispatch that attached a design or a diff read the file, hashed it into `context_hashes`, and **discarded it**, while the model received only the brief. That is worse than dropping the flag silently: the trail asserts a delivery that never happened, so any reviewer, gate or measurement reading the Observation concludes the artifact was supplied. It also defeated the natural detection path — a review seat carrying Read/Grep often recovered by going to the repo itself, which is why substantive findings came back from dispatches that had in fact been handed nothing. New `base.compose_provider_input(prompt, context)` is the ONE place the provider input is assembled, used by all three adapters for BOTH the transport payload and `cap.write_input`, so `input.md` — the forensic surface a human reads months later — now equals the bytes actually sent. Items are delimited and numbered (`BEGIN/END ATTACHED CONTEXT i of n`) rather than bare-concatenated, because an artifact's own text would otherwise read as further instruction to the model. Empty context returns the prompt BYTE-IDENTICALLY, so the many legitimately self-contained briefs are unaffected — pinned by a regression test. Eleven tests: six on the pure composition (order, verbatim inclusion, delimiting, determinism, `None` tolerance, byte-identity when empty) and five at the adapter boundary asserting the payload handed to the transport and the on-disk capture both CONTAIN the context — a stub returning success is not evidence, which is precisely how this went unnoticed. Red-before-green proven by reverting the three adapters while keeping the new function: the four boundary tests fail with 'sent the prompt without the attached context'. Found by the mandatory Step-11 cross-model review on PR #827 and verified independently against source; #826's guard is blocked on this, since forcing a caller to attach a file that goes nowhere would have manufactured false evidence. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Step-11 review then found a **fourth** registered adapter the first pass missed — `hermes_http`, in the `ADAPTERS` registry, still sending `req.prompt` at `:255` and `:280` — now wired, with a test that ENUMERATES the registry so the next adapter added cannot be silently left behind. Two more review findings fixed in the same change: the boundary marker now carries a digest derived from the attached bytes (a fixed delimiter is forgeable by artifact content — this repo's own #829 diff contains the literal marker text, so it was demonstrated, not theoretical), and the composed input states explicitly that attachments are DATA, never instructions. Zhipu gained JSON-payload coverage its stdin-shaped sibling test could not give it. Deferred with reasons on the issue: context is still dropped on the supervised/resume routes (#832), the Observation carries no hash of the exact composed input (#833), and attached bytes are unbounded (#834). Suite 6481->6497.
### v3.116.0 (2026-08-01)
- **The render engine leaves this repo; skills invoke the `design-doc-publish` add-on by absolute path (#807, epic claude-skills#11).** `hooks/render_artifact.py` and its 82-test suite are deleted — the engine landed in claude-skills as the add-on's `scripts/render-doc` (#25, `9bcd0a5`). The five invocation sites (the issue says nine; **measured five** at HEAD — four relative, one a `<plugin-hooks>` placeholder nobody ever implemented) now carry one generated `<render-addon>` block, single-sourced in `shared/blocks/render-addon.md` and synced by `scripts/sync_shared_blocks.py`. **The bug being fixed:** `python3 hooks/render_artifact.py` is a RELATIVE path that only resolved when the shell sat in `projects/rawgentic`, so those skills had never rendered anything from a session bound to any other project. The block resolves `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/skills/design-doc-publish/scripts/render-doc` — absolute, so cwd stops mattering. Rejected alternatives, both on measurement rather than assumption: a resolver/detector hook (`python3 hooks/<x>.py` carries the very bug it would fix), and `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` (substituted into a SKILL.md body at skill-load time but NOT into `references/*.md`, which are only ever Read — it would have worked at three sites and silently failed at two). Four states with distinct exits: `0` rendered, `20` absent, `70` broken, `71` config-error. **`absent` writes its own plain fallback page** rather than delegating to the model, which is what makes AC3/AC5 testable at all; a half-installed add-on (dangling symlink, missing or non-executable launcher, non-zero exit, non-HTML output) is `broken` and never falls back; an unreadable config root is `config-error`, because "cannot tell" must never be reported as "cleanly absent". Rendering goes to a temp file and is renamed only after validating, so a failed render cannot clobber a good artifact, and the five placeholders reach an inline Python program as `argv` inside a quoted heredoc — no shell evaluation, proven with a title carrying `$(...)`, backticks, both quote kinds and a newline. `adversarial_review_lib`'s try/except import of the departed registry is removed outright: the literal vocabulary is now the plain source of truth, and the two silent-success tests that asserted `err == ""` keep passing UNCHANGED, which is the evidence the removal was correct — left alone, the fallback's stderr warning would have fired on every call and failed them. The `_TEMPLATES` equality guard is NOT replaced by a guard pinning the literal to itself (that proves only that nobody edited our copy); it becomes an honest local round-trip contract with the cross-repo dependency documented as unverified. 23 new tests extract the SHIPPED block from real skill files and execute it — every state row, present against a fake launcher so CI never depends on a developer's install, all five broken variants, the injection cases, and the absent path from a non-repository cwd. Migration also covers `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/design-language.md`, `skills/setup/references/integrations.md` and `skills/run-feedback/SKILL.md`. **Diagram decision: the WF14/WF17 "Render Report" station summaries had their tool name corrected, but no station, gate, loop-back or order changed → no workflow-spine change → no diagram REV.** Suite 6542->6481 (net -61: the 82-test engine suite left with the engine; 23 new block tests arrived). Requires claude-skills #26 (`4d7fc97`), which stopped the installed add-on telling users to run the deleted file.

### v3.115.7 (2026-08-01)
- **Shared blocks can target a skill-relative path, and the `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` docs stop lying (part of #807, epic claude-skills#11).** `scripts/sync_shared_blocks.py`'s `_skill_md` appended `/SKILL.md` to every `MANIFEST` entry, so a shared block could only ever be generated into a skill's `SKILL.md`; a `references/` target resolved to a directory-shaped path and raised `NotADirectoryError`. An entry containing `/` is now treated as a skill-relative path and bare names keep resolving to `SKILL.md` — needed by #807, where two of the five render call sites live in `implement-feature/references/steps.md` and `fix-bug/references/steps.md`. Two subprocess tests in a sandboxed tree copy cover both forms; the skill-relative one was red-before-green with the exact `NotADirectoryError`, and the bare-name companion proves the extension did not break today's behaviour. Separately, three places asserted that `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` "does not expand in body text" (`CLAUDE.md`, this script's own docstring, and the drift guard's) — **that flat claim is false and it had already propagated into a design.** Measured on Claude Code 2.1.220: it IS substituted into a `SKILL.md` body at skill-LOAD time, it is NOT substituted when a file is opened with the Read tool (so `references/*.md` never sees it), and it is NOT a shell environment variable; the same line of `pane-handoff/SKILL.md` yields an absolute path one way and the literal the other. The cache's cross-directory path blocking — the other half of the original sentence — remains the real and sufficient reason this generator exists, so the mechanism is unchanged and only the false rationale is removed, with the three-fact version recorded in `CLAUDE.md` §1. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6540->6542.

### v3.115.6 (2026-08-01)
- **The Step-12 deferral gate is now EXECUTED, not re-derived as prose (#796 candidate 3, epic #756).** `assert_pr_body_has_deferred_section` and `assert_deferrals_recorded` have shipped since #138 with **no production caller** — they existed only inside the WF2 completion-gate prose, which is exactly why the #781 H1 slip fired AFTER merge. New `plan_lib.py assert-pr-body` verb is that caller, and the WF2 completion gate item 13 now runs the command instead of describing the functions. Split out of #796 by owner ruling 2026-08-01: candidate 3 ships alone because it had converged, candidate 1 is re-filed as #822, and the other six stay unshipped for reasons recorded on the issue. Exit codes are three-valued so a caller mistake can never read as a gate pass — `0` holds, `1` fails with findings on stdout, `2` caller error — and both review findings that blocked the candidate as first drafted are enforced: **a plan parsing to ZERO tasks is rc 2, never a vacuous pass** (the pure function returns `(True, [])` on an empty deferred list BY DESIGN, since the section is omitted-when-empty, so a wrong `--plan-file` path would otherwise satisfy the gate silently — "no tasks at all" and "tasks, none deferred" are different facts and only the second is a pass), and **`--gate-file` binds the plan to the gate's recorded `plan_digest`**, refusing a plan revised after the gate was taken and refusing a gate that recorded no digest, mirroring the `gate_stale_for_plan` / `gate_missing_plan_digest` rules `executor_routing_lib` already enforces and reusing `complexity_gate.plan_content_digest` rather than a second digest implementation. Fail-closed throughout, per the §3 decision guide, since this is a completion gate. Eight subprocess tests cover pass, gate-failure, both caller-error refusals, the digest-binding pass and both digest refusals; the unreadable-plan test carries a discriminating assert on the real diagnosis because it would otherwise have passed on the parent commit for the wrong reason (argparse rejecting an unknown subcommand with rc 2 — the #730 review trap). Two self-inflicted defects caught by running the WHOLE test file rather than only the new class: the new helpers were named `_run`/`_plan`/`_gate` and **shadowed a module-level `_run` six existing `TestCloseDesignGateCLI` tests depend on** (renamed `_apb_*`), and loading `complexity_gate` a second time via `spec_from_file_location` broke its dataclasses (now the established `importlib.import_module`). No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6532->6540.

### v3.115.5 (2026-08-01)
- **Pane-handoff teardown refused on every evaluated goal, so old panes never closed (#782, epic #756).** `live_owner_goal(strict=True)` trusted a `goal_status` row only when it sat at the record's top-level `attachment` AND carried `sentinel: true` AND had a boolean `met` AND a non-blank condition. A Stop-hook goal EVALUATION satisfies every one of those except the sentinel, so after any evaluation the newest row was classed "fails validation", strict mode raised, and teardown exited before a single handoff step ran — hit three consecutive sessions (D47, D55, D65), with `--no-teardown` as the only escape. Measured before fixing rather than assumed: across the 25 most recent transcripts, of 122 trusted-origin rows **85 are exactly this shape** — unstamped, `met: false`, carrying the armed condition **byte-identical** — because that is what the evaluator writes. Two candidate fixes were ruled out on evidence, not taste. Dropping the sentinel check outright would have broken both #758 forgery guards, which construct forged rows at *precisely* the position real evaluator rows occupy, so origin-plus-sentinel cannot separate the two. Fixing the writer is not available here either: `grep -rln goal_status hooks/` returns one file, reader-only, so those rows are written by Claude Code's own harness and stamping them would be an upstream request. The shipped fix narrows the refusal instead, via a new `_corroborates()` predicate: an unstamped row is CORROBORATION rather than ambiguity when `met` is literally `False` **and** its condition is byte-equal to the row already trusted. Both forgery directions stay dead by construction — a sentinel-less `met: true` still cannot spoof "already cleared", and a sentinel-less row proposing a different condition still cannot inject a goal, because with no trusted row to agree with there is nothing to match. Corroboration deliberately does NOT clear a prior `suspicious` flag, so a torn line earlier in the tail still refuses. The refusal message and two skill-doc passages were rewritten in the same commit: they named the evaluator case as "the usual cause", which after this change is actively misleading — that stale-diagnosis defect is exactly what #802 was filed about and what PR #803 left in place by improving only the wording. **Supersedes #802's guidance: an armed goal is no longer a precondition for teardown.** The #803 remedy test is restated rather than deleted, asserting the new pass and keeping the history of why it flipped, plus a companion test proving the remedy is still named when a refusal genuinely IS correct. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6529->6532.

### v3.115.4 (2026-08-01)
- **A pane-handoff successor was never told to put the visible task list back up (#819, epic #756).** Found live: session `811192ca` was handed the epic #756 queue via `[handoff-epic756-d65]`, worked it for ~40 minutes with nothing on screen, and the owner had to ask where the task list had gone. Root cause is a seam between two skills, both individually correct: `skills/epic-run/SKILL.md:86-94` owns the run's task list and even handles the resumed case explicitly ("a resumed run — including one created by a PRIOR session of the same run — refresh it instead of creating a second list"), but a successor resumed by **pane-handoff** never reaches that step, because the handoff prompt is its only instruction and `skills/pane-handoff/SKILL.md` did not mention a task list anywhere (`grep -riE 'tasklist|task list|taskcreate'` over the skill returned nothing). The predecessor followed its skill correctly; the skill simply never asked. Classic silent-failure shape for this epic — the run continues and reads as healthy while the owner loses the one surface showing where the work is. Fixed by adding a third prompt requirement beside the two the command already enforces, explicitly labelled as the one the command CANNOT enforce: the prompt must tell the successor to check `TaskList` first and **refresh** an existing list rather than duplicate it. It must say so in **PROSE, never a bare `/tasklist`** — `launcher_lib.validate_inserted_prompt:550-566` records the #718 measurement that a bare slash command is inert inside a goal loop, where `/tasklist` sat queued through five goal-driven turns while prose was acted on in 17 seconds. Deliberately NOT a CLI refusal: a purely additive `--no-teardown` helper handoff has no list worth carrying, so a hard refusal would produce false blocks. Guarded by a two-sided drift test in `tests/test_pane_handoff_skill.py` anchored to the one skill file (repo CLAUDE.md mistake #6) — requiring the words alone would pass on a skill that told the successor to paste a bare `/tasklist`, so the refresh-not-duplicate rule is asserted too. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6528->6529.

### v3.115.3 (2026-08-01)
- **Spike evidence for epic #756's five owner-blocked children, in one decision document (epic #756).** Five children (#794, #792, #797, #777, #796) were each blocked on an owner ruling whose evidence lived scattered across a measurement doc, three unmerged evidence branches, and a dozen issue comments — so the rulings could only be made from a summary of a summary. `docs/planning/2026-08-01-epic-756-spike-evidence.md` collects the primary evidence per decision: spike S3's full cache-reuse table (four of five dispatches paid a ~45-51k prefix write, one paid zero and did not repeat) together with the three experiment defects that make it INCONCLUSIVE rather than a NO — dispatches 8.5 min apart when the doc claimed 40 s, provider inputs that were never byte-identical because `prompt_hash` covers only `req.prompt` while the cached prefix spans tools + system, and every write landing on the 1-hour tier when the adapter comment and `phase_executor/README.md` both reasoned from 5 minutes; #735's build-seat spike (works end to end in 17.8 s, canary green, and it isolates by per-dispatch git worktree rather than splitting a pane, so the owner's mental model and the shipped design genuinely diverge); #792's three scope decisions with the four real defects its gates caught; #797's projection table showing the requested 55/75 thresholds fire 5-24 points LATER than today's 50 on a meter that already overshoots; #777's finding that half of AC1 already shipped in #506/#589 and only token attribution is missing; and #796's eight-candidate re-triage. Two author process failures are recorded rather than buried, because in both cases the sequence is the finding: S3 produced four successive wrong answers before review stopped one from unblocking two children on a single anomalous data point, and #796's design shipped a fabricated "verified against the live corpus" claim whose pattern would have rejected five of six real releases. Deployed public to Vercel per the standing design-doc mandate (`rawgentic-analysis-756-spikes`), with the derived docs index refreshed. **Deliberate deviation, recorded because CLAUDE.md §4.13 forbids it by default: this page's `.html` is HAND-AUTHORED, not `render_artifact.py` output** — owner instruction 2026-08-01 after reviewing the `--style report` render, which met no part of the standing visual-quality bar (one flat type weight, no hierarchy pointing at the verdicts, and the template's markdown parser silently dropped every single-asterisk emphasis: measured 34 literal asterisks and 10 literal `---` rules in its output, with `<em>` count zero). The hand-authored page keeps the `.md` as source of record and adds what the decision needs: a cyanotype-blueprint VDL, the S3 cache measurement as the hero (four ~45-51k write bars against one at zero), a sticky five-stop decision rail, and a verdict chip per section. Verified by looking at the rendered page in both themes plus 390/768/1440 widths, zero horizontal overflow. Follow-up worth filing: fold this treatment into a `render_artifact.py` style so future docs inherit it rather than hand-rolling, and fix the template's missing emphasis support. Docs-only; no hook, skill or test behavior changes. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6528->6528.

### v3.115.2 (2026-08-01)
- **`ad-hoc-handoff` had no `--predecessor-goal-condition-file`, breaking a pair its own surface establishes twice (#730, epic #756).** `--goal-condition` and `--resume-prompt` each have a `-file` twin; the third condition flag did not, and nothing signalled it — the skill documented the inline form correctly, so this was an API-shape trap rather than doc drift. It failed live during the epic #722 hand-off (`unrecognized arguments: --predecessor-goal-condition-file`), costing a wasted invocation plus a `--help` round-trip at the moment that session had the least context to spend; recoverable only because a human was driving, and a dead hand-off headless. The missing form is also the one the repo's own convention wants: a goal condition routinely carries backticks and `$(...)`, the exact hazard `CLAUDE.md` §2 answers with a file rather than a command line. Added as a mutually-exclusive pair, `required=False` because — unlike the goal/prompt pairs — this whole input is optional, which is why it routes through a new `_read_optional_text_arg` wrapper rather than `_read_text_arg` directly: both-absent must return `None` instead of reaching `open(None)`. The wrapper DELEGATES, so there is still exactly one file reader, and an unreadable path still fails loudly naming the path. A supplied-but-blank value is refused too — but note the rationale, which Step-11 review corrected: a blank value here does NOT hand the successor an empty goal. The successor's guard is the separate required `--goal-condition` pair, blank guards are already refused by `build_goal_command`, and the clear receipt binds to the transcript-derived `live_condition`, never to this caller-supplied string, which is assertion-only. Blank is refused because it is almost always a file the caller meant to fill, and accepting it would emit a misleading "not the newest guard" diagnostic instead of naming the real problem. The same review also caught that the missing-file test passed on the parent commit for the wrong reason (the old parser rejected the unknown option and echoed the path), so it now rejects `unrecognized arguments` explicitly. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6520->6528.

### v3.115.1 (2026-08-01)
- **`usage_capture` counted every assistant message once per CONTENT BLOCK; token and cost telemetry was inflated 3.49x (#812, epic #756).** `parse_session_jsonl` summed `message.usage` from every `type == "assistant"` transcript row, but Claude Code writes **one row per content block** of a single message and each row repeats that message's **cumulative** usage — so a message emitting text plus two tool calls was counted three times. Measured on a real transcript: 390 usage rows collapse to **137 distinct messages**, and output tokens fall **562,137 -> 160,978**. Every run-record `usage` object and every `cost_estimate_usd` derived from it was overstated, and because the factor scales with tool-calls-per-message it was **not even constant across runs** — comparing two runs' costs partly compared their tool-call density. Fixed by de-duplicating before accumulation on `message.id` (the billed unit) -> `requestId` -> row `uuid`; a row carrying none of the three is still counted, and the same identity recurring with divergent usage keeps the first and warns on stderr rather than silently picking. `message.id` leads deliberately — `requestId` is a transport identifier reusable across a retry — and the two were measured 1:1 on the observed corpus (137/137, zero collisions either direction). Found while mapping #777, which cannot reconcile per-phase totals against an inflated whole. **Records written before this fix carry inflated values, so pre/post comparisons are invalid**; they are distinguishable by the run-record's `workflow_version` field. A Step-9 review then caught that an EMPTY `message.id` would override a good `requestId` and collapse two distinct messages — a silent UNDER-count, harder to notice than the over-count being fixed — so falsy identity values are treated as absent (measured unreachable today: 0 of 271,196 real rows on this host carry an empty or missing `message.id`, fixed anyway). No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6511->6520.

### v3.115.0 (2026-08-01)
- **`Observation.usage` now separates cache WRITES from cache READS; spike S3 is recorded as INCONCLUSIVE and its gate stays shut (#794, epic #756).** #794's own AC2 asked for "a measured before/after on `cache_creation_input_tokens`" — **unmeasurable against the shipped code**: `adapters/claude_cli.py` mapped `cached` to `cache_read_input_tokens` and **discarded the creation counter**, with the observation schema `additionalProperties: false` so nothing downstream could add it. `usage.cache_write` fixes that (Anthropic lane reports it, sum of the 5m and 1h tiers; other lanes yield 0). **The spike verdict is deliberately NOT shipped as a result.** Five dispatches gave `cache_write` 49,106 / 46,825 / **0** / 50,941 / 45,013, and Step-11 review established the run cannot support any verdict: the dispatches were **not** evenly spaced (D4 came 8m31s after D3, not "back-to-back" as an earlier draft claimed), the provider inputs were **not** identical even though the brief was (`prompt_hash` covers only the brief; the cached prefix spans tools + system + messages), and **every write landed in the 1-hour tier, not the 5-minute tier** — which invalidated the TTL reasoning in the first two drafts of the doc, the adapter comment and this changelog. **#799 and #795 remain BLOCKED**: an inconclusive gate is not a pass. `docs/measurements/2026-08-01-794-spike-s3-cache-reuse.md` records all four successive wrong readings (n=1 "works", n=2 "fails", n=3 "works with warmup", n=5 "fails") and specifies what a controlled re-run needs. The one solid conclusion: with the counters conflated, the same data supported three contradictory verdicts — which is the argument for landing the telemetry before the optimisation. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6508->6511.

### v3.114.1 (2026-08-01)
- **The usage rate card was wrong in both directions; every dollar column before today is unreliable (#791, epic #756).** Verified against live published pricing retrieved 2026-08-01 from `platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing` — deliberately NOT the `claude-api` skill's model table, whose cached snapshot is what let the card drift. **Every row was wrong, not just the two the issue named:** `claude-fable-5` sat at Sonnet rates ($3/$15 vs the real $10/$50, **3.3x UNDER**); `claude-opus-5`/`4-8`/`4-7` at deprecated Opus-4.1 rates ($15/$75 vs $5/$25, **3x OVER**); `claude-sonnet-5` missed its introductory cut ($2/$10 through 2026-08-31); and `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` carried **Haiku 3.5's** prices ($0.80/$4 vs $1/$5). The fable error mattered most: the card priced it BELOW opus-5 when it is actually **2x** opus-5 per token, inverting the orchestrator-cost comparison drawn from it. `RATE_CARD_SOURCE` now records the URL and retrieval date in the module, and new guards pin every row against live pricing, assert the published cache multipliers (1.25x write / 0.1x read) hold for every row, pin the fable-is-2x-opus direction, and **fail once Sonnet 5's introductory pricing lapses on 2026-08-31** so the card cannot rot silently. `cache_write` is documented as the 5-minute tier — the transcript exposes one `cache_creation_input_tokens` field with no tier discriminator, so a 1-hour write is under-counted; stated rather than silently assumed. **127 of 188 historical run-records carry a dollar figure computed with the old rates**; the store is append-only so they are NOT rewritten, and `docs/run-records.md` plus the owner-notes measurement doc now carry correction notes saying so. Token counts are unaffected — they are summed from transcripts, never priced. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6498->6508.

### v3.114.0 (2026-08-01)
- **WF2's Step-4 design gate now CLOSES budget-exhausted instead of escalating the owner (#798, epic #756).** Six consecutive epic-#756 children hit that escalation — #735, #733, #758, #767, #765, #762 — and all seven owner answers (counting the ratification) were identical: apply the final fixes, proceed, no further pass. The escalation is codified away and the close is loud. The carve-out is deliberately narrow: self-close requires the `design` SOURCE cap reached AND the global cap NOT reached AND the ambiguity breaker returning `clear`; exhaustion of `spec_tighten`/`tdd`/`review`/`review_design`, a global-cap refusal, and any ambiguous or conflicting finding all still STOP and escalate. The global-cap condition is load-bearing because `consume_loopback` tests the source cap first and returns before the global check, so a `{design:2, spec_tighten:1, total:3}` state refuses for the source reason and would otherwise read as design-cap-caused. The breaker now runs BEFORE the close — the volume-loopback path previously skipped it and relied on a next Step-4 pass, which a terminal close does not have, so ambiguous findings could have closed unescalated. New `plan_lib.budget_exhausted_close` + `design_close_eligible` and an executable `python3 hooks/plan_lib.py close-design-gate` adapter make the close provable by running it rather than by pinning prose: it enforces eligibility itself, exits non-zero writing nothing when ineligible, and emits an `adopted` ledger entry per applied finding (never `declined` — reviewers are instructed not to re-raise declined findings, so a declined security finding could be buried even if its fix never landed), a TOP-LEVEL run-record `extra` row (a gate-row key validates silently and renders nothing), and the canonical session marker. `MAX_DESIGN_LOOPBACK_ITERATIONS` stays **2** — the retrospective data is right-censored (every measured run stopped at three passes, so no fourth-pass evidence exists), and the owner chose automatic close over raising the budget; every close now records `passes=N`, making the real distribution measurable. **Scoped to WF2 only** — WF3 has no `design` source, no persisted counters and no ledger wiring, so copying the predicate there would be unsafe; filed as #808. Known limitation, not claimed as covered: a prose guard can still pass while a model skips the adapter command. WF2 diagram REV 3.114.0 (station 04 delta). Suite 6463->6498.

### v3.113.4 (2026-08-01)
- **Epic #756's queue is re-ordered and the path is documented stage by stage (owner decision).** The nine issues from the executor-economics review (#791-#799) are **front-loaded ahead of the remaining original children**, ranked most impactful to least, with #777 folded into the same ranking: **#798, #791, #794, #792, #777, #799, #797, #796, #793, #795**, then the original tail (#766 onward) unchanged. Ranking principle: fix what every remaining child pays for, before running fifteen more children through it — so the cheapest compounding win goes first (#798 removes one design pass and one owner escalation per child, after six consecutive children produced seven identical owner answers), then the one-dictionary fix that makes every downstream number true (#791), then the architecture gate. `docs/planning/2026-08-01-epic-756-front-loaded-path.md` + rendered HTML (hosted at `rawgentic-analysis-epic-path.vercel.app`) give each stage a before/after/you-can-now with its cost, its payback across the fifteen remaining children, and what it unlocks. **One hard dependency is called out inside the block:** #794 carries spike S3 (15 minutes), and if byte-identical briefs cannot hit cache the executor path itself is in question — so #799 and #795, which both invest further in the executor, must not start until that spike returns. Rendered with the `roadmap` template (second doc-type template exercised after `dashboard`, per the template-example work). Docs only, no behavior change. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6463->6463.

### v3.113.3 (2026-08-01)
- **`pane-handoff` teardown refused undiagnosably; the refusal now names its own remedy (#802).** The default path (retire the predecessor pane) validates the newest `goal_status` row fail-closed, and Stop-hook goal EVALUATIONS carry no `sentinel` — so once a session's goal has been evaluated even once, teardown raises exit 2 BEFORE any step runs, which means `failed_step` is `null` and the skill's diagnostic table said nothing at all. Measured across the 120 most recent transcripts: **53 sessions in the refusing state**, 45 clean, 5 with a live goal. The guard itself is correct and is UNCHANGED — what shipped is diagnosability: `live_owner_goal`'s strict-mode `LauncherError` now names both remedies (`/goal clear` then retry, or `--no-teardown` plus manual retirement), Step 1 of the skill states that clearing an armed goal is **load-bearing for the default path** rather than mere `clear-prep` provenance, and the `failed_step` table gains an explicit `null`/exit-2 row. Two claims the report left unverified were confirmed from transcript evidence before acting and are now locked in as tests: `/goal clear` DOES write a sentinel-bearing `met:true` row and DOES rescue teardown (session 7abd6487, rows 40/148/162), and a sentinel-less `met:TRUE` Stop-hook row refuses too — "the goal is done" is not an escape (session 160a9114). The writer asymmetry that causes all this is #782 and is deliberately out of scope here. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6460->6463.

They now live in the `claude-skills` repo, self-contained (engine in `scripts/`, tests in `tests/`, assets in `assets/`) and installed by symlink; three real changes were needed to make them stand alone, none of them a file move: `admit-to-org-runners` dropped its whole `<config-loading>` block and `capabilities_lib` call for a `--repo` argument so it works in any repo, `charter_lib` resolved its bundled asset by walking up into a `skills/` layout that no longer exists, and `org_runners_lib` imports `atomic_write_lib` (still used by 37 files here) so a vendored copy with a provenance header went with it. Every registration surface moved in this commit — whitelist, codex mirror symlinks, shared-block MANIFEST, README table + four count strings, the config-loading canary (10->9), and the `.rawgentic.json` description straggler that only `hooks/skill_registration_check.py` caught (15/15 surfaces clean). The marketplace guard was repointed at `adversarial-review` and now asserts the whitelist stays sorted rather than pinning one skill's neighbours. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. ### v3.112.0 (2026-08-01)
- **The executor build seat is wired end-to-end — mint-gate → dispatch → exact-path collect → audited landing is now the PRIMARY WF2/WF3 implementation path (#762, epic #756).** The #767 collection contract goes from shipped-but-unused to the operative Step-8 cycle, proven on this PR's own commits (dogfood: Tasks 3–6 and the WF3 drill each ran the full real cycle on gpt-5.6-terra). Task 1 (orchestrator-inline precursor, R5-A — the gate-reviewed sanctioned exception) makes landing safe and audited: `land_work_product` appends a `landed_work_product` audit record bound to the landing identity, the clean-tree gate becomes a SCOPED dirty check (standing telemetry dirt no longer blocks an unrelated landing — the exact case the run's own probe proved impossible before), and the expected-feature-ref persists at collect and is REQUIRED at land (destination authorization; a same-base-sha replay refuses, R5-B). Task 2 (orchestrator-inline per owner decision D31 — an honest task-scoped gate decides bakeoff on `risk_high` and no build bake-off caller exists, so HIGH tasks structurally cannot dispatch today; the live gate-refusal evidence rides this PR, the gap is #779's) arms the reconcile verb: `unlanded_work_product`, `orphan_landing`, `landing_mismatch`, and `landing_conflict` flip `ok:false`, pre-cutover discards classify into the NAMED report-only `pre_cutover_unverifiable` bucket (R5-D), `run_id` passes through, and `LANDING_IDENTITY_FIELDS` goes public with a mirror drift guard. Task 3 retunes the routing table to the owner's verbatim 8-seat matrix (2026-07-31; analysis budget 2.0→10.0; `INCUMBENT_MODEL` → claude-fable-5 — flagged as an owner-veto item R4-E; absent-incumbent now raises loudly; R3-H provenance on `no_sandboxed_mutating_lane`) — and its config-digest change ended run A's dispatch epoch (`run_digest_conflict`, #474 working as designed): run A closed honestly, run B declared, the documented two-run chain with the operational lesson recorded (sequence table retunes LAST). Task 4 lands the WF2 §8 prose truth (audit identity, scoped dirt, dirty baseline, per-workflow-dispatch DISPATCH emission, task-owned legacy restore) and truth-flips the whole-issue-delegation fence to cite follow-up #779 (executor-tier whole-issue receipts + high-risk build path + SHA provenance + build bake-off caller — FILED). Task 5 adopts the build seat into WF3: implementation-role + build contract + the R4-D complexity mapping (`simple_bug`→standard), Step-5 mint-gate plan grammar + plan-level estimated-lines, Step-7 executor-primary, plus a WF3-format mint-gate subprocess e2e. Task 6 flips the docs to the shipped truth (run-records reconciliation states the real landing-audit join; the stale #762 pointer → #779; the false `correlation_id` join-claim corrected) and documents `phaseExecutorTable.file` overrides, `executorRouting` validated-not-selecting, and the R4-G atomic AC1 mutation recipe — executed live this run: all 24 active workspace projects now carry the declarative `executorRouting` block (owner decision D30; backup retained). Task 7 is the AC2 evidence drill: a REAL WF3-format pre-merge dispatch on a throwaway branch (receipt 8a30f0d3, 18.1s, audited landing, byte-exact verify, branch deleted; receipts + landing record persist in run B's audit log), with #730 recorded as the deferred-to-target full check. Step-4 gate: 3 passes, 24 unique findings, 17 dispositions, budget-exhausted close per five identical owner precedents — two owner-veto items presented at the merge gate (R4-E incumbent + the gate close itself). WF2+WF3 diagram REV 3.112.0 (WF2 station 8 delta; WF3 stations 5+7 deltas). Suite 6447->6519.

### v3.111.0 (2026-07-31)
- **The WF2 Step-3 competitive design bake-off is WIRED — owner decision D26, the RETIRE recommendation presented and declined (#765, epic #756).** The carve-out had outlived its closed #472 pointer; the owner-joined fork (two-way /ask-owner RG-761044) chose wiring over retirement. `phase_executor.run_competitive` now accepts and records the caller's `run_id`/`correlation_id` (additive; `Candidate.correlation_id` reaches the AdapterRequest and the harness Observation, so a raising candidate stays attributable — omitted identity keeps the prior random-mint shape byte-compatible), and `hooks/bakeoff_policy.py` gains the workflow-callable `design-round` CLI the Step-3 prose now names verbatim: `--run-id`/`--correlation-id` are REQUIRED (exit 2 — a workflow round can never silently mint a random identity), an interactive judge failure exits 3 fail-loud while `--headless` records a `judge_degraded` incumbent, and `--evidence-out` writes a whitelist-only sanitized record (`sanitize_record`: no payloads, no capture paths; `prompt_sha256`/`rubric_sha256` bind the evidence to its inputs) while the raw sink stays gitignored.
