AC2 is a committed LIVE proving round (`docs/measurements/bakeoff-765-design-round-evidence.json`: sol vs opus concurrent on real quota pools, glm-5.2 judge live, `judge_degraded: false`, run identity `wf2-765-b9a74758`/`765-s8-live-round`) pinned by a fail-closed guard — the artifact's absence FAILS the suite, and a degraded, wrong-model, payload-bearing or absolute-path-bearing record can never pass as evidence. AC4: the shared-block Step-3 row states the wired truth (command, judge-env prerequisite, failure semantics, lane exemption), `_CARVEOUT_TRUTH` flipped with negative pins (no `#472` deferral survives in block, corpus, or `docs/run-records.md`, whose reconciliation section now states the honest per-stream join truth — design rounds carry workflow identity; the build stream's wiring is #762's), and the decision doc pair ships in `docs/planning/` (hosted: rawgentic-765-decision.vercel.app). Owner mid-run scope (decision log D27): the wired round is gated on a per-project `designBakeoff.enabled` opt-in (workspace project entry, DEFAULT OFF, fail-open-to-off) probed via the new `design-round-enabled` CLI verb (exit 0 enabled / 1 disabled) — Step-3 prose dispatches the bake-off only when the gate answers enabled, `_GATE_TRUTH` drift-pins the sentence, and env self-bootstrap + first-adoption + owner-gated default-ON land in follow-up #775. The Step-11 wave then closed the delivery gap all three reviewers converged on — the operative Step-3 command now names `--winner-out` (the only winner-delivery channel; proven live with a second glm-judged round under the hardened CLI) — and hardened the whole boundary: the round path itself re-enforces the opt-in gate (exit 4), score keys are rubric-bound (`_clean_scores`), the judge is pinned to glm-5.2 with `judge_model` in the evidence contract, raw sinks refuse any committable path in any git work tree, identity strings are grammar-validated, output paths must be pairwise distinct, empty briefs refuse, and legacy `run_competitive` callers keep their exact record shape (`correlation_id` omitted, not null). Bonus, flagged: `skills/rev-diagram/scripts/snapshot.sh` gains `--wait-for-timeout=3000` — the sheet's staggered draw-in animation (~1.3s for 19 stations) froze load-time captures with late stations at opacity 0, a defect the previously shipped snapshot pair also carried (undo: drop the flag). WF2 diagram REV 3.111.0 (station 3 delta). Suite 6414->6447.

### v3.110.1 (2026-07-31)
- **Executor build-seat work now lands through a sanctioned, hardened collection path (#767, epic #756).** The first real mutating build dispatch (#735) proved a contained agent cannot commit — its linked worktree's gitdir is read-only under containment, and a live probe showed the issue's widen-the-grant candidate is unworkable (`git add` already needs the common objects DB; a sufficient grant opens most of the shared `.git`). `collect_work_product` (hooks/executor_routing_lib.py) generalizes from appendix-only to an exact-path per-task policy (new `promote_paths_only` factory — component-tuple equality, so a file→directory type replacement cannot smuggle descendants; repeatable `--promote-path` on the CLI), the durable intent and audited bindings carry the promotion identity (`binding_version: 2` with `target_ref` + NUL-joined `paths_digest`; mistargeted or landed-intent re-requests refuse as `intent_conflict`, corrupt intents refuse as `intent_corrupt`, empty worktrees refuse as `empty_work_product`; legacy v1 logs stay readable and reconcile never cross-matches versions), and Step-8 prose documents the two-tier contract — executor: collect onto a temp ref, then a guarded tri-state fast-forward landing bound to the exact symbolic feature ref (bare `update-ref` on a checked-out branch forbidden); legacy: cherry-pick/fast-forward — with whole-issue delegation fenced fail-closed to the legacy architecture pending #762, ratifying-and-superseding the #735 ba4ceec improvisation. Three-pass design gate closed at budget-exhausted per owner precedent (decision log D22); the Step-8a wave then caught 3 High (intent-identity bypass incl. a receipt double-spend path, vacuous empty-commit collection, fence-contradicting operative prose) — all fixed red-before-green; and the Step-11 wave (lane reviewer + adversarial diff, 9 unique findings, TOCTOU breaker adjudicated at source per D23) hardened the whole route to fail-closed: the empty-work-product guard is unconditional (appendix default included), exact-path collects are ENFORCED onto `refs/rawgentic/collect/<receipt-nonce>` with create semantics (`invalid_collect_ref` otherwise; `--kind code` requires `--promote-path`), every unlanded legacy intent refuses, semantically corrupt intents (`{}`, wrong nonce, mistyped or half-bound fields) refuse as `intent_corrupt` and an identity-changed retry first PROVES the old promotion never landed, the guarded landing ships as the production `land-work-product` verb (clean tree, exact symbolic ref, tri-state SHA, ff-only, postconditions, CAS temp-ref delete — the tests exercise the production op, not a facsimile), `PromotionResult` carries the committed tree so collection and reconciliation bind all three worktree snapshots, and the audit binding schemas are mutually exclusive (hybrid v2-fields-no-version records and non-exact-int versions refuse; writers reject empty identity). Tests: live-git end-to-end with binary byte-fidelity + negative landings incl. a CLI subprocess landing, policy/intent/validator units, new §8 drift pins. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6348->6414.
### v3.110.0 (2026-07-31)
- **Owner-authored goals now carry VERBATIM across pane-handoffs, enforced at the handoff boundary instead of trusted to discipline (#758, epic #756).** The measured failure was accretion: owner goals run 1,200–2,000 chars, model-drafted successor goals clustered at 4,000–5,400, and the #720 override rode inside a model-drafted 4,000-char goal — each redraft widening the misinterpretation surface. Now `ad-hoc-handoff`'s retirement path reads THIS session's own transcript (fail-CLOSED when it cannot: it runs before any pane exists, so refusing strands nothing; `--no-teardown` stays the additive-helper escape) and refuses a successor goal that differs byte-for-byte (armed forms, one documented trailing-newline normalization — `strip()` equality was rejected at the design gate) from the live owner goal. The transcript read trusts only `sentinel: true` rows via the new `live_owner_goal` helper — `_find_goal_status` is deliberately recursive, so a forged nested row could otherwise inject a phantom goal or spoof "already cleared" through the sentinel-less `goal_currently_unmet` scan (the pass-2 gate catch, both directions regression-tested). `perform_handoff` gains keyword-only `strict_goal_binding`/`expected_predecessor_goal` (defaults byte-identical for every existing caller): under strict binding the destructive clear re-reads immediately before sending and REFUSES on ANY divergence from the validated snapshot — including a goal appearing where none was (`expected=None` is part of the snapshot) — leaving the pane open (`failed_step: predecessor_goal_binding`) while the successor keeps the validated goal; the residual read→clear race is documented as the platform ceiling (`/goal clear` has no compare-and-clear form; a goal cannot arm mid-turn in a busy pane). A goal change requires the owner's explicit yes/no first, then `--goal-rewrite-approved '<the owner's verbatim answer>'` — a valued flag whose answer rides the output JSON as the audit record, never a bare boolean (no crypto root of trust exists; AC3 permits "rejects or flags"). Refusal messages carry lengths + numeric divergence offset only, never goal content. Skill prose rewritten (the "in its own words" drafting invitation is gone; model state travels in the handoff FILE; the >500-character-paste prohibition is stated) with per-sentence drift guards. Three-pass design gate, budget-exhausted close per owner decisions D15/D18; the Step-8a wave then caught its own Critical (the #707 clear classification consulted the sentinel-insensitive helpers after strict binding passed) plus origin-binding and decode-contract gaps, and the Step-11 wave hardened four more boundaries (affirmative-only approval with consumed-only audit emission and a `--no-teardown` flag refusal; torn/malformed-newest-row ambiguity refuses on strict destructive reads; truncated armed prefixes no longer vouch for differing over-cap texts; `strict_goal_binding` refuses uncoupled parameters via an explicit-snapshot sentinel) — all red-before-green; the pre-existing campaign/mid-child recursive-reader gap is filed as #772, not silently absorbed. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6292->6348.

### v3.109.8 (2026-07-31)
- **The advisory tier stops sending sessions to the skill that never hands over (#732, epic #756).** At the advisory tier `nag_text` told sessions to "run `clear-prep`" — the skill that *prepares* a handoff and deliberately does not perform one — so a compliant session wrote the checkpoint, the handoff file, the resume prompt and the /goal text, then stopped with no successor; #713 fixed exactly this for the directive tier and left the advisory tier (the one most sessions reach since the #716/#717 35%/50% defaults) carrying the bug it had just diagnosed, and the owner watched three parallel sessions do it live on 2026-07-30. The advisory branch now names `/rawgentic:pane-handoff` as the route with `clear-prep` only as the step it wraps, keeps its softer timing ("at the next clean seam", never the directive tier's "Break NOW"), and carries the "`clear-prep` ALONE leaves no successor" clause; `nag_text` gains a pure `herdr_available` param (env read at the caller), and the headless no-launcher branch prefers `pane-handoff` when `HERDR_ENV=1`, keeping the stop-cleanly text only for the genuinely-no-pane case. The second jaw from this epic's own field evidence — advisory prepares, directive permits, so from the advisory tier there was NO compliant path to a successor — is opened too: the pane-handoff provenance gate accepts either own-session tier marker as an explicit `compgen -G` disjunction (directive probed first; the printed marker's tier is the timing authority, never the injectable reminder text; a bare `.*.emitted` and a two-glob `ls` were both rejected by the gates — the former admits future marker types, the latter exits 2 in the advisory-only case), with freshness/standing-authorization deliberately deferred to #760. Tests: per-branch full-canonical-sentence drift guards (the directive sentence captured pre-change proves AC5 against rewrites), executable fixture tests that RUN the gate command per case (each tier alone / both / neither / unknown tier / empty session id), and a `_run` fix popping `HERDR_ENV` so herdr-hosted dev shells can't flip the headless tests. `docs/context-meter.md` documents the three-fact routing precedence. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6273->6292.

### v3.109.7 (2026-07-30)
- **A killed executor seat can no longer read as a passed gate: dispatch success is now an explicit allowlist, applied at every consumer, with the partial output preserved as a lead instead of a lie (#733, epic #756).** The observed failure — a review SIGKILLed at its timeout returning `ok: true` / exit 0 with `parse_status: "timeout"` beside it — was one instance of a class: `verify_post` answers "did we route to the right model?" (identity), never "did the dispatch succeed?", and every consumer equated the two. The new pure predicate `contract.observation_process_failure` (success = `parse_status ∈ {ok, usage_unavailable}`, deny-by-default; timeout evidence, then any non-zero integer exit, then the non-allowlisted status; malformed status fails as `malformed_status`, never a manufactured success) now gates the sync/supervised/resume result assemblies, `recover_run`, `reconcile_run`'s served-call check, and `collect_work_product` authorization — so a killed attempt no longer reconciles as served and a killed build no longer authorizes promoting partial output. Failed dispatches exit 3 (no renumbering) with `partial`/`partial_payload`/`raw_capture_path`/`observation` attached on every correlation-owned failure (foreign-correlation refusals never carry another dispatch's payload); `retryable` derives from the failure class (engine-killed death yes; definite parse/identity failures no — Hermes encodes 4xx submit failures as `parse_error`). The three-pass design gate (owner decisions RG-562362, RG-255373) and the Step-8a wave hardened the fix itself: attested-wrong-identity now outranks non-completed state verdicts (identity_missing deliberately keeps them — synthetic death envelopes are honest deaths), `resolve_parse_status` classifies produced-nothing envelopes `no_response` before the allowlisted `usage_unavailable`, and recovery refuses a child-supplied foreign correlation even when its expected value is null. Prose: the exit-3 gloss + timeout row and the #753 seat-bound `--timeout` default sentence land on the shared block (synced) and fix-bug's bespoke copy, whose stale per-attempt DISPATCH paragraph is harmonized to the #735 owner-ratified per-workflow-dispatch rule; drift guards pin all three surfaces. The Step-11 pre-PR wave then hardened the fix's own boundary (4 more Highs, red-before-green): a timed-out supervised/resume job whose kill was NOT verified is no longer retryable (`Supervisor.job_record` fresh-registry read; residue is not proven death), the completed branches evaluate process-failure evidence BEFORE the unverified verdict (a missing identity no longer makes a failed envelope MORE retryable), `usage_unavailable` with no payload reads as `no_response` on the READER side too (legacy/in-flight producers pre-date the adapter reorder), and the audit READ boundary now schema-validates every inner observation while `collect_work_product` binds its authorizing observation to the passing receipt's seat/run/correlation. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6194->6273.

### v3.109.6 (2026-07-30)
- **The executor's instruction layer stops contradicting its architecture: the always-loaded manuals now route WFn model work to executor seats, the impossible fallback is replaced with a death-proof retry policy, and the stale Step-3 bake-off pointer finally tells the truth (#735, epic #756).** The machinery was never the problem — the spike proved `executor:build` works end-to-end (canary pass, worktree isolation, 17.8 s) — the problem was that the two manuals that never leave context (`~/rawgentic/CLAUDE.md` §2, `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`) named the legacy Agent-tool types (`rawgentic-implementer`, `deep-reasoner`) for exactly the work the executor owns, and the workspace manual's review fallback named a substitution the architecture canary REFUSES (`architecture_self_check`) — a session following it in good faith was routed into a refusal or a violation. This PR ships the in-repo half and carries the exact out-of-repo replacement text: the shared-block Step-3 row now states that #472 closed 2026-07-21 proving read-only seats only and the bake-off decision lives in #765 (the old row claimed #472 as its proof); steps.md §8's modelRouting-delegation block is legacy-conditioned with a new executor-primary sentence (receipts-asserted adoption is #762's surface); and the design doc (owner-ratified rev 4 through three ask-owner round-trips: RG-065627, RG-227616, RG-662910) pins the ratified policies — retry a dead seat ONCE only on proven death, where a mutating attempt's death requires a VERIFIED kill (`supervisor.py:1067-1102` writes timeout observations with unverified kills — residue is not death), and a failed Codex review is retried once then honestly reported NOT OBTAINED (no substitute exists today: the cross-model rule leaves only the just-failed pool eligible, verified against the chain). The standing dispatch authorization is NARROWED to the exact CLI + three seats (new mechanisms need fresh owner approval). Acceptance evidence from THIS run: the first `executor:build` dispatch by a real WF2 run in recorded history (correlation `735-task2-build-r1`, receipts in `.rawgentic/runs/wf2-735-048888d0/`), plus two systemic finds from actually using the seat — with the bake-off unwired, a plan-wide #429 gate that trips any bakeoff reason makes the build seat structurally unusable for that plan (noted on #762), and a mutating build agent cannot COMMIT because the worktree's git metadata is read-only under containment (the run recovered by orchestrator collection of the agent's diff, provenance in the commit message; filed as #767). Three issues filed from this run: #765 (wire-or-retire the bake-off), #766 (receipt-without-observation, previously untracked), #767 (build seat cannot commit). The pre-PR review (two executor review-seat agents) then caught the fix's own residue and hardened it: the delegation conditioning now names its retry/never-blocks items, the same-model-inline sentence is legacy-scoped (the #332 pin deliberately updated), the shared block's contradictory per-attempt DISPATCH paragraph is replaced by the owner-ratified one-line-per-workflow-dispatch emission rule, and `review_fast` is de-seated across WF2/WF3 dispatch prose (it is a #491 lens tier, not a wired seat — `classify_seat` rejects it). No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6190->6194 (+4 drift guards: carve-out truth, Step-8 executor-primary, conditioning reach, review_fast de-seating).

### v3.109.5 (2026-07-30)
- **The `code-review` CI lane is removed — it was the biggest single slice of PR wall-clock and the smallest slice of signal (owner decision 2026-07-30).** `.github/workflows/claude-code-review.yml` (#196) ran Claude's built-in `/code-review` on every PR to `main`. It was **advisory** — never a required check, so a red result never blocked anything — and it **duplicated WF2's own Step 11**, which dispatches two independent reviewers and *is* a hard gate. Cost, measured on this repo's own PRs today: **~6 minutes hosted**, and up to **~15 minutes** when the fleet's single linux runner serialises the lanes (`docs/ci-review-lanes.md` records that serial-vs-parallel gap). Nothing downstream depended on it: no branch protection required it, and its verdict never fed a run-record gate. **`security-review` is retained, deliberately** — it is the one lane with *no* in-workflow equivalent that fires on non-WF2 PRs, so a human-opened PR gets a security pass it would otherwise never get. Removed with it: the whole `tests/test_code_review_workflow.py` guard file (#196's shape assertions, meaningless once the workflow is gone), and the lane's entry in `tests/test_review_lanes.py`'s `LANES` list — which keeps its loop parameterised over one entry rather than being un-generalised, so a future lane is an append. In its place a new `test_code_review_lane_stays_removed` asserts the workflow does **not** exist: this was a judgement call about value, not a bug, so a well-meaning restore should have to delete that guard and say why in the same commit rather than sliding back in. Doctrine updated in three places that would otherwise have gone stale and misled a future reader: `docs/ci-review-lanes.md` (retitled to the singular, with a section recording what was removed and why), the repo `CLAUDE.md` §2 CI-lanes rule, and `skills/setup/SKILL.md`'s activation nudge, which had been globbing `claude-{security,code}-review.yml`. Also fixed the now-dangling `on:`-block comment in the surviving workflow that referred readers to the deleted file for its `paths-ignore` rationale. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6199->6190 (-9 net: the 10-test `test_code_review_workflow.py` file removed, +1 for the new stays-removed guard).

### v3.109.4 (2026-07-30)
- **A complete operative-content audit of the #720 `switch` trim, run because the owner asked how confident I actually was — it found no fourth missing step, and two small information losses that are now restored (follow-up to #720, epic #722).** #720's Step 11 caught three items that had been demoted from operative instruction to rationale, which raised the obvious question: were there more, and had two reviewers' spot-checks found all of them? Spot-checking cannot answer that, so this replaces it with an exhaustive method — **68 candidates extracted mechanically** from the pre-trim 12,369-byte original (every double-quoted message, every fenced block, every bolded directive), each one then adjudicated against the trimmed `SKILL.md` and `references/why.md`. **The headline is a negative result: no operative step, command, or deny-message is missing, and nothing operative is hiding in `why.md`** — that bucket holds exactly two genuine rationale items. The three already fixed were the whole set at that severity. Of 47 initial "absent" flags, 41 were artefacts: lost **bolding** on structural labels (`**File missing**`, `**Malformed JSON**`) whose content survives as prose, or template placeholders renamed for brevity. Two that looked alarming were false positives worth naming so nobody re-raises them: the registry-append `printf` is present with a byte-identical JSON shape (only `<SESSION_ID from call 1>` → `<ID>`), and the headless "not enabled" STOP message is present with "in the project's entry in" shortened to "in". **What the audit did find is a category the per-finding reviews had no way to see: six verbatim user-facing messages had become paraphrased instructions to emit a message.** Four of those are conversational prompts where varying wording is defensible, and pinning chat phrasing is what bloated this file in the first place — they stay paraphrased, deliberately. **Two were real information losses and are restored here:** a failed project lookup now echoes what was typed (`"No project matching '<input>'. Did you mean one of these?"` — previously the user was asked "did you mean one of these" without being told what had not matched), and the config advisory again states that `/rawgentic:setup` **preserves existing values**, which is the sentence that makes it safe to act on. `SKILL.md` 5,651 → **5,766 bytes** (+115), still under the guard's 6,000-byte regrowth ceiling and still −53.4% against the pre-trim original. **The honest limit, unchanged by any of this:** the audit proves the *text* is present, and cannot prove a fresh session holding only the 5,766-byte file behaves correctly — that needs a plugin reinstall and a real bind, which has still never been run. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6199->6199 (0 new tests: this restores prose inside messages no test pins, and the audit is a one-off method, not a guard — the durable guards are `tests/test_switch_skill_trim.py`'s size ceiling and eight rationale anchors from v3.109.2).

### v3.109.3 (2026-07-30)
- **Every executor dispatch that did not hand-tune `--timeout` was silently getting a sixth of its seat's sanctioned budget, and two of two real code reviews had already been killed by it (#735 F4).** `hooks/executor_routing_lib.py` defaulted `--timeout` to a flat **300.0 seconds** while `phase_executor/src/phase_executor/engine.py:86-93` `_effective_timeout` is `min(caller, bounds.timeout_s)` — documented as *"the declared bound tightens, never loosens"*. The seat manifests declare far more: `review` **1800**, `build` **3600**, `analysis` **1200**. Because the clamp only ever tightens, the flat default was not a conservative guard, it was the binding constraint on every un-tuned call — and it discarded 83% of the review seat's budget and 92% of build's. **The cost was already paid twice, measured:** #719's Step 4 review died at `timing_ms: 300053` with `process.exit_code: -9` (a SIGKILL at the default), and its surviving Step 11 reviewer needed **788 s**; #720's Step 11 reviewer needed **399.7 s**. Both would have been killed by the default; both exceeded it on the first try. The fix removes the flat default entirely — `--timeout` now defaults to `None`, meaning *"resolve from this seat's own `bounds.timeout_s`"* via a new pure `resolve_dispatch_timeout()`, applied once in `_do_dispatch` at the single point every dispatch caller routes through. An explicit `--timeout` is still returned unchanged rather than pre-clamped, because `_effective_timeout` owns clamping and one clamp in one place is the property worth keeping. Fail-**safe** rather than fail-closed: a seat declaring no usable bound keeps the historical 300 s instead of dispatching unbounded, and `bool` is rejected explicitly because `True` satisfies `isinstance(x, int)` and would otherwise become a **1-second** timeout. 13 new guards, red before green, covering both real seats, an explicit caller value above and below the bound, and eleven malformed-bound shapes. **Two findings from the same spike are recorded but NOT fixed here**, because one PR is one issue: the `build` seat is fully wired and works — a dispatch completed in 17.8 s, passed the `codex_mutating` canary on all three required checks, and ran inside an executor-created git worktree — so `executor:build`'s zero dispatch count means *never called*, **not** never wired. An engine-level census over all 31 recorded runs found build receipts in only `559-cell1`/`559-cell1b` (development test cells) against `review` receipts in 22 of 31, and **zero** in every real `wf2-*`/`wf3-*` run. That relocates #735's remaining work entirely into the instruction layer, where the always-loaded manuals name `rawgentic-implementer`/`deep-reasoner` and nothing resident in context ever names a build seat. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6186->6199.

### v3.109.2 (2026-07-30)
- **`switch`'s SKILL.md is 54% smaller, the <=3,000-char target is MISSED at 5,591 — and Step 11 is why both of those numbers moved: two independent reviewers found the trim had quietly demoted three OPERATIVE items to rationale (#720, epic #722).** The body is injected into the session as a user message on *every* bind, so its size is a per-invocation tax on every project switch: 225 lines of which only 12 sat inside a code fence, the rest largely rationale re-paid in full on binds where nothing went wrong. `skills/switch/SKILL.md` 12,369 -> **5,651 bytes** (5,591 chars, -54.3%), all of it relocated rather than deleted into a new `skills/switch/references/why.md` (9,699 bytes) that loads only when a step fails or the reader wants the reasoning. `switch` is the **pilot**, not the prize — it is the twelfth-largest skill body; the same split applied to `implement-feature` (51,837) and `fix-bug` (30,669) is worth roughly 8× more, and stays out of scope here deliberately. **The trim first hit a test that forbade its own deliverable.** `tests/test_corpus.py:33` asserted `not (SKILLS_DIR / "switch" / "references").is_dir()` — literally the directory this issue creates. That test's subject is `skill_corpus()`'s behaviour when a skill has *no* `references/` dir, not `switch`'s layout, so the no-references candidate is now **computed from the tree** with a non-vacuity assert so it cannot pass by selecting nothing: a fix, not a relaxation, and the same over-specific-pin class this epic keeps turning up. **Then Step 11 returned FAIL from both reviewers, and that is the value of this child.** Three items had been moved into `why.md` that a bind actually *executes*, leaving them unreachable at the moment they are needed: Step 1 lost free-form target extraction, so `/rawgentic:switch bind me to my-api` would have searched the entire phrase and reported no match; Step 5b item 1 lost both the three protection-level descriptions **and** the instruction to write the answer at the workspace **top level** by full read-modify-write, so a user could choose `sandbox` — *no guards active* — without being shown what it means, or the choice could land in a project entry and silently fail to take; and item 2b was compressed to "Advisory, fail-open", dropping the operative rule that a non-zero exit **or** empty output both mean "nothing to nudge, continue" — one of the issue's own four explicit must-nots. All three are restored. **The floor claim was wrong twice, and is now stated at the size that survived review.** It was first justified as "every remaining character is a test-pinned string" — checked and false: a literal sweep of the seven switch-coupled test files matches only 428 of 4,796 chars. Reviewer 1 then made the sharper point, that 4,844 was not a floor at all because it had been reached *by omitting the operative content above*. Restated honestly at **5,651 bytes**, the per-section budget is frontmatter + preamble 647, Step 1 258, Steps 2/3/4 795, Step 5 bind 924 (two expansion-free bash blocks ≈430 of it), item 1 678, items 2 + 2b 652, the headless verdict 738, item 3b 434, Confirm Ready 114, Step 6 321, section heading 30 — and reaching 3,000 from there means replacing quoted user-facing messages with descriptions of them, which changes what the skill emits. **The new guard was itself fake-green on both reviewers' count, and that fix is the more useful half.** A byte floor on `why.md` passes happily while an entire rationale section is deleted: removing `why.md:103-158` drops 3,105 bytes and still leaves 6,594 over a 5,000-byte floor. `tests/test_switch_skill_trim.py` now also pins eight short topic anchors, one per relocated rationale area — deliberately topic keys rather than sentences, because pinning prose verbatim would recreate precisely the `test_corpus.py:33` defect this issue had to repair — and it was confirmed RED on reviewer 1's exact deletion before being accepted as green. The regrowth ceiling rose 5,200 -> 6,000 **in the same commit that restored the three operative items**, which is the raise the guard's own failure message prescribes for an operative addition; it fired on its own author, which is the guard working rather than a nuisance. `tests/test_switch_loads_project_manual.py` — #721's LOCATION pin, written specifically to fail if this very trim swept the bind-time load step into `references/why.md` as prose — passed throughout. **Two further findings are real but PRE-EXISTING, and are filed rather than fixed here**, both confirmed byte-identical at `origin/main`: a headless bind to a `configured: false` project never reaches the fail-closed headless verdict at all, because Step 5 skips item 5b wholesale (base `SKILL.md:119`; 0 of 24 active projects affected today, and already carried as #721's open question), and the registry-append `printf` interpolates workspace-derived names and paths into shell literals. AC3 was verified live against the trimmed procedure: exactly one well-formed registry line appended (376 -> 377), `lastUsed` advanced, the staleness nudge advisory and fail-open, the config verdict reported, and all 24 active projects left active. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6183->6186.

### v3.109.1 (2026-07-30)
- **The four `CLAUDE.md` tiers stopped contradicting each other — and the review that checked the checking found two safety rules had been silently deleted (#719, epic #722).** Phase 1 is **placement only**: text moves byte-for-byte between tiers, or is deleted where a verbatim-equivalent copy already survives; the single piece of new prose is the tier map. Three of the four files live in **no git repo** (owner ruling D12), so the PR carries `projects/rawgentic/CLAUDE.md` plus a design doc whose placement tables **are** the reviewable artifact for the other three, with `claude_docs/md-backups/epic-722/719-pre-*.md` as their only undo. **The highest-value move is the one nobody would have noticed was missing:** `operating-instructions.md` — which loads in *every* session in *every* directory — had **no never-push-to-`main` rule at all**, no merge-authorization rule, and no test-first mandate for features (its existing floor covered only reproducing a reported *bug*). Those, plus two escalation gates and escalation hygiene, moved up from the workspace tier, which in exchange gave up 12 of its 19 mistake-catalog entries that duplicated the project manual in staler form. **The instructive part is the granularity.** Step 4's design gate — itself killed at the 300 s dispatch default mid-sentence, which is #733 — got one finding out before dying: a `delete-dup` survivor was materially weaker than the table claimed, because the verdicts had been made at **headline** granularity and never at **clause** granularity. That turned a clause-level verbatim diff of every losing copy against its named survivor into a **prerequisite of the edit rather than a review afterthought**, and the pass found the design's own framing understated the exposure three ways: the tables carry **22 deletions, not the 12** the brief named; **6** rule-3 instances where the doc claimed **2**; and **3 of the 6 real problems sat in the 10 deletions that were never in the checked set**. One verdict **flipped outright** — workspace §6.1 stays, because it is a *consent gate* under "Stop and ask the user" while its proposed survivor only requires knowing the undo and explicitly says it *"never adds a hold"*. Also kept rather than deleted: "Leave `.claude/` untracked", whose only other copy is one project's manual, so a blanket delete would have dropped it for every other project. **Then Step 11 returned FAIL and caught three more, all confirmed at source.** The universal tier had been made to **both forbid and require a hold** — two consent gates moved into the file whose preamble says it never adds one, which is the *same* distinction applied correctly to §6.1 and missed one tier out; the **handoff-file append-only rule had no survivor in any tier**, because the accepted survivor attaches "append-only" to session notes alone (that pair had been marked "clean" by the clause pass — wrongly); and the destructive-command gate had moved **below the tier map's own stated minimum**, so a workspace-root session lost the `git status`/`git diff` pre-check and the warning that `git checkout -- <path>` also discards uncommitted changes. All three restored. **Two acceptance criteria were documented as owner-only and that was false.** The claim "a skill cannot invoke `/context`" was asserted, propagated into a run handoff, and used to defer AC7 and AC8 — until a reviewer disproved it with one command: `claude -p '/context' --output-format json --no-session-persistence --tools ""` runs **locally**, `num_turns: 0`, `total_cost_usd: 0`, `duration_api_ms: 0`. Measured memory-file tokens: `/tmp` **9.2k** (user + universal via `@import`, no workspace manual), `~/rawgentic` **14.1k** (no project manual), project subtree **24.7k**. AC8 is therefore **mechanically verified with zero owner involvement**, and it independently corroborated the third High finding; only the *pre-edit* reading is missing, which was a process miss rather than a tool limit. A deferral is a claim and needs the evidence a confirmation needs. Both Step-11 reviewers were **confirmed non-empty before the gate was treated as met** — `parse_status`/`process.exit_code` read directly, never the `ok` field — because #721 merged on single-reviewer coverage after one died silently; the surviving reviewer needed **788 s**, 2.6× the dispatch default, which is now evidence on the executor spike (#735). Total bytes 65,849 -> 64,488 (-2.1%), reconciling to ~3,907 duplicate deleted plus 2,219 relocated; the saving is *smaller* than the pre-review figure because restoring those two safety rules put 1,694 bytes back, which is the review working rather than a regression. Phase 2 (per-rule shrinking) stays out of scope, and the per-project cleanup splits into a cross-project duplicate-strip plus one issue per project. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6183->6183 (0 new tests: no test pins any manual's content, and pinning prose phase 2 exists to rewrite would fight its purpose; the v3.109.0 entry's "6182" was a transcription slip, re-measured as 6183 on the identical tree).

### v3.109.0 (2026-07-30)
- **Binding a project now actually puts its rules in context — and the four design gates it took to get there are the more useful half of this entry (#721, epic #722).** `/rawgentic:switch <p>` bound a session and left the project's `CLAUDE.md` outside the window: the harness auto-loads a manual lazily, on the first use of one of **its own** file tools (`Read`/`Edit`) inside a subtree, and `switch` reads every scrap of config through shell one-liners — deliberately, so a `Bash(printf:*)`-style allow-rule can auto-approve the bind without a prompt. The harness cannot see inside a bash one-liner, so the project tier arrived whenever a session happened to open a file in that repo, and never at bind. Measured live twice on the way in: binding `sysop` all-shell loaded nothing, while a later `Read` of `projects/sysop/.rawgentic.json` injected that manual in full; and during this very run a `Read` of a small test file pulled all 26,569 chars of `projects/rawgentic/CLAUDE.md` into a session whose all-shell bind minutes earlier had not. Same skill, two runs, two outcomes, the only variable being which tool the model reached for — which is the whole bug. **The fix is one `Read` tool call on the bound project's `.rawgentic.json`**, placed after the fail-closed Headless Access Check and before `Confirm Ready`; the harness turns that into a full `CLAUDE.md` injection, and the injected copy is the one that carries memory-file authority rather than being data the model has merely seen. Reading the *config* rather than the manual is what makes the 14-of-24 projects with no `CLAUDE.md` bind **silently** — no existence check, no branch, no message; AC2 is satisfied structurally, by doing less. **The instructive part is what got deleted.** Earlier revisions added a shell `test -f` guard for one cosmetic reason — avoiding a "file not found" on those 14 projects — and that guard, not the feature, generated **every** security finding across four cross-model review passes: command injection through a project path containing `$(...)` (verified live, `"a$(id -u)b"` came back as `a1000b`, and `new-project` accepts absolute external paths), a symlink escape past `wal-bind-guard`'s lexical own-project prefix check, and a check-then-use window between the guard and the read. Each fix was more shell, so each fix grew the surface; the plain version has none of it, because a `Read` tool call runs no shell at all. Three claims made in those revisions were **wrong and are retracted in the design doc rather than quietly dropped**: `wal-bind-guard` was declared `fail-loud` when its own line 7 says fail-**open**; a bounded 60-line read was said to degrade gracefully when line 60 falls *before* the operative sections of both large manuals (rawgentic's conventions start at line 71, chorestory's Golden Rules at 81) and its cost claim was out by 4×; and the assertion that no pytest could verify the injection was false — `claude --plugin-dir` exists and this repo already runs `RUN_LIVE` live-CLI tests, so the dependency is now recorded as **verification deferred to target** (#138) with a local proxy and an owner-attended `/context` check, not dressed up as untestable. The residual symlink exposure is stated plainly as **unchanged by this PR** — the harness already injects whatever a manual points at, unprompted, the first time any session opens any file in that project; this alters *when*, never *whether* or *what* — and the real fix is filed against `wal-bind-guard`, where own-project reads are authorised for every caller instead of one. 7 new drift guards read `skills/switch/SKILL.md` **directly** rather than through `skill_corpus`, because this is a location pin: sibling #720 trims this same file by ~75% into `references/why.md`, and a corpus read would still pass after the step was swept in there as rationale. They pin one canonical operative sentence carrying tool class + target + the Bash prohibition together, three ordering anchors (after the registry append, after the headless verdict, before Ready) with an explicit non-vacuity check so none can pass by matching nothing, the mandatory silence, and the fail-honestly-not-Ready rule. Also recorded as follow-ups, all found during the design and none solved here: rebinding accumulates manuals with no precedence and no harness unload; the headless verdict is skipped entirely for unconfigured projects; and model context is not enforcement. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6175->6182.

### v3.108.0 (2026-07-29)
- **The meter stopped hoping and started typing: at the act tier it now INSERTS A PROMPT (#718, epic #626).** #713 made the meter speak where `/goal` decides whether to re-prompt, but it still spoke by *injecting text* — and #713's own probe 12 had already shown injected hook text is **data a model may decline**, naming the directive as possible prompt injection and refusing its imperative while faithfully reporting it. An inserted prompt arrives as **user input**, the one channel treated as authoritative. **AC6 was blocking and it was answered by measurement, not argument**: in a scratch pane running a real unmet `/goal`, prose inserted via `herdr pane send-text` + a separate `Enter` was **acted on in 17 seconds** — the marker file landed while the next tick file did not yet exist, so the loop was provably still running — whereas a **bare slash command sat queued through five goal-driven turns** and was taken up only after `✔ Goal achieved`. Both verdicts rest on file mtimes rather than scraped terminal text. A third observation (a bare slash at a *met* goal, which did execute) is recorded in the design doc as **non-discriminating and explicitly not used as evidence**, because the predecessor session's retracted mistake had exactly that shape. So AC3's "prose, never a bare slash command" is **load-bearing, not stylistic**: the wrong form of the same message is inert for precisely as long as the loop it exists to interrupt keeps running, and `validate_inserted_prompt` refuses anything whose stripped text starts with `/` while deliberately still allowing prose that *contains* a command. **Then the WF5 cross-model design review returned 6 findings (2 High) and all six were confirmed against the code — none refuted, none applied piecemeal.** Four became design changes: the insert takes its **own `stop-insert` reservation** rather than sharing the emit's, released on failure, because sharing it would have spent the session's single attempt on a transient herdr hiccup and left only the channel a model may refuse (H1); the once-only claim is restated with all four keys, `(session, window, directive, stop-insert)`, since the marker's identity includes the channel (M6); "bounded" became a number (M3); and insertion now **requires a resolved project, fail-closed** (H2) — the kill switch lives in a project's `.rawgentic.json`, so outside a project it could not be reached, and the first draft had accepted default-on there while arguing the prose "only asks", which contradicts the very section that relies on the imperative being obeyed. That guard keys on the **project path, not on the config block being non-empty**, because most projects carry no `contextMeter` block at all — this repo included, verified — so an empty-block test would have silently disabled the feature everywhere it matters. **The review's other two findings became a live spike, and it caught a bug that would otherwise have shipped green.** A throwaway `Stop` hook in a scratch session established that the hook **does** inherit `HERDR_ENV`/`HERDR_PANE_ID` and **may** spawn a subprocess — and that an `Enter` sent immediately after the paste returns **rc 0 and submits nothing**: the text sat in the input box through two further goal-driven turns and past goal completion until a later bare Enter arrived. Every gate would have reported success over a paste nobody submitted. With `time.sleep(1.5)` between the paste and the Enter, still inside the hook, it submits — so the hypothesis that produced that experiment ("Claude Code cannot read input while a hook runs, therefore no in-hook delay can work") was itself **refuted**, and round 1 was a race with the bracketed paste rather than a barrier. `INSERT_SUBMIT_DELAY_S = 1.5` is documented as **evidenced, not tuned**: it is the only value measured, so lowering it needs its own measurement. The return value is named `delivered`, never `submitted`, and an earlier plan to verify submission by re-reading the pane was **dropped as unsound rather than shipped** — `pane_shows_unsubmitted_paste` needs a *collapsed* paste marker that short prose never produces, and its own contract records that the marker also appears on successful submissions, so the check could not discriminate in either direction. What the pane read does instead is veto: it runs **before** typing and refuses when a permission dialog is on screen, because an `Enter` accepts whatever is there rather than starting a turn. Terminal primitives stay in `launcher_lib.py` per `skills/pane-handoff/SKILL.md`'s one-rule-that-must-not-drift, as a new `insert-prompt` subcommand over the existing `build_send_text_argv`; the meter reaches it through a single bounded subprocess, which narrows this module's older no-subprocess stance on purpose rather than by accident. New `contextMeter.insertPrompt` kill switch, strictly boolean because `insert_enabled` compares with `is True` and a truthy `"yes"` would be staged by setup and read as OFF by the hook — the exact defect #701 exists to prevent. 40 new tests pin the prose-only rule at both the function and CLI level, the paste-delay-Enter ordering, the permission-dialog veto, release-and-retry after a failed insert, and that the emit's own `stop` reservation is never consumed. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6131->6175.

### v3.107.3 (2026-07-29)
- **The context meter was safe and still too late to be useful, so the defaults move to 35%/50% (#716, epic #626).** #687 sized the 60/70 pair against a measured auto-compaction ceiling — sampled 1M sessions reach 99.5–100% before anything resets them, so a 70% directive carried ~30 points of margin. That reasoning was sound and answered the wrong question: **surviving until compaction was never the binding constraint, having room to hand over well is.** #713's run took the directive at 70% of a 1M window — 300,000 tokens still in hand — and rode to ~98% anyway, with the quality of its work degrading in step with the pressure and its final task abandoned rather than done badly. At 35% of the same window a session has ~650,000 tokens: enough to finish the phase it is in, write a resume prompt worth reading, and verify the handoff landed. So the shipped pair is now deliberately **early, not merely safe** — 35% to start writing, 50% to go — with compaction margin restated as a floor these must clear rather than the number they are set to. The gap is 15, above `MIN_TIER_GAP_PCT`, so the pair survives `thresholds()` rather than being silently discarded back to defaults. Every current-state surface moved with the constants (`docs/context-meter.md` tier table, config table and example; `docs/config-reference.md` table and example; `skills/setup/SKILL.md` shipped-default prose; the README hook table), while historical changelog entries keep their original numbers because they are history. Three test classes needed the change and each for a different reason, which is the useful part: the defaults assertion, the fallback-on-bad-config assertion, and the **tier-band fixtures** — `ADVISORY_ROW` was 124,000 tokens (62% of 200k), which is advisory under 60/70 and **directive** under 35/50, so a fixture that silently changed meaning would have made `test_stop_never_carries_the_advisory_tier` assert nothing. It is now 80,000 (40%). Likewise the setup validator's per-key case `checkInPercent: 50` was valid against a default act of 70 and is a zero-gap pair against 50, so it moved to 30. Per-project `contextMeter` blocks and the env twins are untouched and still override, so any project can keep the old pair. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6131->6131.

### v3.107.2 (2026-07-29)
- **The context meter never spoke where `/goal` decides to keep going — and one of its two arms had never spoken at all (#713, epic #626).** A real run armed `/goal` with "LOOP until DONE", shipped 14 PRs, and rode a 1M window to **~98%** without handing off; its `Stop` hook fired ~10 times and not one firing carried a handoff instruction, because `context_meter` was registered on `PostToolUse` + `UserPromptSubmit` and **`/goal` re-prompts at `Stop`**. AC1/AC2 were blocking and both landed before any production edit, with the probe harness committed under `docs/planning/2026-07-29-713-probes/` so every citation is re-runnable. **Five live probes on Claude Code 2.1.220 established the contract instead of assuming it**, and two of them changed the design: **(1)** at `Stop` there is **no read-only channel** — both `decision: "block"` and `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` continue the conversation (`hooks.md:2271`), measured as a second assistant turn for a one-prompt session, so an ungated `Stop` arm would have turned a convenience nag into a turn-blocker; **(2)** injected hook text is treated as **data, not instruction** — inside a real `/goal` loop the model named the injected directive as a possible prompt injection and declined to act on its imperative while still reporting it, which is why the new text states measured **state** ("a handoff SATISFIES a loop goal") rather than issuing an order, and why AC4 carries the causal weight. So the `Stop` arm is narrower than the other two: **directive tier only**, only when **`stop_hook_active` is true** (a hook-driven loop is already continuing, so speaking costs no turn it was not already taking — confirmed true from the second `Stop` onward in a real goal loop), and **exempt from the cadence throttle**, because being throttled at the decision point is the exact mistiming being fixed. Delivery markers are now keyed by **channel** (`midturn` / `stop`); without that the mid-turn arm's 70% delivery would consume the only marker and silence the `Stop` arm forever, reproducing the reported bug through its own fix. **A live bug in shipped code fell out of the research:** `emit_payload` sent the TOP-LEVEL `additionalContext` shape on `UserPromptSubmit`, recorded in-repo as verified live 2026-07-28 — probes 14 and 14b measured that shape being **silently ignored** (registered alone, with no sibling hook to confound the merge, the probing model replied `NONE`), so that arm had delivered nothing since #687 and the meter had been running on `PostToolUse` alone. One nested shape now serves all three events, confirmed delivered on each. **The cross-model design review returned 7 findings (3 High) and every one was accepted or answered in writing** (dispositions table in the design doc): its refutation of the "no extra turn" claim was correct and the residual cost is now stated plainly — at most **one** extra turn per session per window, only above the act threshold, in a session already looping, carrying the handoff instruction; its refusal of AC4 as an unproven hypothesis produced **probe 15**, which armed a real `/goal` whose condition said a recorded handoff satisfies it and got exactly that verdict from the evaluator, in its own words, with the loop ending instead of demanding another work turn. Two recommendations were **declined with reasons** (dropping the `Stop` arm entirely; an explicit client-version gate). **A second live report the same day fixed the other end of the same gap:** a session broke at a clean seam, wrote its handoff, then **asked permission** to spawn the successor and idled overnight. Per owner decision 2026-07-29, `skills/pane-handoff/SKILL.md` now says **run it, do not offer it** — the earlier "ALSO offer it unprompted" wording was the actual lever, not the skill's per-step gates, which are verification gates inside one tested command rather than permission prompts. AC5 reworded the check-in tier to "write the resume prompt now" instead of "look for a seam", which named no deliverable; AC3/AC6 name `/rawgentic:pane-handoff` as the route and `clear-prep` as what it wraps, because `clear-prep` alone leaves no successor. New `docs/context-meter.md` sections; hook table updated; design doc + rendered HTML committed. **The Step-11 cross-model diff review then returned 4 findings (2 High) and three became code.** Its sharpest was a security one: the unprompted handoff path is triggered by recognising *injected text*, and injected text is data — anything in a file or tool result can imitate a meter reminder, and the action it triggers spawns a session and clears a guard. The skill now requires one check before an unprompted handoff, verifying this session's own directive marker (`~/.rawgentic/context-meter/$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID.*.directive.emitted`); a user-requested handoff needs no marker. It also caught a real upgrade hazard: the channel-keyed marker changed the namespace, so a session that crossed a tier under <= 3.107.1 would find no marker and re-deliver a tier it had already been given — now a duplicate handoff, not just a duplicate nag. `has_marker` honours the legacy unchannelled marker for the `midturn` channel only, deliberately NOT for `stop`, since that channel never delivered before this version and treating an old record as covering it would leave the new arm born silenced. Third, the real captured `Stop` payload from probe 11 is committed as a fixture with a test pinning it, so the hand-built test payloads are anchored to a live one, plus a parametrized test that only a real JSON `true` opens the gate (a truthy check would admit the string `"false"`). Its first finding — that `stop_hook_active` proves *a* Stop hook continued, not that `/goal` did — was **verified and answered rather than coded around**: `wal-stop` writes only to a notes file and always exits 0, so it cannot continue a turn, and no `/goal`-specific identifier exists in the Stop payload to test against; the gate's honest meaning is "a hook-driven loop is running", and at >=70% context that is when a handoff is wanted regardless of which hook drives it. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6109->6131.

### v3.107.1 (2026-07-29)
- **The #701 validator said "ok" to two classes of block the hook then ignores — including the exact failure #701 exists to prevent (#701 follow-up, epic #626).** The Step-11 cross-model diff review of #701 returned 2 High findings and both held on checking. **(1) Unknown keys were not refused.** The validator only checked the keys it knew about, so `{"windowSzie": 1000000}` returned no errors, the CLI printed `ok`, setup staged it — and the hook, seeing no `windowSize`, kept using the 200,000-token fallback. That is the live failure #701 was filed about, reproduced by the fix for it. Unknown keys are now refused against `SETUP_BLOCK_KEYS`, a module constant naming all five documented keys (the three setup offers **plus** `everyTurns`/`everySeconds`, because refusing the cadence pair would make the validator unusable on a block a user had already tuned by hand), and the refusal names the offending key so a typo is obvious rather than mysterious. **(2) The validator could fail OPEN.** `cmd_validate_config` caught only `ValueError` around `json.loads`, and this module's `__main__` deliberately swallows every exception and exits 0 — a PostToolUse hook must never block a turn — so any other exception was reported to setup as SUCCESS and a block nothing validated got staged. `json.loads` raises `RecursionError` on deeply nested input, which is a `RuntimeError` and not a `ValueError`: **verified live**, 5,000 nested arrays still parse and 10,000 raise. Now caught broadly and returned as exit 2, with a test that drives the real CLI at that measured threshold — 100,000 nesting was tried first and died on Linux's 128 KB per-argument cap instead of exercising the path, which is why the depth is documented rather than round. Also re-pinned `canary.EXPECTED_REGISTRATION_DIGEST` again, and the second re-pin exposed a trap worth recording: both digest strings are the same length, so the file size never changes, and two writes inside one mtime second let Python trust a stale `__pycache__/canary.cpython-312.pyc` — the import reported the OLD pin while the source held the new one, and 20 canary tests failed against a file that was already correct. Delete the `.pyc` when a same-length constant is rewritten twice quickly. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6098->6109.

### v3.107.0 (2026-07-29)
- **The two percentages and the window that decide when a session is told to hand off are now reachable from setup (#701, epic #626).** #687 shipped the `contextMeter` block, its thresholds and a deliberately conservative 200k window fallback, with an env twin per key — and no way to SET any of it short of hand-editing `.rawgentic.json` after reading the config reference. The cost showed up live on 2026-07-29 in this very repo: a 1M-context session was told it was at **88% of an assumed 200,000-token window** and directed to stop and hand off mid-task, when the real figure was about **18%**. The advisory was working exactly as designed against a denominator nobody had been asked to declare, which is why this is a discoverability gap and not a meter defect — `hooks/context_meter.py`'s reading, thresholds and nag behaviour are untouched. New setup **Step 2l** offers `windowSize`, `checkInPercent` and `actPercent`, each with its shipped default and its consequence in one line (check-in = advisory "look for a seam"; act = directive "break now"), and **states the units and the denominator explicitly** because that is the thing whose absence caused the failure: the percentages are of `windowSize`, and `windowSize` is a count of tokens rather than a percentage of anything. **The validator lives in `context_meter.py` beside the constants it enforces**, exposed as a `validate-config` subcommand in the same shape setup already uses for `telemetryAlerts` — and that placement is the whole point rather than a convenience. The issue's AC asked for `checkInPercent` "strictly less than" `actPercent`; the HOOK requires a gap of at least `MIN_TIER_GAP_PCT`, so validating the weaker rule would have accepted pairs `thresholds()` then discards, leaving a user's tuned values **inert** and the meter silently back on 60/70 — indistinguishable from the bug being fixed. A validator anywhere else would have had to copy that constant and the 1..99 range, and a copy is precisely the drift this removes. Two tests prove the two sides agree rather than assuming it: everything the validator accepts survives `thresholds()` unchanged, and everything it refuses is something the hook would have discarded anyway. `bool` is rejected as a percentage on purpose — it is an `int` subclass in Python, so a bare isinstance check would accept `checkInPercent: true` and quietly mean 1%. **Declining leaves the block ABSENT**, deliberately UNLIKE Step 2j's answered-defaults sentinel: a written-out `60`/`70` is indistinguishable from a deliberate choice on the next read, so a later change to the shipped defaults would silently never reach that project. An existing block is preserved verbatim on decline and read-modify-written on accept. The refusal path fails **CLOSED** even though this module's `__main__` deliberately swallows exceptions and exits 0 for the hook paths — a validation gate that passes on error is worse than none, and a test pins that a bare invocation still runs the hook. `docs/config-reference.md` now says setup configures the first three keys, so the doc and the skill stop disagreeing about how the block gets set; `everyTurns` and `everySeconds` stay hand-edited and are named as such. **A FIFTH version-linked surface surfaced doing this and is worth recording:** `canary.EXPECTED_REGISTRATION_DIGEST` is a length-framed sha256 over `hooks/hooks.json` plus every script its command fields reference, so editing any REGISTERED hook script — `context_meter.py` is one — invalidates the pin and fails 20 canary tests with a digest mismatch. The repo manual documents four version surfaces; this is a separate pin with its own trigger (a registered-hook edit, not a version bump), and `launcher_lib.py` never trips it because it is not a registered hook. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6059->6098.

### v3.106.2 (2026-07-29)
- **Teardown could not tell "there was no guard to clear" from "the clear did not land", so it stranded the pane on the documented happy path (#707, epic #626).** Reported from a live lumenquire session on 3.106.1: all four handoff gates passed, the successor came up bound, prompted and guarded — and then retiring the caller's own pane failed with `failed_step: predecessor_goal_clear`, exit 4, and *"Its guard state is AMBIGUOUS"*, when the actual state was **already cleared and safe to close**. The reporter marked their mechanism inferred; it was **confirmed by reading**. v3.106.1's teardown sent `/goal clear` unconditionally and then required a NEW `met:true` sentinel receipt below a pre-send baseline, so with nothing armed no receipt is ever written, the poll exhausts, and three states collapse into two: armed+landed (close), armed+not-landed (leave open, correct), and **nothing-armed (left open, wrong)**. **It is the common path, not an edge case** — the skill tells the operator to run `clear-prep` first and `clear-prep` step 6 clears the guard, so the documented happy path *produces* the precondition that breaks teardown. Fixed by detecting the state explicitly instead of inferring it from a missing receipt: the predecessor's WHOLE transcript is read (the guard was armed long before this baseline, so the tail cannot answer the question), and when nothing is currently armed the clear is skipped, `results.predecessor_goal_clear` records `already_clear`, and the pane closes normally with no `failed_step`. **Liveness is keyed on the NEWEST `goal_status` row, deliberately NOT on the caller's `--predecessor-goal-condition`, and that is the hazard in this fix rather than a detail:** a caller whose supplied condition has since been superseded would otherwise read as "already clear" while a replacement guard is live, and the pane would be closed over it — the same trap #665 Step-11 pass-3 documented for the confirm side, arriving from the other direction. The transcript is therefore the single authority, the supplied condition is demoted to an assertion whose disagreement is recorded as a note, and the confirm binds to the live condition too (binding to a stale string would wait for a receipt nothing will ever write). State 2 is untouched and pinned: a genuinely unconfirmed clear still leaves the pane open and still returns non-zero, and an unreadable predecessor transcript is explicitly **not** evidence that nothing is armed. One older test was rewritten rather than patched — it put ONLY a foreign `met:true` row in the transcript, which under the new logic correctly means "nothing armed", so it had been asserting a failure that no longer makes sense; the intent survives as a coherent history where a foreign receipt sits behind our own live guard. Two smaller reported defects fixed in the same pass, both mine: the skill told operators to read their goal condition "with the `read-goal-condition` subcommand" without naming the flag, and the natural reading is the `--transcript-dir` + `--session-id` pair `ad-hoc-handoff` itself takes — it is `--transcript <file>`, and the exact command is now shown; and Step 2's template still printed `--project-root <project path>` against `--cwd <workspace root>`, precisely the combination Step 1 says `resolve_cwd` refuses as an escape. The delivery sequence and its four gates are untouched — all four passed in the report, so nothing there was implicated. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6051->6059.

### v3.106.1 (2026-07-29)
- **The first real handoff left a live pane guarded and looping, and three layers disagreed about whether it should (#700, epic #626).** Found by USING it, on thewanderinginn epic #7 child #5: the handoff itself succeeded — all four gates passed, successor `w1:pDJ` — and then the predecessor pane sat open with its `/goal` still armed and **looped its Stop hook four times** before the owner intervened. That is the #694 pathology reached through a different door, and three separate defects produced it. **(1) Contradictory defaults:** `perform_handoff` defaulted `teardown=True` while the `ad-hoc-handoff` CLI defaulted it False, so a library caller and a CLI caller got opposite behaviour for one operation. Removed rather than reconciled — `teardown` now has **no library default at all**, so every caller states its intent; that also un-vacuums the "teardown is blocked until every check passes" tests, which had been asserting a blocked teardown they never asked for. **(2) Teardown closed a pane whose guard was still armed:** `build_teardown_argv` is close-only, while the successor-owned `retire-predecessor` path (#665) has always done clear → confirm → close. Usually masked because the session dies with the pane — but when the close fails or is skipped, the owner is left with a session that **can never stop**, which is exactly what happened. Ad-hoc teardown now sends `/goal clear`, **confirms it** from the predecessor's own transcript below a pre-send baseline, and only then closes; an unconfirmed clear **leaves the pane open** on purpose, because an ambiguous guard is recoverable and a wrongly-closed pane is not. The receipt is bound to the condition when the caller supplies it (`--predecessor-goal-condition`), because #665 Step-11 pass-3 already proved that accepting any `met:true` row lets a row belonging to a DIFFERENT guard confirm our clear and close a live pane. **The campaign path keeps its previous observable behaviour** — close-only when no predecessor session id is known, since the campaign launcher does not know one and cannot confirm a clear — with the gap RECORDED on the step record rather than silently assumed harmless; giving an unattended path new refusal semantics on the strength of a unit test is not a trade worth making from here. **(3) Teardown OFF said nothing at all:** the default path left the guard armed and never mentioned it, so the surprise was silent. It now returns `predecessor_guard` naming the condition and the required `/goal clear`, printed on stderr as well as in the JSON, and the skill is told to relay it verbatim. **The skill also fought its own triggers**, which is why the owner met the surprising branch: it fires on "pass off", "pass everything over", "clear the context into a new session" — phrasings that mean *retire this one* — and then instructed the model to add teardown "only when the user has actually asked". Rather than have the model classify phrasings, it now **asks once** which the user wants before running. And its documented invocation was simply **wrong**: it said `--cwd` is the workspace root and `--project-root` the project directory, a combination `resolve_cwd` REFUSES as an escape (exit 2), so the first real attempt bounced — both are the workspace root, which is also where `.rawgentic_workspace.json` lives and therefore the only cwd from which the successor's bind can work. **The same PR also carries the WF2 Step-11 diff review #704 never got, run retrospectively.** #704 ran a real design gate but shipped without a pre-PR diff review, on a change that touches `perform_handoff` and therefore the campaign handoff path too. Running WF5 over the merged diff returned **3 findings (2 High, 1 Medium)**, and the Medium was the only genuinely new one: **nothing bound the anchor pane to the calling session.** Verified by reading rather than accepted on assertion — `perform_handoff` validates the anchor's *shape* (`validate_pane_id`) and nothing else, so a stale or mistyped `$HERDR_PANE_ID` passed with `--teardown-predecessor` would split from, and then **close, a stranger's pane**. `retire_predecessor` has held the right rule since #665 (`hooks/launcher_lib.py:2108` — a destructive target must prove it hosts the session claiming authority over it) and it was simply absent on the new path. Fixed by mirroring it: with teardown requested the subcommand now requires `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (the same discipline as `_own_session_id(require_env=True)`, because "no environment at all" is not a state a real Claude session is in) and refuses unless `herdr pane get <anchor>` proves that pane hosts it, failing **closed** on an unreadable probe. Scoped to the destructive request deliberately: without teardown a wrong anchor is a recoverable mis-split, and demanding a live herdr probe for the harmless case would refuse every environment that has no herdr — which is every CI run. One High was **accepted and strengthened rather than closed**: the `PROMPT_MARKER_MIN_LEN` floor admits an 8-character *phrase*, which is ordinary prose, so a marker must now be a single token with no whitespace — the bind turn's own transcript output is prose and tool JSON and will not contain a `[handoff-700]`-shaped token. Still a heuristic, and the code and the skill both say so; real structural uniqueness would mean mutating the caller's prompt to inject a nonce, considered and rejected as the worse trade. The other High is **recorded as an unfixed residual risk**, which is the honest answer rather than a worse mechanism: a stale paste affordance plus a *reworded* permission dialog could still let a nudge Enter through, and there is nothing better available — the affordance is the only durable evidence about the input box, `agent_status` was measured useless for exactly this (#694), and a structured transcript match was already falsified (`hooks/launcher_lib.py:573-582`). Its reachable path is narrow (the nudge runs only while `prompt_landed` is false, so the affordance in view is normally the unsubmitted prompt's own) and bounded at four Enters into a session we just created — **it is the claim in #700 most likely to be wrong.** One earlier test was **deleted rather than duplicated**: the new precondition means `teardown=True` cannot reach `perform_handoff` without ownership setup, so `test_teardown_is_opt_in` became a strictly weaker copy of `test_teardown_proceeds_when_the_pane_is_provably_ours`, and a pointer replaces it. Dispositions for all three findings are appended to the committed design doc. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6036->6041. Teardown default reversed to ON by owner decision 2026-07-29, superseding #700 AC4. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 6036->6051.

### v3.106.0 (2026-07-29)
- **A callable pane handoff, and the unsubmitted Enter that would have made a helper worse than doing it by hand (#700, epic #626).** #696 shipped **no** helper on purpose and recorded its revisit trigger verbatim — *"Revisit if ad-hoc pane handoffs become routine enough that hand-rolling the poll is a recurring source of error rather than a one-off"* — so this is the documented next step, not a reversal. The trigger was met twice over: the sequence was hand-driven twice in one day, and a mining pass over 94 transcripts from 36 hours found **seven reproaches** ("Why didn't you finish the handoff?", "Why aren't you opening the new pane and passing the session over?") — each one a session that should have fired a skill that **did not exist anywhere**. New `/rawgentic:pane-handoff` plus a thin `launcher_lib ad-hoc-handoff` subcommand: the project, prompt, goal condition and anchor pane arrive **directly**, with no driver-state file and no `--launcher-armed` (which is exactly why `_cmd_handoff` could not serve this — it opens a state file as its first act and gates on `fresh_session_available`), and `perform_handoff` runs unchanged, so the gate order, the ladder and #694's refusal of a self-binding prompt are inherited rather than re-implemented. Teardown shipped **opt-in and OFF** (AC4) and was reversed by owner decision the same day — see v3.106.1 below; the reasoning was sound and the first real handoff refuted it. **The issue's own comment then changed the shape, and the fix deliberately lands in `perform_handoff` rather than only in the new path.** Driving the sequence by hand exposed a third state distinct from both success and transport failure: `project_switched` proves the bind's registry row **landed**, not that its **turn ended** — the same reasoning error #694 corrected for `goal_status met:false` and left standing on its sibling — so the prompt's Enter was eaten by the still-running bind turn and sat unsubmitted as `[Pasted text #1 +9 lines]` while a 120 s poll expired. A bare Enter recovered it with **exactly one** marker occurrence and no double submission. A helper that merely wrapped the sends would have **inherited** that bug and been less reliable than a human who notices the buffer sitting there, so the recovery is now inside send 2 — which is neither a send-order nor a gate change (the order and `prompt_landed` are untouched; a nudge can only let a gate pass where the buffer was intact all along), a reading the issue explicitly invited and one that is **flagged rather than assumed** since the scope line says "unchanged". It also cannot live anywhere else: the subcommand does not send, so fixing it in the skill would mean re-implementing the send, which is the very thing AC2 forbids. **One nudge would not have been enough, and the arithmetic says so:** the row appeared at ~24 s into a turn observed cooking for ~50 s, and `prompt_landed`'s budget is `GOAL_POLL_ATTEMPTS x GOAL_POLL_DELAY_S` ~ 18 s, so a lone Enter at ~42 s lands **inside the same turn**; it is `PROMPT_NUDGE_ROUNDS` (4) rounds of {bare Enter, re-poll}, ~90 s worst case, and zero cost on a handoff that submits normally. Never a re-paste (double submission) and never a truncation (silent corruption) — #696's rule, which this path must not quietly break. **The cross-model design review returned 8 findings and its sharpest one rewrote the recovery.** Bounding the nudge count is **not** a bound on privilege: a bare Enter accepts whatever is on screen, so each nudge is now gated on a fail-safe check — `herdr pane read --source visible` must show a paste affordance (`[Pasted text` / `paste again to expand`) and must **not** show a permission-dialog signature, the dialog check running first because a scrollback paste marker can sit above a live dialog. A failed read, an unrecognised screen and a missing affordance all resolve to **do not nudge**, i.e. byte-identically to the pre-#700 behaviour, and the honest limits are recorded rather than implied: the affordance does **not** prove the buffer is unsubmitted (§7.1.2 says it shows on successful submissions too — the claim is only "an input box, not a dialog"), and the dialog denylist is user-visible strings that a future reword would cost us. `agent_status` was deliberately **not** used for this, per #694's measurement that it is not a synchronisation signal. **Half of one High finding was refuted by reading the code and is recorded as refuted:** it claimed the marker check has no pre-send baseline and no binding to the new successor's transcript, but `transcript_baseline` is captured before SEND 1 and `_prompt_landed` scans only the tail below it on a path built from the session id herdr returned. Its **uniqueness** half held — the contract is membership, not distinctiveness, so `--prompt-marker` is now **required** (an optional marker means the gate is silently skipped, and a skipped check is not a gate) and must be single-line, control-character-free (a transcript stores a newline **escaped**, so a multiline marker could never match) and at least `PROMPT_MARKER_MIN_LEN` characters, a floor documented as the heuristic it is. Four more findings were accepted as-is: `--project-path` is **required** (the caller reads it off its own registry row, so optionality bought nothing), a failed nudge send is its own `send_resume_nudge` failure rather than reported as poll exhaustion, the skill reaches the CLI through `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` (a bare `hooks/...` path only resolves when the session is bound to *this* repo), and the context-pressure trigger is reworded to what the skill can actually do — react to the context-meter hook's own reminder, whose threshold and window belong to `contextMeter` (#687, configurability #701) — because the draft claimed a capability it does not have. **The AC2 guard got simpler and stronger from the review too:** it forbids the terminal primitives **anywhere** in the skill file, prose included, instead of only inside code fences — the first draft's own stated limit was that unbackticked prose could still instruct a hand-rolled send — so the skill points at the runbook rather than restating it, which deletes both the parser and the hole. The description carries the **mined** vocabulary rather than guesses, including the dictation variants that a description keyed on "handoff" would miss entirely (`pass off` / `passoff` dominates real requests, and "Herder" for herdr and "pain" for pane are how the fastest asks arrive), with 6 evals covering each phrasing cluster plus the already-running-pane refusal. `clear-prep` is **consumed, not invoked** — it lives outside this repository, so the coupling stays a pointer. Registered across all surfaces via the checker (whitelist between `new-project`/`peer-consult`, codex mirror symlink, counts 21->22, workspace management 9->10, codex `longDescription`, and two stragglers the checker named that hand inspection missed: `.rawgentic.json`'s own description and a hand-pinned assert). §7.1.2 rewritten — it claimed no helper is shipped, and now splits the two cases explicitly, because the by-hand recipe remains correct for a pane **already running** a session (not a herdr-registered agent, so no readiness primitive, so none of the gates apply) while the skill owns the fresh-successor launch. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 5995->6036.

### v3.105.3 (2026-07-29)
- **A child shipped outside the epic driver never wrote its terminal status back, so a resume could re-do a merged child (#695, epic #626).** Observed live on epic #684: `claude_docs/.driver-state/epic-684-watcher-fires.json` still read `{"number": 687, "status": "queued"}` after #687 was **merged** (PR #691) and closed, and the fresh-session resume — obeying its own correct rule, *"derive position from durable state, never in-context memory"* — duly announced #687 as the next ready child. **The good rule produced the bad outcome, because the state it trusted was wrong.** It is structural, not an operator slip: WF2 Step 14 merges and Step 16 writes the run-record, and **neither touches the driver-state file**; the epic driver writes it only when IT sequences children, so any run invoked as `/rawgentic:implement-feature <n>` directly — which is exactly what an epic auto-run does after a handoff — bypasses the writer entirely, silently, on every non-driver child. AC1 was a design decision ("pick one owner and state it"), so it went through a real design gate, and that gate is most of this entry's value. The in-repo reviewer agent **refused** (`{"refused": "architecture_self_check", "reason": "defaultArchitecture is not legacy (#474)"}`) because this project routes review seats through the executor — a dead agent, not a passed gate, and it was not treated as one — so WF5 ran a different-model review of the design instead. It returned **7 findings, 4 High, and all seven held on checking**.
