Two refuted the first decision outright. **The owner is therefore the locked `record-child-outcome` command, not a step:** v1 named WF2 Step 16, and the review pointed out Step 16 is **not atomic with the merge**, so a crash between merging and reaching it reproduces the defect faithfully — the command now runs at Step 14 immediately after the merge is confirmed AND at Step 16 as idempotent reconciliation, which is what keeps "one owner" true across two call sites (one implementation, one lock, one transition table). **AC2 was shipping DEAD:** v1 added an *optional* probe to `next_ready_issue` and boasted that every existing caller stayed untouched, but `fresh_session_handoff` (`hooks/driver_lib.py:446`) is the sole production caller, so an unwired option changed nothing — the probe is now threaded through it and built by `launcher_lib handoff` from a repo **derived from the project's own `origin` remote** rather than a flag someone must remember. **The probe was also unbuildable as specified:** the installed `gh issue view --json` exposes neither `stateReason` nor `closedByPullRequestsReferences`, so the query was verified live via `gh api graphql` against the actual regression (#687 → `CLOSED` / `COMPLETED` / closed by PR #691 with `merged: true`) and cited as platform-feasibility evidence (#226). It is a **tri-state** — `confirmed_merged` / `confirmed_abandoned` / `confirmed_open` / `unknown` — and a confirmed verdict becomes an **effective-status overlay applied before BOTH dependency evaluation and candidate selection**, because filtering only the candidate (v1, and an earlier draft of the test file asserted it) would leave a child whose prerequisite really merged blocked forever, so an already-stale campaign reports "no ready child" while the prerequisite sits merged on GitHub. The overlay reaches the **complete** verdict too, or a fully-shipped stale campaign would keep its epic open with nothing runnable. `unknown` deliberately does **not** veto, and that is tested: the probe is corroboration and the file is primary once the write-back keeps it correct, so turning a GitHub outage into a total campaign stall is the worse failure — a duplicate PR is visible, a stall is silent. Status validation is now a **transition table** rather than a vocabulary check: `merged` and `abandoned` are non-regressible (membership in `VALID_STATUSES` proves a legal *word*, not a legal *transition*, and walking a merged child back to `queued` is precisely how a shipped issue gets re-run), while `deferred` stays re-queueable. Campaign files are **discovered** rather than configured — a single-session run does not know its campaign, so with `--driver-state` omitted the command scans `claude_docs/.driver-state/*.json` and updates **every** queue naming the issue in sorted order, since writing only the first match leaves the others stale. Fail-open is scoped precisely and is **never silent**: no campaign or no state directory → exit 0, write nothing, reason on **both** streams (stdout alone is lost to any wrapper that captures one, and then a real miss looks like a deliberate no-op), while an off-vocabulary status, a terminal regression or a corrupt state file exits non-zero with the file untouched. The AC2 probe is **opt-OUT** (`RAWGENTIC_ISSUE_STATE_PROBE=0`) with the default pinned ON by its own guard, because corroboration you must switch on is corroboration nobody switches on — the opt-out exists only because several of this repo's own handoff fixtures reuse issue numbers that really are merged here, which turned synthetic queues into genuinely "complete" campaigns and made two CLI tests depend on live GitHub state. `docs/multi-issue-driver.md` now names the owner, replacing an answer that was *"the driver, except when it isn't"*. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 5896->5995.

### v3.105.2 (2026-07-29)
- **`pane run` is a shell-command runner, `paste again to expand` is not an error, and `herdr wait` was never a command (#696, epic #626).** Docs plus one drift guard; **no runtime code changed**, deliberately — `launcher_lib.build_send_text_argv` already emits `send-text` and a **separate** `send-keys Enter` and already cites #654 for why, so the defect was guidance, not behaviour. Handing work to a sibling pane by hand, a ~1,400-character prompt sent with `herdr pane run` **did not submit**; the pane showed `paste again to expand` and that string was read as evidence the content had been mangled. **The reporter's own headline finding was the misdiagnosis, not the failure.** `paste again to expand` and `[Pasted text +N lines]` are Claude Code's collapsed-display affordances and appear on **successful** submissions — confirmed live on 2026-07-29, where a ~2,500-character multi-line prompt delivered by the two-call pattern submitted, ran, and showed `paste again to expand` in a `pane read` while the receiving agent was already editing files. So the runbook now states that the buffer is **INTACT and must be submitted as-is**, never retried and never truncated, and names the cost of each wrong response rather than just forbidding it: a retry risks **double-submission**, a truncation **silently corrupts the handoff**, which is worse because nothing downstream can tell. The sharper diagnosis is that `pane run` is the **wrong tool** rather than the right tool used badly — its own help reads *"Run a command in a pane"* — so the rule is "never for prompt text, at any length" rather than a byte threshold: the ~1,400-char failure and the ~2,500-char success differ in **method**, not only length, and the mechanism by which `pane run`'s Enter is lost was **not traced** and is recorded as unconfirmed rather than asserted. Submission is verified **from the transcript**, never from `agent_status`, which #694 independently measured as useless for this — `idle` immediately after a prompt was submitted, `done` mid-output, `working` at an empty input line. Two CLI facts are corrected: the top-level `herdr wait` does not exist in 0.7.5, and `herdr agent wait` **keys on an agent NAME, not a pane id** (a pane id for a shell-launched `claude` returns `agent_not_found`), which together mean an ad-hoc sibling pane has **no readiness primitive at all** and must be verified by polling its artifact. The runbook cited **#659** in three places as tracking the missing `wait`; #659 is herdr **version-drift detection**, so nothing actually tracked it — all three are re-pointed, and the old number is named where it is corrected so the wrong attribution cannot quietly return. New `tests/test_herdr_runbook_doc.py` (12 guards) pins all of it, and its `herdr wait` guard is **scoped to instructional surfaces** (`docs/runbooks/`, `skills/`) rather than the whole corpus: ~12 `docs/planning/` files and a README changelog entry legitimately quote the broken command while recording that it does not exist, so a corpus-wide regex would fail on contact — workspace mistake #11. The guard fails on an invocation inside a fenced block or on a bare command line, allows a prose mention only alongside its own negation, and was **verified to bite** by injecting a fenced `herdr wait` and watching it fail. A companion guard refuses to let it be satisfied by deleting the correction outright. **No helper was shipped, on purpose:** `build_send_text_argv` + `_poll_for` + `transcript_has_marker` already compose into a send-and-confirm wrapper and `perform_handoff` IS that composition, so the recipe is documented rather than duplicated, with the revisit trigger stated. **The user-level half cannot ship from here and the docs say so:** `~/.claude/skills/herdr/SKILL.md` documented five invocations of the non-existent command, is fixed locally, and lives outside any git repository, so it is neither committed nor CI-tested here. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 5884->5896.

### v3.105.1 (2026-07-29)
- **Three sends, each gated on an artifact the successor itself wrote, and `/goal` last (#694).** The owner reported a fresh successor sitting with "the initial prompt stuck in queue", and #694 named the cause as send ORDER — `/goal` before the prompt — asking for switch -> prompt -> goal. **Reproduction refuted the stated mechanism, and a first fix built on the refutation was then falsified by measurement before it shipped.** Both dead ends are recorded here because the conclusion survived while both explanations of it died. Four live runs against a real herdr 0.7.5 server: goal-then-prompt back-to-back ran NOTHING (no transcript file at all, 0 registry rows); the issue's own prescribed order, back-to-back, **failed identically** with `/goal` left unsubmitted; goal alone with a gap ran fine (1 turn) and the prompt still did not land; a lone `/rawgentic:switch` into a settled pane ran correctly. Ordering never discriminated — what did was whether each send was **gated on evidence** rather than fired back-to-back. The first fix concluded "readiness" and gated the resume-prompt paste on `agent_status == "idle"`; two measurements killed it. **X1:** after arming a *real unmet* goal the pane reported `working` on consecutive reads while the `goal_status met:false` row was **already present**, so an idle gate placed after `goal_armed` refuses **every** real handoff — strictly worse than the bug. **X2:** a `/goal` pasted into a session *actively mid-turn* produced its row **while that turn was still running**, so the guard needs no idle window, which is precisely what lets it go last. `agent_status` is not a synchronisation signal at all — `idle` immediately after a prompt was submitted, `done` mid-output, `working` at an empty input line — so `parse_pane_agent_status` survives for **diagnostics only** and no control flow may branch on it. The 22 tests that went green on the falsified gate proved nothing, because the fake runner returned a canned `agent_status: idle`: the same fixture unrealism the module docstring already blames for letting an ordering-free prompt ship. What ships instead is the owner-specified sequence, every gate a **durable artifact**: `/rawgentic:switch <project>` as SEND 1 of its own, gated on the session-registry row; then the resume prompt, gated on its marker reaching the transcript; then `/goal` LAST, gated on the `goal_status met:false` row; then the predecessor is retired. **The bind therefore LEAVES the resume prompt, reversing #682's `resume_prompt_binds_first` precondition and both `driver_lib` prompt builders** — which is the change #682 itself named correct and deferred, verbatim: *"the launcher should send the bind as its own verified turn — it already sends `/goal` that way — which removes the dependence on prose ordering entirely."* #682 checked the bind as a prompt **prefix**, and its own validator docstring is honest that a position check is a *proxy* for "first" rather than a proof; a separate verified send makes the ordering **structural**, so the proxy has nothing left to do. `perform_handoff` now refuses a resume prompt that carries a bind at all, since send 1 already did it and a second one makes the successor run the switch skill twice. A new `include_bind` flag on both builders defaults to **True**, which is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: the interactive hand-back and the `claude -p` fallback launch each deliver exactly ONE prompt and have no second send to put a bind in, so a default of False would silently strand every single-prompt path at an unbound session — one shared `_lead_with_bind` helper decides it for both builders, because a flag copied into each is how the two prompts drifted before. Both verification ladders were reordered to match the send order (`spawned -> project_switched -> goal_armed`, and the mid-child six to `spawned -> project_switched -> prompt_landed -> goal_armed -> position_rebuilt -> state_claimed`), which matters because `evaluate_verifications` stops at the FIRST failure and a ladder listing rungs out of send order reports the wrong step as the thing that broke; a reordered ladder stays refused outright, so the old order cannot be reintroduced through `steps=`. The old ordering's objection is **answered rather than discarded** — the predecessor is not retired until the last rung passes, so "work begins unguarded" never coincides with "the predecessor is already gone", which was the actual harm. **The one open cross-model review finding is fixed:** `_poll_for` bounded attempt count but not wall clock, and each attempt does I/O with no ceiling of its own, so the polls now carry a wall-clock deadline of `POLL_WALL_CLOCK_SLACK` (2x) their nominal `attempts x delay` budget with the first attempt always running — and the same bound was applied to the `agent_start` retry, where the arithmetic actually bit (15 attempts against the runner's 180 s timeout is a 45-minute ceiling on a condition that resolves itself in about a second). **#694's AC4 answered, and the answer is no:** #682's three `project_switched` observations are NOT explained by this, and are contradicted by the H7 live handover, which armed the goal FIRST and landed its prompt with the bind 25.5 s later (`docs/planning/2026-07-28-667-uat-plan/harness/evidence/682-h7-live-handover-2026-07-28.md`); #682's own confound — a synthetic campaign pointing at nonexistent issue 99998 consuming the 120 s budget — remains the best-supported explanation, and its successor transcript shows prompt-DERIVED work, which only exists if the prompt landed. A permission-blocked successor swallows a pasted prompt, but that case was **induced by the test setup** (`--permission-mode default`); the launcher spawns plain `claude` and `_ALLOWED_CLAUDE_ARGS` refuses `--permission-mode` as authority-bearing, so a non-blocking permission mode is a **precondition** of unattended handoff that this code cannot assert — it fails loudly on a stalled bind rather than building an auto-accepter. The `clear-prep` half of AC2 is **not shipped and cannot be**: that skill lives at `~/.claude/skills/clear-prep/SKILL.md`, outside any git repository, so it is neither committed nor CI-tested here. `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` §7.1/§7.1.1/§7.4 rewritten to the shipped design. No workflow-spine change -> no diagram REV. Suite 5840->5884.

### v3.105.0 (2026-07-28)
- **A hook that cannot forget: the context-pressure trigger, and the four review passes that reshaped it (#687, epic #684).** #654 measured how to read a session's context usage and PR #675 committed the findings, but implementation was out of that spike's scope — so every "if context is running low, checkpoint and resume" line in WF2 was model judgment with no signal behind it, and the owner reported it "works sometimes, but not reliable at all." New `hooks/context_meter.py` (pure core + thin CLI) reads the session's own transcript, expresses usage as a **fraction of the context window**, and injects an advisory (default 60%) then a directive (default 70%) nag, **once each per session per effective window**, tunable via a new `contextMeter` config block with an env twin per key. **The load-bearing design choice is that it registers on BOTH `UserPromptSubmit` and `PostToolUse`**: the first draft used `UserPromptSubmit` alone, and the cross-model peer consult's third risk pointed at why that ships a dead feature — that event fires once per *user prompt*, so in a long autonomous run (one prompt, then hours of work) the meter would evaluate once on an empty context and never again, silently dead in exactly the runs that need it most. The 5-turn arm rides the prompt event; the 5-minute arm rides tool calls, and the honest limit is documented rather than implied — hooks fire on events, not on a timer, so the minute arm is *evaluated at the next event*. **Facts were established live instead of assumed, with the harness committed** under `docs/planning/2026-07-28-687-probes/` after the Step-4 verifier rightly refused a citation whose method lived in an unauditable `/tmp` dir: `PostToolUse` does deliver `additionalContext` but **only nested under `hookSpecificOutput`**, not the top-level form `wal-context` uses on `UserPromptSubmit` (spiked with a real `claude -p` session; the canary came back verbatim) — the adversarial reviewer had correctly refused the `UserPromptSubmit` call site as proof for a different event under #226's exact-surface rule; `transcript_path` is present on both events with basename exactly `<session_id>.jsonl`, which is what makes the hardening rule satisfiable; a session's **model name does not reveal its window size** (a 1M session records `claude-opus-5` with no `[1m]` marker), so the window is declared with a conservative 200k default that self-corrects by escalation once a session provably exceeds it; and an **interrupted turn writes an all-zero usage row**, so the reader takes the last NON-ZERO row — a last-row reader reports 0% on a nearly-full session. **The threshold stopped being a guess: both design reviewers flagged it as unevidenced, so it was measured.** A committed scan over 266 transcripts puts the highest in-context reading ever observed at **999,803 tokens = 100.0% of a 1M window**, so auto-compaction did not fire below ~99% in any sampled 1M session and a 70% directive has ~30 points of margin — **this answers the 1M half of #654's Q4**. The 200k window is stated as **unmeasured, because it is unmeasurable here** (zero such sessions in the corpus), so that finding is recorded as mitigated-not-resolved rather than argued away. **Three correctness defects were caught before merge and none by inspection alone:** window escalation would have let a premature 200k-era warning suppress the real one at 600k (emissions are now keyed by effective window, with directive implying advisory); the reservation marker created a *permanent* lost-emission window if the state write then failed, so the marker is now the single record, created immediately before stdout and **released** on a failed delivery; and `os.environ` is `os._Environ`, **not a `dict`**, so an `isinstance(env, dict)` guard silently disabled the entire env-override layer while every dict-based unit test still passed — only the subprocess tests saw it. The seam rule reports a **candidate**, never "safe": a step-state pointer transition proves the recorded step changed, not that the tree is committed or that no review wave is outstanding, so the advisory asks the session to confirm what only it can, and a session with no pointer at all fires immediately because it has no phase to interrupt (found by its own test — folding that case into "wait" would have made the advisory permanently silent in ordinary sessions). AC4's split is **two independent values**, `headless` and `fresh_handoff_capable`, because a headless session with no armed launcher would otherwise be pointed at a command whose guard legitimately refuses it. **AC8's claim was shrunk to the truth:** `clear-prep` does not write a task snapshot (checked, after the reviewer disputed it), so the home is its existing `next actions, in order` list and transfer is **re-derivation via `/tasklist`** — identity-preserving transfer is explicitly DEFERRED with both missing call sites named. Reads the transcript backward in bounded 64 KiB blocks under a 4 MiB cap (the largest transcript here is 83 MB and this hook rides every tool call); fail-OPEN but never invisible, with a once-per-session stderr diagnostic on each of the three self-disabling outcomes. `phase_executor`'s `_add_record` now treats a repeated rel path as **idempotent** rather than fatal, since one script may legitimately be registered on several events and the digest is over the *set* of (path, content) records. New `docs/context-meter.md`; `contextMeter` documented in `docs/config-reference.md` and the config template. **Then Step 11 returned BLOCK from both reviewers, with 21 findings — 1 Critical, 6 High — and every one of them was real.** The Critical was a prompt-injection channel: the step-state pointer's `workflow`/`step` were interpolated VERBATIM into `additionalContext`, and that pointer is a project-controlled file, so a hostile repo could put instruction text where the model reads it as trustworthy (now allowlisted through `_echo_safe`). The worst High was that the feature was dead in its own main case: the `PostToolUse` matcher was `Bash|Edit|Write|NotebookEdit|Task`, omitting `Read`/`Grep`/`Glob`, so a long autonomous run spent reading fired no invocation at all — and the minute arm was the whole justification for riding that event (wildcard matcher now, with a test that fails on any narrowing). The rest: `canary._add_record`'s duplicate branch returned before its symlink/containment checks (my own dedup fix had introduced that); registry-derived `project`/`project_path` reached a filename and a read with no validation or containment; state containment was checked after `mkdir`+`chmod` and only on the final component, so a symlinked `~/.rawgentic` redirected every write; and stdin, the whole registry, config, the pointer and non-regular files were all read unbounded on a hook that runs per tool call under a 5 s timeout. `O_NONBLOCK` was found by the TEST, not the review — the FIFO case hung the suite for five minutes because opening a FIFO blocks AT `open()`, before any check can run. Mediums: window overflow past the largest known tier used the observed count as the window, so the marker key changed every reading and re-delivered the directive forever; the seam search now drops when pressure falls back; the sweep no longer deletes a live session's markers; `reserve` unlinks on post-create failure; tier comparison is integer-exact (`116000/200000*100` computes as `57.99999999999999`); a throttled `PostToolUse` writes nothing. And the sharpest finding was about one of my own tests — the release test redirected stdout to `/dev/null`, where printing succeeds, so deleting the production `release()` call left it green. Two follow-ups are recorded rather than hidden: a single pure `evaluate()`, and a fully fd-based transcript read. A policy contradiction was also resolved — `CLAUDE.md` called `capabilities_lib derive` the "ONLY sanctioned way" to read `.rawgentic.json` while seven hooks read it directly, so the manual now states the real rule and its narrow exception. **A verification pass over the fixes then found three more, all real:** `load_state` had been left out of the bounded-read conversion (a FIFO at the state path hung every invocation), `cmd_read` still called the buggy float tier path, and the `_echo_safe` allowlist was insufficient because `IGNORE-PRIOR-INSTRUCTIONS` satisfies any identifier pattern — so the pointer-echo channel is now **removed** rather than narrowed. A fourth was mine to own: the fixed tail-slice registry read traded one bug for its mirror image, making a live session look unbound when enough was appended after its row, so it is now a bounded backward scan with a visible diagnostic when the row is not found. Two limits are documented rather than papered over: a residual TOCTOU window on the state directory (a fully descriptor-relative write is a filed follow-up) and the absence of a single pure `evaluate()`. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5703→5840.

### v3.104.2 (2026-07-28)
- **The handoff's resume prompt must bind FIRST, and a review pass caught that the first version of this fix would not have worked at all (#682, epic #684).** `perform_handoff`'s `project_switched` check allows `SWITCH_POLL_ATTEMPTS (40) × SWITCH_POLL_DELAY_S (3.0)` = 120 s for a fresh successor to bind and append to the session registry, then declares `failed_step: project_switched` and **closes the successor's pane** — observed live three times in the epic #667 UAT, with the successor's own transcript showing it was executing the bind when its pane closed. The observation is CONFOUNDED and the issue says so (a synthetic issue 99998 sent the successor investigating a nonexistent issue's provenance); what is not confounded is that the 120 s budget assumes the bind comes first and **nothing enforced the ordering**, while a prudent successor verifies first. The two resume-prompt builders disagreed and only one was right: `_build_mid_child_resume_prompt` (#665) already led with the bind and its reason, while `_build_resume_prompt` (#569) buried it in a comma list that reads as parallel tasks. **Then the Step-4 review found the real defect: both builders emitted a BARE `/rawgentic:switch`, which does not bind anything** — the switch skill enters **list mode** and asks a human which project to use (`skills/switch/SKILL.md` Steps 1-2), so an unattended successor sits at a question, never appends the registry row, and loses its pane when the budget exhausts. Both builders now emit `/rawgentic:switch <project>` through a shared `_bind_clause`, and when no project is known the clause carries no valid command so the handoff is REFUSED rather than a doomed successor spawned. New pure `resume_prompt_binds_first(prompt, project=None)`, called fail-closed from `perform_handoff` before the split so a refusal never leaves a pane behind: with a known project it is an **exact literal match**, fully mechanical; without one it can only check that the command has some argument, a limit stated in the docstring and pinned by a test because it cannot tell a project name from the next English word; and the position check is an openly labelled **proxy** for "first", not a proof. That honesty was earned — the validator's first version was a positional prose classifier, and the same review refuted it by producing both a false positive and a false negative in one pass. The guard also earned its cost immediately: the `RESUME_PROMPT` fixture had **no** `/rawgentic:switch` directive at all, so 55 `perform_handoff` tests were passing against a prompt that could never have made `project_switched` succeed — exactly the unrealism that let an ordering-free prompt ship. **Two claims withdrawn rather than defended:** that 120 s is "generous" (the switch skill does a workspace read, a full workspace REWRITE, an env lookup and a second shell call before appending, none of it timed — now `verification_deferred` against this run's own live handover), and that only the prompt wording changed. **The better design is on the record, not lost:** the launcher should send the bind as its own verified turn — it already sends `/goal` that way — which removes the dependence on prose ordering entirely; deferred because it reorders a ladder whose comment says the order is CAUSAL and whose six steps each carry an ownership invariant earned in #665's review. Also ships the epic #667 **UAT plan rework** (owner instruction): a new tier 7 for #687's context-pressure trigger, and **six checks moved from human-only to agent** after the owner authorised the handoff and the plugin reinstall — six owner tasks become four owner judgements, with the four owner ids deliberately left unregistered in the harness so an agent cannot record them. **A second review round then found the guard itself unsound and the child-boundary path broken by it.** `perform_handoff` requiring a bind it could not verify meant the documented child-boundary handoff would have refused EVERY time: no caller supplied a project, the CLI had no `--project`, and `state["project"]` is absent from the schema and from every real state file — so `--project` is now required on that subcommand and threaded through, and the disposition returns `no_project` **before** `open_handoff` persists anything, because refusing after it bumped `generation` stranded a handoff every retry refused identically. The validator is now a **prefix** check with an **exact token** compare and a **required** project: substring matching had accepted `rawgentic-next` for `rawgentic` (binding the successor to the wrong project), an optional project degraded it to "has any argument" (accepting the English word after a bare directive), and every position-based proxy still admitted "read this first, then bind" — the ordering the whole issue is about. `valid_project_name` now gates the name before it is interpolated into pane-bound prompt text, and the design note's "no new external input" claim is corrected: that interpolation IS a new data flow across a trust boundary. The UAT rework was corrected too — the harness now genuinely **refuses a PASS without a readable, non-empty evidence file** (the plan claimed that and it was false), W8a is reworded to bridge ACCEPTANCE because `notify.sh` discards the body and treats any 2xx as success so it cannot prove handset delivery, R3's pass condition now requires every hook-using session to be handed off or stopped and CONFIRMED before the reinstall, and C3 fails on any two checks inside the window rather than only on consecutive turns. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5681→5703.

### v3.104.1 (2026-07-28)
- **The blocked-pane watcher fires — and there were TWO reasons it never could, not one (#679, epic #684).** `hooks/pane_watch_lib.py` gains `poll_lines`, a drop-in for `socket_lines` that diffs `herdr api snapshot` reads, plus `merge_agent_sequences`, `_revision_of`, `clamp_poll_interval`, `write_heartbeat(extra=)` and `--source {poll,events}` / `--poll-interval-s`, with **poll** the default. **Cause 1 is what #679 diagnosed:** `events.subscribe` on herdr 0.7.5 delivers ~39 frames in under 3 s and then nothing, ever, so the watcher drained the replay and sat silent forever while reporting healthy. **Cause 2 was found by running the thing live, because the existing synthetic fixtures could not discover the external contract:** a real Claude pane driven to a real permission prompt went `working@revision 3` → `blocked@revision 3`, because herdr bumps `revision` on pane-record changes and not on every `agent_status` transition — so `Reconciler.accepts`, which requires a strictly newer key, refused the one frame the feature exists for. The watcher polled happily, `poll_failures: 0`, and sent **zero notifications**. The `revision 1 → 3` measurement everyone trusted spanned TWO transitions, which is exactly what hid it, and every fixture in the 100-test suite had supplied its own revision numbers — the seventh instance in this epic of a green suite over an invented contract. The dedup key is now **`state_change_seq`**, which lives on `snapshot.agents[]` and advanced 2148 → 2151 across that same transition: the key #612's first design wanted and abandoned **because the `pane.updated` EVENT does not carry it — the SNAPSHOT does**, so the poll path can have what the event path never could. `watch_stream` already took its lines as an argument and never touched a socket, so the debounce, provenance refusal, delivery-retry and startup-sweep logic — nine guards each earned by a separate Step-11 finding during #612 — survive the input swap; the seam is not quite as clean as "only the source changed", because source-specific key normalization also crosses parsing, reconciler initialization and deferred registration (a Step-11 finding corrected the broader claim). **Three review passes each found something real.** Closes now lead every batch absolutely, ahead of the ack and ahead of the regression error, because `watch_stream` runs its whole drain on ANY line once the heartbeat is due and a drain before a close is applied retries a pending send for a pane the same snapshot proves is gone — a test caught the ack itself doing that. Only agent-bearing panes are diffed: `state_change_seq` counts in the thousands and `revision` in single digits, so a fallback between them meant a routine agent detach (a session exits, its pane stays open) read as `2148 → 5` and killed the entire watcher as a bogus source reset; an agent ATTACHING re-baselines through a synthetic close rather than comparing across kinds. `read_snapshot` deliberately does NOT enrich — enriching centrally looked tidy and silently broke the retained events path, whose frames carry only a `revision` that would have been compared against a sequence baseline and dropped the day herdr repairs the feed. A key moving BACKWARDS stops the watcher loudly, compared against a per-pane high-water mark so an unreadable key cannot bridge the regression, because a poll loop survives a herdr restart that used to kill the socket and stale baselines would make it silently blind. The ack is emitted only after a poll has actually succeeded. Tolerated snapshot failures no longer beat once per failed poll, which would have moved the whole drain cadence from 300 s to the poll interval. And the heartbeat now carries `polls`/`poll_failures`, because `events` alone can never prove the input layer is alive — a healthy watcher on a quiet fleet reports a flat `events` too, which is precisely how a permanently silent subscription passed for healthy through an entire epic. **The detection gap is stated honestly rather than understated:** the generator is pull-driven and one drain walks every pending registration and every pending send sequentially, so the gap is cumulative and can be minutes when the transport is already failing — a block that persists is always detected on the next sample, so what degrades is lateness, never loss, and a test pins it. Verified live against a real herdr server (twice failing, then passing): one notification per real block, no pane contents in the body, `esc` clears it with no re-notify, throwaway pane closed; evidence retained at `docs/planning/2026-07-28-667-uat-plan/harness/evidence/`. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5618→5681.

### v3.104.0 (2026-07-28)
- **`--sender-cmd` makes the blocked-pane watcher testable without paging the owner — and the epic #667 UAT it enabled found that the watcher can never fire (#612, epic #667).** `hooks/pane_watch_lib.py` gains `sender_from_cmd` (pipes the notification body to a shell command on STDIN) and `resolve_sender` (production `_default_sender` unless an override is explicitly asked for), wired into `_cmd_watch` and exposed as `--sender-cmd`. Before this the only transport was `notify.sh`, so every acceptance check for the watcher's behaviour paged a real phone and the feature was effectively unobservable; `--dry-run` prints the subscribe frame and exits, which proves nothing about delivery. The seam already existed — `sender` was injected into both `startup_sweep` and `watch_stream` — only the CLI hardcoded the transport. `shell=True` is deliberate and documented: the command is an operator-supplied argument on the same command line that launched the process, never read from config or wire data, and the body travels on STDIN so pane text can never reach the shell. It is fail-OPEN as a sender (any exception becomes a non-zero rc) because `_send` already records a non-zero rc as a failed send, and a watcher that dies on a mistyped test command is worse than one that reports the send failed. **What it immediately bought:** running the shipped watcher against a live herdr server showed `events: 39` and no growth — `events.subscribe` on herdr 0.7.5 delivers a one-time backlog burst of ~30-40 frames in under 3 seconds and then delivers **nothing, ever**, so the watcher drains the replay and sits silent forever while reporting healthy. Confirmed by five independent lines including a controlled stimulus (three `herdr pane rename` calls on a pane we owned produced 0 frames on a live subscription) and duration-independence (39 events at a 12 s window, 39 at 12 s, 39 at 50 s). Ruled out with evidence rather than assumed: the request shape is correct (`type`, not `event`); `events.subscribe` accepts only `subscriptions` with no `since`/`replay` field; `ping` on a subscribed connection returns **`Connection reset by peer`**, so no keepalive is even possible; and the server exposes exactly two events methods out of 90. Filed as #679 with the fix-vs-redesign call left to the owner. The same UAT confirmed the premise the watcher rests on IS real — a Claude pane does reach `agent_status: blocked` via detection rule `bash_permission_prompt` (priority 850), `revision` 1→3 — and filed #680 (herdr's manifest matches a dead string, `contains expansion`, where Claude Code v2.1.220 now prints `Contains command_substitution`), #681 (the `audit_completeness: 0.75` stale-record theory refuted, quota-denial lead open) and #682 (a confounded observation that `project_switched`'s 120 s window can close a successor mid-bind). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5612→5618.

### v3.103.0 (2026-07-28)
- **The blocked→notify-owner watcher, built on the herdr events its design was WRONG about first (#612, epic #667).** Closes the epic's last implementation child: `hooks/pane_watch_lib.py` subscribes to herdr's socket, recognises a pane entering `blocked`, and hands one line to the transport that **already exists** — `projects/sentinel/bin/notify.sh` via the `notify-owner` skill (F-1: not rebuilt, and no BlueBubbles POST hand-rolled). **The design's first revision was refuted by its own Step-4 review and by the probes, and that is the useful part of this entry.** R1 was built on `pane.agent_status_changed` plus a `state_change_seq` dedup key; live capture found that event **never fires** (0 frames across 135 s subscribed to all 8 panes and 90 s on two named panes), that it cannot be subscribed globally (its subscription requires `pane_id`, and a global attempt is refused `invalid_request: missing field 'pane_id'`), and that it carries **no sequence number** at all — `state_change_seq` lives on `snapshot.agents`, not on the event and not on `snapshot.panes`. R1's 54 tests passed only because they synthesised the shape R1 assumed, the fifth green-suite-over-an-invented-contract in this epic. **What actually works, all observed on the wire:** `pane.updated` is GLOBAL, needs no `pane_id`, fires on real status changes, and embeds a full pane record with `agent_status`, `label`, `workspace_id`, `tab_id`, `agent` and a monotonic **`revision`** — the dedup key R1 wanted and could not find. So the watcher subscribes globally to `pane.updated` + `pane.closed`, which also dissolves the per-pane problem that subscriptions are fixed for a connection: a pane created later is covered by the same feed. **The backlog replay is real and is why reconciliation is load-bearing:** a fresh subscription delivered created/closed frames for six long-gone panes within 450 ms, one of them created AND closed inside the replay, so every frame is reconciled against `session.snapshot` (whose panes carry `revision`) and a frame with no revision fails CLOSED — without one there is no way to tell a replay from a live event, and guessing wrong pages the owner about a dead pane. A first observation of `blocked` counts as a transition, because an agent already waiting when the watcher starts is exactly the case F2 says to expect. **Debounce now reserves and only a CONFIRMED send commits**, closing a hole the review found in R1 where one failed transport consumed the window and, with no further transition, the owner never heard at all. **AC5 became a PROVENANCE boundary rather than keyword hygiene:** refusing forbidden keyword arguments stops nothing when a caller can pass `label=event.title`, and every pane record carries `terminal_title`, so `body_for_pane` is the only public builder, takes a pane RECORD, and selects `label`/`name`/`pane_id` itself — there is no parameter for caller text to abuse, a label identical to the record's own `terminal_title` is refused, and `pane.output_matched` (which carries `matched_line` and a whole `read.text`) is never subscribed. **AC3 ships with its limits stated:** the heartbeat needed a timer and an observer, so the socket read carries a timeout that yields on expiry and the heartbeat is written to disk for something external to check — a heartbeat nobody reads proves nothing, and that observer is a named follow-up. The stall warning is AC3's literal signal and its message says outright that it is **NOT a missed-prompt detector**, because an idle finished pane and a missed approval are indistinguishable by status and elapsed time. **AC2 is NOT discharged** and is recorded as verification_deferred: driving a real Claude/Codex approval screen needs a live blocking prompt on this host, and since `pane.agent_status_changed` was never seen firing, what herdr emits when a pane genuinely blocks has not been captured. Also narrowed #665's AC7 guard, which correctly fired on this watcher's `["herdr","api","snapshot"]`: it now flags only DRIVING verbs (split/close/send-text/send-keys/start/wait), with tests proving both that observing calls pass and that driving argv is still caught. **Step 11 then returned FAIL with a Blocker and four Highs, all confirmed:** the watcher registered new panes from a `pane_created` frame it **never subscribed to**, so every pane created after the initial snapshot was ignored forever — and the test that claimed to cover it manufactured the unrequested frame. `pane.created` is subscribed now. A pane **already blocked when the watcher starts** was never reported (the snapshot was not examined, any replayed frame is rejected as equal to the baseline, and the transition map starts empty), so a startup sweep notifies for it. A failed transport **still** lost the notification, because `prior` advanced to `blocked` before the send and the next blocked frame was therefore not a transition — the earlier test hid this by inserting a `working` frame between the attempts. Socket EOF **exited 0**, so an unattended watcher stopped watching in silence after a herdr restart; it is now a reported error and a non-zero exit. The AC5 refusal **logged the screen text it was refusing**, since the message interpolated the identity and `main` prints it to stderr — the value is withheld now and only the pane id and field name appear. A frame with an unrecognised status advanced the revision baseline, so a corrected frame at the same revision was then dropped. And `stall_warning` shipped as **dead code** — nothing tracked `since` or called it — so it is wired into the heartbeat tick. **A second Step-11 pass caught that the `pane.created` fix had REOPENED the replay hole** — the replay carries `created → updated(blocked) → closed` for long-gone panes, and an unconditional registration gave such a pane no revision baseline, so its blocked frame was accepted and the owner paged about a pane that died hours ago. A creation is now believed only when the pane is in a CURRENTLY observed pane set, and an unreadable set refuses — fail-closed, because a false page is the failure this class of bug produces. Four more: the stall warning rendered the identity **without** the body builder's content refusal and the heartbeat loop emits it, so a label copied from `terminal_title` leaked into the log (one shared `_refuse_screen_derived_identity` now guards both call sites); a failed transport could **still** lose the block permanently, because `accepts()` consumed the revision before the send and a stable blocked pane producing no further update was never retried (the baseline is rolled back on failure); a suppressed transition left `prior` unset so a later frame after the window looked like a NEW block and paged again though the pane never left `blocked`; and `since` was populated only from accepted stream updates, meaning the stalest panes — those already idle at startup — were exactly the ones the warning could not see, while closing a pane cleaned neither `since` nor its `stalls` entry. **A third Step-11 pass then found the delivery guarantee still broken on two paths, and the fix was to stop patching per-case:** a failed startup-sweep send scheduled no retry and a stable blocked pane emits nothing newer, so the owner was never told; and `rollback()` restored only integer baselines, so a newly registered pane (baseline `None`) had its consumed revision stay consumed and redelivery dropped. Both are now covered by ONE explicit pending-retry mechanism that the heartbeat drains, so delivery no longer depends on another frame arriving — and pending sends are reported at exit rather than vanishing with the process. Three more: a transient live-pane-set failure refused a creation (right) and then FORGOT it (wrong), blinding the watcher to a real pane forever — deferred registrations are retried; `live_pane_ids()` caught only `WatchError`, so a `TimeoutExpired` killed the watcher instead of returning the empty set its contract promises; and the startup sweep could page twice for one continuous block, because the stream began with an empty `prior` — it is now seeded from the sweep. **Two more of my tests were dishonest and a reviewer caught both:** one had a clock that never crossed the debounce window (so `suppressed` was 0 and the assertion proved nothing) and one would have passed with the `since` cleanup removed. Both fixed, and the surviving claims were mutation-checked — where a mutation did NOT fail a test, the test now says so instead of claiming a pin it does not have. **A fourth Step-11 pass found one more way to lose a block, in the deferred-registration path I had just added:** a creation deferred because the live-pane set was unreadable, followed by a `blocked` update that is DROPPED while the pane is still unknown, followed by a heartbeat that registers only the stale creation record (`unknown`, revision 0) — nothing ever notified, and `pending` was empty so there was no evidence either. The heartbeat now sweeps the pane's CURRENT state on registration. Three more from the same pass: a newer frame succeeding did not clear an older pending entry, so the next heartbeat paged the same continuous block twice; a pending send for a pane CLOSED in the same batch was delivered because the drain ran before the close was processed; and sender calls were unguarded while `main` catches only `WatchError`, so the real sender's `TimeoutExpired` killed the watcher AND bypassed the pending queue — every sender call now routes through one guard that treats an exception as a failed send. Four more of my tests were shown not to be mutation-sensitive and were rewritten; each surviving claim in the delivery path was then **mutation-verified** by removing the behaviour and confirming the test fails. **A fifth Step-11 pass found five more, four of them reproduced on HEAD, and one was an AC5 failure I had introduced myself:** the deferred registration was dropped the moment `register_pane` succeeded — BEFORE the current-state snapshot — so if that second snapshot failed the pane was registered but never swept, ending with no notification AND no pending evidence; the registration now stays pending until its sweep resolves. `startup_sweep`'s sender call was still unguarded while the guard lived only in `watch_stream`, and the sweep runs FIRST, so a real `TimeoutExpired` killed the watcher before any pending map came back. The heartbeat drain still ran BEFORE a `pane_closed` line in the same batch, so it paged for a pane that very line said was gone — lifecycle frames are now applied first. And **AC5 failed through a REPORT rather than a body**: a failed startup send stored the whole snapshot record in `pending`, which `_cmd_watch` prints as JSON, so `terminal_title` reached stdout — every record leaving the module now passes through a `safe_record` allowlist. Two more of my tests were caught unable to observe what they claimed (one called `forget_pane()` by hand instead of feeding a close event; one had a clock that never made the heartbeat due), and every pass-5 fix is mutation-verified. **A sixth pass found three more, and confirmed AC5 now passes clean:** `pending_at_exit` reported only failed SENDS, not unresolved deferred REGISTRATIONS — so a genuinely blocked pane whose registration never resolved could exit with `notified=[]` and `pending_at_exit=[]`, its only trace a log line about a deferred creation that says nothing about an undelivered block. Both queues are now reported separately, and an unresolved registration is recorded as an ERROR. A successful deferred sweep also left an older failed stream send queued, so the next drain paged the same continuous block twice. And the None-baseline test passed with `rollback()` no-op'd because the heartbeat retry bypassed reconciliation, so a stream-only test now pins it. **This child took six Step-11 passes and every one found something real** — the review record is the honest artifact here. **A seventh pass found one more, and it is the neatest illustration of the whole child:** the AC5 refusal — added to STOP a leak — was itself raised out of `watch_stream`, bypassing report finalization, so a blocked pane whose label equals its `terminal_title` ended with no notification AND no structured evidence. Refusing to name the pane is right; losing the accounting that proves something was owed is not, so a refusal is now a recorded failed send that lands in `pending_at_exit` with an `AC5 refusal` error. That pass also caught the deferred-sweep regression test pre-registering its pane, so `current_pane` was never called and it exercised an ordinary retry rather than the deferred sweep it named. **An eighth pass then found that my pass-7 AC5 fix had CREATED a real content leak** — the pending record kept the offending `label` while `safe_record` stripped `terminal_title`, so the retry could no longer repeat the provenance comparison and DELIVERED the screen text (reproduced on both the stream and startup paths). A refused pane is now re-queued by `pane_id` ALONE, so its identity falls back to something that is never screen-derived. Two more from the same pass: an unhandled refusal inside the stall loop aborted report finalization, so a DIFFERENT pane's stale label destroyed the accounting for a genuinely blocked one; and startup refusals were stranded in `sweep["errors"]` rather than reaching the top-level report the previous pass claimed they did. All three are mutation-verified. **A ninth pass then found the same leak by a third route, and the real lesson is that I had been patching symptoms of ONE root cause:** `safe_record()` retained a `label` while stripping `terminal_title`, so every later consumer — the pending retry, the deferred sweep's failure branch, the stall warning reading cached metadata — held the offending label but no longer had the field to compare it against, and the provenance check silently could not fire. Fixed at the boundary instead: `safe_record()` now DROPS an identity it cannot verify, so no stored record can carry a screen-derived name on any path, and the identity falls back to `name` or `pane_id`. Removing that one guard fails four tests. The deferred sweep also installs the fresh record into the reconciler, so the stall warning no longer reads scrubbed creation metadata. **A tenth pass confirmed no content leak by any route and found one last structural gap:** installing that fresh record set fresh METADATA but not a fresh revision BASELINE — `safe_record()` deliberately omits `revision`, so the baseline stayed `None` and `accepts()` would then take any positive revision, including one older than the snapshot just read (reproduced: a fresh `working@9` followed by a stale `blocked@1` notified the owner). The baseline is now installed explicitly alongside the metadata. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5510→5612.

### v3.102.2 (2026-07-28)
- **The herdr runbook gains workspace conventions, attach/detach/remote recipes, measured rough edges and a from-scratch walkthrough (#613, epic #667).** Closes the epic's documentation child, and every figure was read from this host rather than repeated: `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` §9-§12. **§9 corrects the acceptance criterion itself** — AC1 says "one workspace per project", but the live layout is ONE workspace for the whole fleet with ONE TAB PER PROJECT (`w1`, 7 tabs, 8 panes: rawgentic, saystory, sysop, lumenquire, herdr-dashboard, model_bench, thewanderinginn), and the doc recommends keeping it that way because glancing at every project's `agent_status` at once is what `tab list` inside a single workspace gives you. It also records the trap that a tab's display `number` and its `tab_id` suffix are unrelated (`number 30` is `w1:tY`, `number 36` is `w1:t14`), so a tab must be resolved by label or by reading `tab_id` — never built from the number — and that pane labels are optional and sparse (3 of 8 here) while `pane_id` is the only always-present field. **§10** documents attach (`herdr`, `herdr session attach <name>`), detach (**`prefix+q`**, a keybinding not a subcommand, leaving agents running), remote (`herdr --remote <ssh-target>`, `--remote-keybindings` defaulting to `local`, `--handoff`), the `[remote] manage_ssh_config` semantics read from the shipped config — herdr's generated ssh config includes yours FIRST and adds keepalives only as fallbacks, and setting it false does **not** force keepalive or multiplexing off — and why `server reload-config` is the safe lifecycle command while `server stop` takes every pane with it. **§11 measures the rough edges instead of repeating them:** the server (PID 8287, ~89 h elapsed) shows a cumulative average 10.9% CPU and 26.5 MB RSS, while three instantaneous idle samples read 10.0%, 0.0%, 0.0% — so the lifetime average is real but it does not spin at 11% when idle. **Key repeat is reported as UNVERIFIED**: grepping the shipped default config for `repeat|cpu|poll|interval|fps|tick` finds no relevant knob, so rather than assert the AC's claim the doc names what would settle it. Two more measured edges: `allow_nested = false` means an agent inside a pane cannot start its own herdr (so cross-pane work must go through the existing server), and `pane_history = false` means a server restart loses pane screen content — which is precisely why the handoff verifies against files and never scraped pane text. **§12 is the AC4 walkthrough**, with the honest caveat that its own criterion — a *second* operator driving a run from the page alone — cannot be self-certified by its author and is discharged only when somebody else follows it without asking. Suite 5510→5510. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV.

### v3.102.1 (2026-07-28)
- **#654's Q1 answered from live captures: context pressure is measurable mechanically, and the authoritative source needs no arithmetic (#654, epic #667).** Owner decision D-16 split the context-driven handoff into a mechanism (#665, shipped) and a *measurement* half, and this is the measurement half's findings — Q1 ONLY. Q2 (can a goal be set without a human), Q3 (restart mechanisms), Q4 (threshold arithmetic) and Q5 (recommendation + decomposition) are NOT answered and **#654 stays open** for them. **Source 1, the statusline payload, was captured LIVE** rather than read from a schema: one `printf` line inserted into `~/.claude/rawgentic-statusline.sh` after its existing `input=$(cat)`, a render observed, then the script restored from backup and the restore confirmed by sha256 with the probe line gone and the script still exiting 0. It delivers `context_window` with a pre-calculated `used_percentage: 65`, `context_window_size: 1000000`, `session_id` alongside it, and `exceeds_200k_tokens` for free. **The load-bearing correction:** `total_input_tokens` is NOT a session cumulative but the in-context total for the current request — `2 + 1257 + 652960 = 654219` exactly matches the reported field — so a consumer needs ONE field, not a sum. #654's warning that `usage_capture.py` is off by ~477x applies to the transcript rows, not to this. **Source 2, the transcript fallback, is required rather than optional** because headless and cron runs render no statusline, so nothing ever invokes the command: sum the last `message.usage` row's `input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens`. On the same live session seconds later it read 652,415 tokens = 65.2%, agreeing with the statusline's 65% to **1,804 tokens, 0.18% of the window** — cache-read drift between two readings, not a methodology error. A second reading from a session with **no statusline at all** (a subagent transcript, confirmed non-interactive) gave 173,667 tokens = 17.4% of a 1M window but **86.8% of a 200k one**, which is why any threshold must be expressed against `context_window_size` and never as an absolute token count. Stated limits rather than glossed: `/context` was NOT run (it is interactive and this run is unattended), so the cross-check is between the two mechanical sources instead; the no-statusline reading is from a subagent rather than a cron-spawned session, so cron is inference on an identical row shape and wants one confirming reading inside the first real cron run; and `used_percentage` is an integer, fine for tiers and too coarse for fractions. Nothing was implemented — #654's Scope puts implementation out of bounds and the statusline is a user-level file outside any git repo, so the ~3-line bridge cannot ship as a tested rawgentic PR; that placement question is Q5's. Findings: `docs/planning/2026-07-28-654-q1-context-measurement.md` (+ `.html`). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5510→5510.

### v3.102.0 (2026-07-28)
- **Interactive mid-child session handoff, built on #569's primitives rather than beside them — plus the pane-readiness fix the first live run of #611 exposed (#665, #673, epic #667).** #569 crosses a **child boundary**: a child ends, the session ends, a fresh one starts the next child. Nothing covered *"I am mid-child, out of context, hand me over and keep going"*, and doing it by hand on 2026-07-27 half-worked in three ways worth naming, because they are what this prevents: the goal never armed (an AUTO-MERGE run with no completion guard), the predecessor would not die (its unmet goal blocked Stop nine times, leaving the session alive and idle), and the task list did not transfer. The root cause was not a missing call — it was **no verification between steps**, because the handoff bypassed `driver_lib` entirely. The trigger here is **context exhaustion, never cron** (owner decision D-16 decoupled the two: the 5-hour cron window is a separate service that resumes the ALREADY-ACTIVE session, so it needs none of this machinery, and #611's ACs 2/3/5 were withdrawn into it rather than left dangling). **Three things were missing and only three** — durable mid-child position, the claim/ack wiring, and successor-driven teardown — so `driver_lib` gains `mid_child_handoff`, whose disposition the SAME `open_handoff` consumes unchanged, and the successor claims and acks through the same `handoff_claim`/`handoff_ack_started`. `handoff_claim` is deliberately NOT modified: it is #569's tested primitive, so the new cancelled-record refusal lives in the new callers instead. `handoff_pending` gains an optional `kind` and a ten-field `position`; with no position the written record is **byte-identical** to what #569 writes, which is the compatibility proof, and `queue.schema.json` already carries `additionalProperties: true` at every level. **`kind` is a CLOSED allowlist, not an equality test**, because #611's launcher reads the same file: absent means the legacy handoff, `mid_child` is refused there (a second successor would compete for one generation), and any other value — a misspelling, a different case, a non-string — is refused as unrecognised, since equality-only matching would let `MID_CHILD` or `42` fall through to the legacy branch. An aborted handoff **cancels its own record**: the record is written before the pane is split, and until a later `open_handoff` bumps the counter the abandoned record IS the current generation and therefore claimable, so a stray successor could take a lease on it; the cancel is monotonic, winning before the claim is `started` and refused after. Every `.driver-state` read-modify-write on this path goes through **one locked helper** holding `plan_lib.file_lock` across read → validate → atomic replace — an advisory lock only serialises writers that participate, so holding it for the write alone would still let another writer's update land between this one's read and its replace — and it locks a stable `<path>.lock` **sidecar** because `flock` follows the opened inode while an atomic replace installs a new one at the pathname. **The ladder is six checks in causal order**, each reading an on-disk artifact or live git state and none reading pane text: `spawned`, `goal_armed`, `prompt_landed`, `project_switched`, `position_rebuilt`, `state_claimed`. #611's three-step launch ladder is untouched (a separate tuple), and each step carries an `owner` that is load-bearing rather than decorative — a ladder carrying successor-owned checks forces predecessor-side teardown **off**, so a predecessor can never retire itself on the four checks it can make. `prompt_landed` matches its marker as a plain **SUBSTRING**: a live probe found pasted prompt text persisted verbatim in `queue-operation` and `queued_command` attachment rows and **not** in a `type:"user"` row, so a shape-keyed match would have failed every handoff — the same defect class as #611's invented `goal_status` shape, which passed its own tests by feeding the invention back. `project_switched` now requires the session id **and** project **and** project_path on the same line, because matching the id alone let a successor bound to the WRONG project pass and then retire a healthy predecessor. `spawned` is an **identity binding**: a session cannot discover its own pane id (herdr 0.7.5 exposes no pane environment), so the predecessor records `successor: {pane, session}` under the lock immediately after its own `pane get`, and only that recorded session may retire anything. **`position_rebuilt` is an ATTESTATION and is documented as one** — two earlier attempts read stronger than they were: an echo of values the successor had just copied proved nothing, and live `git rev-parse` readings were **vacuous**, because the shared checkout is already on `position.branch` (the predecessor was working there), so a successor that did nothing at all would have passed. What ships is a receipt carrying `{generation, claimant, branch_observed, repo_root_observed, step, test_baseline_observed, ts}`, written under the lock and validated **as read back**, so a stale or foreign receipt fails; `--show-toplevel` is compared before the branch, since a same-named branch in a different repository would otherwise authorise teardown for the wrong tree. No artifact this platform exposes would give independent proof, and the remaining protection is structural: teardown is a separate explicit command, successor-driven, runnable only by the recorded claimant. **Teardown refuses before anything destructive** on a foreign `kind`, a cancelled record, an invalid position, a self-predecessor, a caller that is not the recorded successor, or an `--anchor-pane` disagreeing with durable state; the claim is idempotent, because `handoff_claim` (probed) refuses a same-claimant re-claim inside the lease AND after `started`, so without that branch one failed teardown would block its own retry for the whole 1800 s lease. Then it proves the anchor still hosts the recorded session (a pane id is a reusable handle), and **re-checks that the successor's own guard is still in force** — `goal_armed` proves a guard existed at some point, only this proves the run is guarded NOW, and a **replacement** guard (a newest row for a different condition) refuses too, since a re-armed session has retired what was handed over. `teardown_phase` is persisted BEFORE anything is sent, then `/goal clear` goes out with **both** return codes checked and is **confirmed semantically** by polling for a `met:true, sentinel:true` row — a zero return code proves keystrokes were transported, not that the slash command was parsed, and without that confirmation a silently ignored clear reaches close-before-clear with every other check green. The close retries twice; if it never succeeds the predecessor is **re-armed from its own recorded condition** (reading it from the successor's transcript would arm the predecessor with the wrong guard, silently truncated if the 4000-char cap applied), reporting `alive_and_re_armed`, or `alive_and_unguarded` — the one state treated as an incident. **What is NOT closed is stated rather than buried:** between a confirmed clear and a successful close the predecessor is alive and unguarded, and a successor dying there re-arms nothing; the window is bounded (up to three close attempts, each fenced and probed) and made **discoverable in the normal case** by that persisted phase — a newer generation correctly refuses the generation-scoped incident write, and the report then says so, the consequence is a stalled run rather than lost work, and the fenced recovery actor that would close it properly is filed as a follow-up instead of half-built. AC7 ships as a source-level drift guard whose own negative cases are tested — five synthetic modules exercising each bypass form must each be flagged, because a guard never shown to bite is not a guard — and it is pinned for precision too, since `herdr` is a legitimate backend NAME in three real hooks. AC4's stated order is **amended, not deviated from**: the registry row is a consequence of the resume prompt, so checking it earlier could only pass on stale evidence, and arming the goal after handing over work reproduces failure 1. **#673 rides along because it is the same machinery, found the hard way:** the first live execution of #611's sequence aborted at `agent start` with `{"error":{"code":"agent_pane_busy"}}` — a freshly split pane is not yet an available shell — and the same call succeeded ~30 s later, so the condition is self-resolving. All 145 of #611's tests had passed because an injected runner answers instantly; the gap was between the split and the shell, which only a live run has. `agent start` now retries while herdr reports exactly that code, keyed on the machine-readable `error.code` and never on the human message (which embeds the pane id), with any other refusal staying terminal and a never-ready pane still failing closed and still closing the tentative pane. herdr's error payload is also **preserved on the step record**, which it was not — that omission is why diagnosing a 30-second condition needed a hand reproduction. Also fixed: `plan_lib.file_lock` became public in this change's first task and no skill referenced it, which `test_every_public_helper_is_referenced_in_skill` caught only on a whole-suite run; it is now documented where the loop-back counters it guards are described, rather than the guard being weakened. Docs: `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` §8 (and §7.1/§7.6/§7.8 corrected — §7.6 had claimed the sequence never ran against a live herdr server, which #673 falsified), `docs/multi-issue-driver.md`, and the design doc + rendered `.html`. **WF2 Step 8a then hardened the destructive path, and both independent reviewers converged on the same core defect:** `cancelled` and the claim were validated only at ENTRY, so a cancel or a superseding generation landing mid-teardown could not stop it, and the `teardown_phase` write validated nothing and ignored its own failure — it could stamp a DIFFERENT generation's record. Design §3 R4 had required exactly that re-validation under the lock immediately before the destructive step; the code did not do it. Now one fenced, locked write re-checks kind, cancellation, both generations and the claimant, and a write that does not land aborts rather than opening the unguarded window with nothing on disk to find it by. Five more confirmed findings from the same wave: `--session-id` overrode `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, so ANY session could pass the recorded successor's id and authorise the teardown — recreating the predecessor-driven path approach C was rejected for; the environment is now authoritative and a contradicting override is refused. Pane identity was proved once and the handle then reused across the clear, the close and every retry, with `pane get`'s return code ignored — it is now re-proved after the clear, and a pane that no longer hosts the predecessor reports `target_changed_after_clear` and touches nothing, because closing or re-arming would both act on a stranger's session. A `TimeoutExpired` from `pane close` escaped to the outer handler, skipping the remaining retries AND the whole re-arm while reporting a generic `error` instead of `alive_and_unguarded`; runner exceptions in that region are now failed attempts, not aborts. A successful `send-text` with a failed Enter left `/goal clear` STAGED in the predecessor's input while the code reset the phase and reported "STILL guarded" — that state is now recorded discoverably as `clear_staged_unsubmitted` and named in the reason. And the ladder itself is validated: an empty `steps` list authorised teardown with nothing proven, and a hand-built subset carrying no `owner` silently disabled the successor-owned half of the gate. Also bound `--repo-root` under `--project-root`, so `project_switched` and `position_rebuilt` can no longer prove two different repositories; gated the predecessor-owned checks BEFORE acking, since a `started` claim is never reclaimable and acking on already-failed evidence stranded the takeover; and re-ran the `kind` allowlist against a locked re-read immediately before launch, narrowing a genuine TOCTOU window. **Step 11 then REFUTED several of those fixes rather than confirming them, and both of its reviewers returned FAIL** — three defects were reproduced by direct probe before being fixed. Narrowing the identity override was not enough: `env -u CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID … --session-id <recorded successor>` restored the whole impersonation, so the environment is now REQUIRED wherever the value carries destructive authority. "Known step names" was not the ladder invariant: `teardown_allowed({"spawned": True}, steps=[{"step": "spawned"}])` returned True, so a ladder must now BE one of exactly three canonical tuples, which also rules out a reordered or duplicated one. Re-proving the pane once after the clear still left the close retries and the re-arm acting on a handle that a close timeout explicitly admits may already be reused, so identity is re-proved before EVERY destructive call. The post-clear identity probe itself bypassed the exception hardening, so a timeout there skipped both close and re-arm while reporting a generic error. A `send-text` that RAISED was treated as proof nothing was transported, erasing the evidence of a staged clear. `_record_successor` no-opped on a superseded generation while `perform_handoff` ignored its verdict, so a losing invocation kept arming a successor nobody could ever retire — that is now a failed launch. `--goal-condition` had no provenance check and `last_unmet_goal_condition` returns a condition a later `met:true` row has satisfied, so the mid-child path now validates the condition is the guard actually in force. The cancel-on-every-exit path caught too narrow a set to survive a `UnicodeDecodeError`. And the rebuild receipt carried a `step` that read-back never compared. Three doc claims were falsified and corrected rather than defended: the design's failure table said `clear_unconfirmed` leaves the predecessor "STILL guarded" (the guard state is genuinely ambiguous there), the runbook claimed the fence stops a supersession "arriving mid-teardown" (it stops one that has landed BY the fence — a lock cannot be held across a herdr call, and closing the final gap needs the fencing token already listed as out of scope), and the position_rebuilt failure row implied a `test_baseline` comparison the receipt does not carry. **A second Step 11 pass then refuted several of THOSE fixes in turn** — both reviewers returned FAIL again, and the honest summary is that this destructive path needed four review waves. The fence had been placed before `send-text` only, so a cancellation or superseding generation landing during the Enter, the confirmation poll, or a close retry stopped nothing; **every** destructive call now re-runs the full state fence, not just an identity probe. The predecessor's OWN guard was never revalidated at teardown time, so a predecessor that had been re-prompted and re-armed a different condition would have that new guard cleared — it is now checked against the recorded condition. The transcript baseline had been hoisted above the fence, which would let a `met:true` row predating the send "confirm" a clear herdr had ignored; it is taken immediately before the send. A transient post-clear `pane get` failure was treated identically to a proven mismatch, abandoning every remaining close retry and the re-arm, so the probe is now tri-state and only a PROVEN mismatch stops the sequence. An identity refusal between the text and the Enter erased the staged-clear warning it had just earned. `--goal-condition` provenance was unenforceable in production because the parser made it mutually exclusive with the transcript that would validate it — the transcript is now required and the flag is an assertion checked against it (and the test that claimed to pin this had fabricated an argparse state the CLI cannot produce). Cancel-on-every-exit moved to a `finally`, since `KeyboardInterrupt` is not an `Exception`. Terminal incident phase-writes now report when they cannot be persisted. `_close_tentative_pane` re-checks the pane still hosts the expected session before closing it. **Two claimed Criticals were NOT fixable and the CLAIMS were withdrawn instead:** teardown identity is an interlock against mistakes, not an authentication boundary — a caller controls its own child's environment, and any party who can set `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` can equally edit `.driver-state` or run `herdr pane close` directly — and the gap between the last check and the syscall cannot be closed without a fencing token herdr does not offer. Four dishonest TESTS were fixed rather than left passing: one asserted `... or True`, one confirmed the clear on `send-text` so it could not tell whether the Enter mattered, one overwrote the very receipt whose rejection it claimed to prove, and the shared fixture's `met:true` row lacked `sentinel:true` so it could never have backed the clear reader the design cited it for. **A THIRD Step 11 pass — the fourth review wave on this branch — found the decisive defect, and both reviewers again said DO NOT MERGE:** clear confirmation accepted ANY `met:true, sentinel:true` row rather than one carrying the recorded condition. One reviewer walked it end to end — the predecessor acquired a REPLACEMENT guard, clearing that produced its own `met:true` row, the poll took it as proof that our `/goal clear` had been executed, and an irreversible `pane close` followed on a live session. Confirmation is now bound to `position.goal_condition`. Four further confirmed findings: the predecessor-guard re-check FAILED OPEN (it refused only a differing condition, so an absent row, an unreadable transcript, or a newest row that was already `met:true` all passed) and now positively requires an unmet row for the recorded condition; the transcript baseline still preceded the state fence and identity probe, so a row landing during those round-trips could confirm a clear herdr never ran — the order is now fence → probe → baseline → send; a TRANSIENT state-read failure was collapsed into the same verdict as proven cancellation, so one I/O error after a confirmed clear abandoned every remaining close attempt and the re-arm; and an explicitly asserted condition could be a REPLACED guard, because the still-unmet predicate examines only matching rows, so it must now also be the newest. Plus: the rebuild receipt now carries and compares the recorded test baseline, which #665's own AC4 addendum required and the code had omitted; the staged-clear and `clear_unconfirmed` branches report a phase write they could not persist; and one more of my tests was dishonest — a "supersession" case that never created a supersession, since the command opened a fresh generation before the callback ran. Two doc absolutes were softened to match: the confirmed-clear window is discoverable **in the normal case** (a newer generation correctly refuses the generation-scoped incident write, and the report now says so), and the fence gap is not "one lock release" because a `pane get` follows it. The design's failure table gained the `target_changed_after_clear` state it could produce but did not list. **A fourth Step 11 pass — the fifth wave — found two more, one of them a race neither earlier wave had reached.** First: the pass-3 "fence → probe → baseline → send" claim was still FALSE, because `_destructive_call` re-ran its own state read and a whole `herdr pane get` subprocess between the baseline and the transport, so a `met:true` row for the recorded condition landing during those duplicated reads still confirmed a clear herdr never executed — a reviewer reproduced it by making the transport a no-op. The clear send now goes straight through `_run`, with the fence and probe done once immediately above, so nothing at all happens between the baseline and the send. Second, and independently reproduced: the close path re-checked driver state and pane identity but never GUARD state, so a predecessor that armed a NEW guard between the confirmed clear and the close was closed anyway — a live, guarded session destroyed. The declared clear-to-close window assumes the predecessor stays unguarded; this was the case where it does not. Every close attempt now refuses on a re-armed predecessor (`predecessor_re_armed`) and neither closes nor re-arms. Both reviewers also caught the receipt test being unable to fail if the read-back comparison were deleted; a stale-receipt rejection case was added. The two reviewers independently settled the one open disagreement — a third-wave run had reported `test_driver_bench` failing with `audit_completeness: 0.75`, and neither could find any ordering that reproduces it (isolated and full-suite runs pass, and the generated report's SHA-256 matches the committed file), so it is recorded as an environment artifact with a real underlying non-hermetic risk: the bench CLI recreates a shared capture directory, so genuinely PARALLEL runs can interleave. Filed as a follow-up rather than fixed here. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. **A fifth Step 11 pass — the sixth wave — found the re-arm fix had the SAME structural flaw as the clear fix**, and both reviewers reproduced it independently: the check ran, then `_destructive_call` did a state read and a whole `herdr pane get` before the close, so a guard armed during that window was closed anyway. The close loop is now explicit — state fence → identity probe → re-arm check → close, with nothing between the last two — and the re-arm check FAILS CLOSED on an unreadable transcript, because a reviewer hid a real re-arm behind one read error and got a close. Refusing costs a recoverable stall; proceeding destroys a live context. Two of my tests were also shown to be unable to fail: the ordering test asserted an always-empty slice and only checked probe adjacency, which the buggy version also satisfied — it now logs runner AND reader events into one ordered list and asserts the baseline is the last event before the send; and the re-arm test injected its guard before the check rather than during the pre-close probe it was named for. Remaining doc absolutes were reconciled: the receipt shape, the close sequence naming `predecessor_re_armed`, the identity wording (interlock, not authentication) in both the runbook and the design, and the driver doc's claim that every refusal leaves the predecessor guarded — true only before the clear. Suite 5362→5510.

### v3.101.0 (2026-07-28)
