- **A herdr launch mode for the durable resume launcher: the ordered handoff sequence, an in-repo entry point that drives it, and verification bound to the launch — the workspace launcher that would call it is not in git and is not wired here (#611, epic #667).** A herdr-gated project routes its WF2 build seat through `herdr pane split --current`; a cron-spawned launcher has no current pane, so the session it starts the ordinary pane-less way dies at its first build-seat dispatch with `no_current_pane` — which is why `twi-epic7-resume.sh` was disabled and epic #7 had no unattended path. **The fix rests on one probed fact and needs no `HerdrBackend` change:** `herdr pane split` accepts an EXPLICIT pane id, not only `--current`, so splitting from a named anchor pane needs no current pane, and the session started in the resulting pane *has* one. `hooks/launcher_lib.py handoff` is the entry point; it reads a campaign's driver-state, takes the disposition from `driver_lib.fresh_session_handoff`, and drives `perform_handoff` through an injectable runner (`subprocess.run(argv, shell=False)`, never a shell string). The workspace `*-resume.sh` launchers that would invoke it live **outside any git repository**, so wiring them is not part of this PR and no crontab line is installed. The sequence is: inventory panes → split from the anchor → `agent start` (carrying no goal) → `agent wait --until idle` → `pane get` for the successor's session id → paste and submit the goal → **verify the guard actually armed** → only then paste the resume prompt → verify the successor switched project → retire the predecessor last. Both orderings there were wrong in earlier revisions: a goal only re-prompts a session that tries to **stop**, so a successor armed but never given work sits idle and the run stalls silently with the predecessor already retired — while work handed to a session whose guard never armed is an unguarded run. The resume prompt is `driver_lib`'s own canonical wording for the next ready child so the two cannot drift, and the goal condition is read **verbatim** from the predecessor's last unmet `goal_status` row rather than retyped. **Verification reads on-disk artifacts only, never scraped pane text**, and is bound to this launch: each artifact gets a baseline of `(length, digest of that prefix)` that only later content is measured against — the registry's taken before the split, the successor transcript's once its session id is known and before the goal is pasted, since the file does not exist any earlier. The digest matters because `registry_prune.py` legitimately replaces the registry wholesale via `os.replace`, and a same-or-longer replacement slips straight past a positional offset. `goal_armed` additionally requires the row's condition to be the one actually armed, in its truncated form when the 4000-char cap applied, and `met: false` specifically — an already-met goal proves nothing. Only `FileNotFoundError` counts as an empty baseline; any other read failure refuses rather than letting a whole pre-existing file read as fresh evidence. Reads are bounded polls with an injected sleeper, since a single read races the hooks that write those files, and a `UnicodeDecodeError` from a read landing mid-character is retried rather than fatal. **Pane ownership is never guessed.** A pane is closed only when proven ours — herdr named it, it is not the anchor, and it was absent from the mandatory pre-split inventory; a `pane list` that fails refuses before anything is created. Otherwise the launcher prints a `POSSIBLE ORPHAN` line naming what appeared and closes nothing: `herdr pane split` takes `--env`, but 0.7.5's `pane get`/`pane list` expose no environment (verified against the live server), so a creation token cannot be read back, and closing a concurrent session's pane is worse than leaking one. That residual leak is a stated limitation, not a fixed problem. The wired path takes a **typed launch mode** rather than caller-supplied Claude options, and there is exactly one, `fresh`: a resumed successor can already own a registry row and an unmet goal row, making its evidence temporal rather than causal, and #569's contract was a fresh no-`--resume` successor anyway. `handoff` likewise accepts only the `herdr` verdict, because `pane_less` means "use the retained `claude --print` path" and splitting for it would retire the predecessor after launching by a different mechanism. The campaign's own `session_mode` decides whether a boundary exists at all, the launcher asserts its own capabilities instead of having them assumed, and `driver_lib.fresh_session_available` now **fails closed when the terminal-backend verdict is omitted** on a fresh-session campaign — the guard is structural rather than a matter of skill prose remembering to pass it, since by the time the launcher discovers the truth the driver has written `handoff_pending` and ended, and "keep the current loop" is no longer possible. `skills/epic-run/SKILL.md` resolves the verdict at that boundary. Two design claims were falsified by review and corrected rather than defended: the goal is no longer armed at birth (herdr refuses a control character in a native agent argument and requires a readiness timeout above 3000 ms, so argv-at-birth fails on exactly the multiline condition the requirement exists for — it is pasted after readiness via the route already proven for a 2847-char, 41-newline condition, and this module validates control characters and the timeout floor itself rather than trusting herdr to); and "argv-only" is no longer overstated as "no shell is involved", since herdr shell-quotes each element and submits a shell command, making the real residual risks argument and authority injection — hence the Claude-option allowlist that refuses `--permission-mode`/`--config`, and a `cwd` canonicalized and confined below the project root. 145 tests drive the module, including every abort path. **Shipped deliberately partial:** ACs 2 and 3 need an owner-gated crontab write, AC5 is blocked by #671, and the sequence has never run against a live herdr server or a real cron — all recorded in `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` §7.6 and in a blocker comment on #611 rather than claimed. Also corrects a documented gotcha: the top-level `herdr wait` is absent in 0.7.5 (#659), but `herdr agent wait` exists and is the readiness primitive. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5211→5362.

### v3.100.0 (2026-07-27)
- **The herdr Claude Code integration is now documented from measurement, and this repo has a herdr runbook (#610, epic #667).** `herdr integration install claude` was already installed at v7 on this host, so per the epic's verify-and-document rule the install was never re-run against the live harness; instead the footprint was captured by running the real installer against a sandboxed `$HOME`, with the live file's sha256 recorded before and after and proven **unchanged**. The committed evidence is labelled by strength rather than uniformly called "exact": **byte-exact** for the v7 hook script (`tests/fixtures/herdr/herdr-agent-state.v7.sh`, digest-asserted in CI) and for the installer's own unified diff (`tests/fixtures/herdr/install.diff`, regenerated and compared by a test); **redacted** for the before/after settings pair, which is real installer output over a SYNTHETIC settings file carrying a real file's shape and none of its content; **semantic** for claims about the live host. No committed artifact is a verbatim capture of a live `~/.claude/settings.json`, and the runbook says so where a reader will see it — this repo is public and that file holds the operator's permission allowlist. The semantic footprint is exactly **one** `SessionStart` entry — but the installer also re-serializes the whole settings.json with alphabetically sorted keys and drops the trailing newline, so a one-hook change renders as a ~300-line diff; that trap is now written down rather than rediscovered. Two more measured behaviours are recorded: install is idempotent on an exact-path match yet **appends** a duplicate when the wired path differs (so a settings.json carried between hosts or `$HOME`s accumulates a stale entry `install` will never clean up), and uninstall removes only its own entry, dropping the `SessionStart` key entirely when it was the sole member. AC2 is settled by reading rather than assertion: **wal-guard is not in `~/.claude/settings.json` at all** (the plugin registers it, so the installer cannot reach it) and mempalace's `PreCompact`/`Stop` entries survive unchanged — and the disjointness half is now an executable invariant, so a future edit claiming herdr owns `Stop` fails the suite. The runbook's reproduce-it-yourself recipe is **redaction-safe by construction**: it prints only the JSON *paths* that changed, never their values, cleans up its copy of your settings via a `trap`, and routes the unredacted-diff option to a mode-`0600` file in a trusted terminal with an explicit warning never to run it where a transcript would capture it. Lands as `integrations.claude` in `hooks/herdr-pin.json` (a top-level sibling of `pin`, because a v7 host integration and a 0.7.5 binary release version separately) plus `docs/runbooks/herdr.md` + rendered `.html`; 15 new guards extend `tests/hooks/test_herdr_pin.py`, split deliberately into record-integrity guards (which compare the authored record against constants and say so in their docstrings) and fixture-based guards that assert real installer output — the install adds exactly one `SessionStart` entry and every other hook owner comes out value-identical, which is what AC2 actually claims. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5196→5211.

### v3.99.0 (2026-07-27)
- **The herdr install is now vetted and pinned machine-readably — `hooks/herdr-pin.json` (#609, epic #667).** herdr became load-bearing for two projects' build seats on 2026-07-27 (`projects/rawgentic` at `e673192`, `projects/thewanderinginn` at `f64b467`) while sitting on an install of unrecorded provenance; this closes that gap. **The installed binary is now proven authentic bit-for-bit:** `sha256sum ~/.local/bin/herdr` returns `3dc83288…c59253`, and `gh api repos/ogulcancelik/herdr/releases/tags/v0.7.5` reports asset `herdr-linux-x86_64` with `digest: sha256:3dc83288…c59253` and an identical size of 21,315,048 bytes — so it is the official `v0.7.5` release (`isPrerelease: false`), not a look-alike. Provenance was established by **verification rather than re-installation**: the live herdr server was backing 8 panes with 4 sessions actively working, including another project's in-flight WF2 run, and re-installing would have risked them for no additional assurance. The release publishes no `checksums.txt`, so the API's per-asset `digest` field *is* the published checksum — recorded in the vet artifact so the method is reproducible rather than folkloric. **The pin is one file, not two truths:** `HERDR_VERSION_FLOOR = (0, 7, 5)` already shipped at `phase_executor/src/phase_executor/herdr_backend.py:84` (from #633), and mirrored constants drift silently, so a test asserts the pin file and that floor are equal — neither can move alone. Placement follows the existing convention (`hooks/security-patterns.json`, the same kebab-case JSON-beside-its-consumer shape) rather than inventing a home. **A misread was corrected by reading the source issue instead of trusting the AC text:** AC6 asks for the pin to be "verified by workspace-doctor", and #390 is still OPEN with no doctor in the tree — which looked like a hard block, until #390's own re-scope turned out to gate its herdr checks on *this* issue ("Only applies when the workspace has herdr adopted (HD1.1 config baseline present)"), so #390 depends on #609 and #609's job is only to record the pin in the shape check 8 will read. **Every config key in the hardened baseline was read from `herdr --default-config`, never assumed** — the load-bearing one is `[update] manifest_check`, whose default `true` makes herdr fetch a remote agent-detection manifest from herdr.dev in the background, meaning behavior can change under a pinned binary with no version bump; that is the hole a version pin alone does not close, and why AC2 exists. The baseline is documented with its apply procedure but **not applied to this host**: `herdr server reload-config` reloads config in the running server, so applying it needs no restart and endangers no pane — the earlier assumption that a restart was required was wrong and is corrected in the artifact. The one claim deliberately left unproven is that `reload-config` covers `[update]` and `[ui.sound]` specifically; executing it would have mutated the live shared server, so it is named as the residual risk rather than dressed up as verified. Vet artifact: `docs/reviews/2026-07-27-609-herdr-supply-chain-vet.md` (+ `.html`). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5189→5196.

### v3.98.0 (2026-07-27)
- **The herdr terminal backend is now ON for the `build` seat in this project (#638, epic #635).** `.rawgentic.json` gains `"executorTerminalBackend": { "version": 1, "build": "herdr" }` — a one-line config change and no code change; the backend and its gate shipped in #638, and this is the switch being thrown after UAT-3 passed live. Evidence for the flip: a real `dispatch --seat build` launched under herdr (registry row `terminal_backend: "herdr"`, `run_socket: "w1"` — a herdr *workspace id*, not a tmux socket path), the worker genuinely mutated its own isolated worktree while the shared checkout stayed clean, `status --run` reported `completed` with `probe_error: null` (#647's fix reading a real herdr-backed record), and no pane leaked; a review seat in the same run returned `action: "executor"` rather than `executor_supervised` and registered no terminal-backed row at all, which is the negative result confirming only `build` is gated. Verified after the flip through the sanctioned reader rather than by eye: `capabilities_lib derive` yields `executor_terminal_backend: "herdr"`, and a malformed value still fails closed (`build must be "tmux" or "herdr"`, rc 1) rather than silently reverting to tmux. **Operational precondition, and the reason this was not flipped earlier:** GAP-3 — `pane split --current` resolves the *calling* process's pane, so a build-seat dispatch from a detached cron process has nothing to resolve and fails loud. The owner's crontab carried eight active launchers, two of them (`epic475-resume.sh`, `epic-635-resume.sh`) pointed at this project on a 20-minute cycle against epics that are both CLOSED. All eight are now disabled — following the crontab's own established `#DISABLED-<date>-<reason>` convention rather than deleting the lines, so the record of what existed survives — leaving zero active entries and therefore zero paths by which an unattended process can attempt a herdr launch. Rollback is one line: delete the `executorTerminalBackend` key (absent means tmux); the crontab restores from `~/.crontab.bak-20260727-134406`. **`/rawgentic:setup` now offers the choice** — new **Step 2k** (`skills/setup/SKILL.md` + a walkthrough in `references/integrations.md`) presents tmux-vs-herdr, states up front that only the `build` seat is gateable and that every other seat stays on tmux regardless, and makes the operator confirm GAP-3 before herdr can be staged — choosing it means build-seat dispatches and unattended cron-resumed runs are mutually exclusive for that project. It validates the answer through `capabilities_lib derive` rather than by eye (a present-but-invalid section raises, so a non-zero exit re-offers instead of staging), stages the #531 answered-defaults sentinel `{"version": 1, "build": "tmux"}` when the user declines so the staleness nudge stops re-firing, keeps an existing key verbatim on decline, and is registered in `post_update_reconcile.py`'s `FEATURE_MANIFEST` as `project_config`-sourced (`since` 3.98.0) so projects predating it get nudged exactly once without workspace-sourced false positives. Six new tests cover the gap firing, the `since` boundary, both answered shapes, the fail-open on an uninspectable config, and the manifest entry's own shape; one existing end-to-end test had its fixture completed, because its premise that `phaseExecutorTable` was the *only* project_config gap candidate stopped being true. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5183→5189.
### v3.97.3 (2026-07-27)
- **Spike #654's two open compaction questions answered live, written up as a runnable test plan; the epic #635 UAT console refreshed down to the one item that still needs the owner (#654, #656, epic #635).** Docs-only. Both items the #654 brainstorm left as "open, NOT verified by anyone" were settled by live probe in a throwaway herdr pane, using an env-gated hook rig that exits 0 unless `RG_PRECOMPACT_TEST=1` so it could never touch a live session: **(1) a PreCompact block reason DOES reach the model**, delivered as `<local-command-stderr>` attached to the `/compact` command message — proven by the pane's agent quoting the unguessable marker back from context with no tool call, which also confirms the payload's `trigger` field is present and correctly valued (`trigger=manual`) and so D3's "never hijack a manual compact" rule is implementable as written; and **(2) repeated vetoes do NOT wall the turn for `trigger=manual`** — 5 `/compact` submissions, ≥3 distinct blocks each followed by a genuine assistant reply, pane ending idle, no `stopHookBlockingCount`-style cap hit. Result (2) is deliberately scoped narrowly: it says nothing about an *auto* compaction vetoed at real context pressure, which is the case D3 actually depends on and which is now T4 in the plan. New `docs/planning/2026-07-27-spike-654-test-plan.md` (+ rendered `.html`) covers what remains — T1/T2 for AC1's two reading paths, T3 for the real auto-compact percentage on 1M and 200k windows (shrinking the window via the confirmed `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONTEXT_TOKENS` knob rather than spending hours filling 1M), T4 as the explicit decision gate with its inversion stated in advance, and R1 as the end-to-end restart rehearsal — plus the two herdr gotchas that each nearly produced a wrong answer: `herdr wait …` is documented by the skill but absent from the installed 0.7.5 binary (a live instance of the drift #659 tracks), and `pane wait-output --match` matches your own prompt echo rather than the reply, which produced a false "ALL ROUNDS SURVIVED" before it was caught. `docs/planning/2026-07-24-635-epic-uat-plan.md` and its console are refreshed against the four decisions taken since they were written: UAT-1 retired (the #647 WF2 run drove real executor dispatches through #636's seam under the tmux default — stated precisely, those were *review* seats, so the build-seat argv path still rides UAT-3), UAT-5 retired (all three gate arms observed), UAT-6 closed (GAP-3 accepted as a scope boundary, D-4), GAP-1 filed as #661, GAP-4's "nobody noticed herdr changed" half filed as #659, and GAP-5 marked shipped by #647. The console's checklist drops from six checks to three so the owner is not asked to re-run anything already evidenced — retired items keep their cards and evidence in a new SETTLED section outside `CHECKS`, deliberately not deleted, since a removed item reads as a forgotten one. **UAT-3 itself is rewritten as a verified standalone drill** after the owner reported the step-by-step instructions were confusing: the old version opened with a precondition phrased as a step, gave the config change as a bare JSON fragment with no indication of where it goes, and asked the reader to "run a real issue whose plan dispatches a build seat" — naming no issue, no command, and no way to tell whether an issue qualified, which made the one open UAT item effectively unexecutable. It is replaced by an eight-step ~20-minute drill needing no real issue, whose every command was executed twice on this host before being written down: `mint-gate` (a build seat requires an authenticated gate bound to the plan by digest, and `parse_tasks` fails closed without a `- riskLevel:` line) → `begin-run` → flip the top-level `executorTerminalBackend` key → dispatch → three evidence checks → unconditional teardown. Verifying it surfaced the reason the old wording could never have worked: **the herdr pane self-closes the instant the worker exits and the drill takes ~10 seconds**, so "watch for a new pane" is unobservable unless a watcher is started first — the drill now makes that a required step and points at the durable evidence instead (the registry row's `terminal_backend: herdr` + `run_socket: w1`, a herdr *workspace id*, and `status --run` reporting `completed` with `probe_error: null`, which is #647's fix reading a real herdr-backed record). Two further observed-but-expected surprises are called out so they are not read as failures: the build seat routes to codex/`gpt-5.6-terra` via its cross-model chain entry, and a no-op brief yields `parse_status: no_response`. Verifying the drill then **narrowed UAT-3 itself**, on the owner's call: the launch path is now proven, so asking them to re-run it would be busywork, but the drill's no-op brief deliberately never exercised the two things that matter most. UAT-3 is therefore reduced to exactly those — **3a**, a build seat that actually mutates its isolated worktree (a real build runs for minutes, not the 10 seconds the plumbing test took, and nothing has watched a herdr-hosted worker do real work), and **3b**, the live negative that a review seat in the same run still launches under tmux, which until now was confirmed only by reading `supervisor.resolve_backend` rather than by watching one launch. The proven half moves into the evidence panel labelled as not-to-repeat, and both documents state plainly which commands were executed here and which are the owner's to run — 3a's real-work brief and the whole of 3b are written from the verified skeleton and the CLI's own argument list, not from a run anyone watched. Finally, the owner reported that the confusing item was actually **UAT-2**, not UAT-3 — UAT-2 is the first check on the page, so "three steps, same as before" was UAT-2's untouched three-step body. **UAT-2 and UAT-4 are therefore fleshed out to the same standard**: exact file locations instead of "read the WF2 prose", the dense contract restated as six plain checkable claims, and a decidable question at the end. Writing them surfaced a genuine doc defect — UAT-2 and #637's AC5 both said the contract lives in "WF2/WF3 skill prose", but `skills/fix-bug/` has **zero** `park`, **zero** `worktree` and **zero** build-seat references: WF3 never puts a build seat in a worktree so it has nothing to park, and its design-flaw path hands off to WF2 (`skills/fix-bug/references/steps.md:227`). Readers were being sent hunting for prose that should not exist; the AC text overstates, the implementation is not missing. Both items also now carry a **verified-against-the-code** block, since whether the prose is *accurate* is checkable even though the acceptance is not: UAT-2's park-before-reset and never-trust-`rc==0` claims hold (`worktree.py:585-599`, and the code is in fact stricter than the prose — it resolves its own stash from the `refs/stash` reflog by message, because a positional read is unsafe under concurrency), and all four of UAT-4's risks accurately describe the module (`herdr_backend.py:149`/`:128`, `:389-394`, `:380,418,422`, `:422`). That removes the reading burden from both and leaves only the judgment. The redeploy notices in both documents were then updated once the owner reinstalled the plugin: the installed cache is now **3.97.2**, verified 2026-07-27 to carry `liveness_unknown`, the new `status_live_verdict` helper and the module-level `resolve_backend`, so UAT-3 is ready to run — with the caveat, now stated in both docs, that a session keeps the plugin version it booted with, so UAT-3 must be run from a session started *after* the reinstall. After three rounds of rewording failed to fix it, the owner named the real problem — the instructions were structurally unusable: steps referred to "(command 1)" pointing at copy-boxes rendered further down the page, nothing said these were *terminal* commands rather than something to paste into Claude, no working directory was given, and three separate UAT items had no stated order. The UAT session is therefore rebuilt as **one flat numbered runbook, steps 1–17, top to bottom**: a preamble answering where to type them (a terminal), which directory (one `cd`, in step 1), and what order (1–4 = UAT-2, 5–6 = UAT-4, 7–17 = UAT-3); every command inline under the step that uses it; each decision step ending in "write down PASS or FAIL"; and a results table to post on #656. The console keeps its verdict-recording but its renderer was changed so commands are emitted INSIDE their `<li>` rather than in a trailing block — the cross-reference that caused the confusion is now structurally impossible — and its banner points at the flat list. Hand-editing JSON is gone: flipping and restoring the `executorTerminalBackend` gate are both one-liners. The owner then ran UAT-3 and **both halves passed**: the build seat launched under herdr (`terminal_backend: "herdr"`, `run_socket: "w1"`), the worker really wrote its file into its own isolated worktree with the main checkout left clean, `status --run` reported `completed` with `probe_error: null` — #647's fix reading a real herdr-backed job — and no pane leaked; the review seat in the same run returned `action: "executor"` rather than `executor_supervised` and registered no terminal-backed row at all, which is the negative result. GAP-2 is closed. Running UAT-4 also **overturned a claim this PR had already shipped**: it asserted "4/4 recorded risks are accurate", but what had actually been verified was that the code does what each risk describes — nobody had checked all four were *recorded in the docstring*. Three are; `exec`-injection is not in `herdr_backend.py` at all, living instead at `tests/phase_executor/live/herdr_ac1_protocol.py:144` (the `launch_argv` docstring, pinned EQUAL to `new_session`'s real call by a CI-safe test) and `README.md:745`. ### v3.97.2 (2026-07-27)
- **The read-only run-status surface now resolves each record's OWN terminal backend instead of always probing tmux, and an undeterminable probe reports `liveness_unknown` rather than claiming the job exited (#647).** `hooks/executor_routing_lib.py`'s `live_fn` ran `tmux -S <record.run_socket> has-session` unconditionally; a herdr-backed record's `run_socket` is a herdr *workspace id*, so tmux exited nonzero for an entirely ordinary reason, the closure returned `(False, None)` with no `probe_error`, and `derive_state` reported `exited_no_sentinel` — a positive claim that the job EXITED, derived from a probe that never ran against the right runtime. This is the last instance of the defect class #638 spent eleven review passes removing from `_live`/`recover`/`reap`/`has_session`/`list_sessions`: an operational failure read as a definitive answer. The fix routes this surface through the seam that already existed — `TerminalBackend.probe_session(endpoint, name) -> Liveness`, contractually non-raising, tri-state — via a new module-level `status_live_verdict(record, *, tmux, herdr, tmux_present)` whose backends are INJECTED, because a closure offers no seam for the acceptance tests and CI has no herdr binary to drive the real thing. Backend resolution moved to a new module-level `supervisor.resolve_backend(terminal_backend, *, tmux, herdr)` that `TmuxSupervisor._resolve_backend` now delegates to, so one rule serves both callers; the lift was necessary rather than cosmetic because `TmuxSupervisor.__init__` constructs a `JobRegistry` whose own `__init__` mkdir/chmods the registry root, a metadata write the `AC-J3` read-only invariant forbids on this surface — the same reason #471 W8 lifted `read_sentinel`/`derive_state`/`run_status`. `derive_state` gains keyword-only `liveness_unknown` (default `False`, so `TmuxSupervisor.status()` is byte-identical — it never sees an indeterminate probe, since `_live()` raises `SupervisorError` on `INDETERMINATE` first), ranked below a valid sentinel and a terminal recorded state and above the `exited_no_sentinel` default; `run_status` derives it from `probe_error is not None`, which already meant "could not tell" on both pre-existing paths, so `live_fn`'s injected contract did not have to change. The new state is DERIVED-ONLY and never written to the registry, so `JOB_STATES` and per-row `recorded_state` keep distinguishing every OQ-8 state. A tmux record's routine "no sessions on this socket" still classifies `CONFIRMED_GONE` through the existing allowlist and still derives `exited_no_sentinel` with `probe_error: null`; what DOES change for tmux is that a socket permission error or unparseable body now reports `liveness_unknown` instead of a false death, which the cross-model reviewer judged the intended correction rather than an AC3 breach. The tmux-availability check moved after backend resolution — evaluated first, a herdr record on a tmux-less host carried `probe_error: "tmux unavailable on this host"`, a true degradation flag with a false reason. Beyond the issue's ACs and flagged as such, `resolve_backend` now RAISES on an unrecognised backend instead of falling through to tmux: `registry.KNOWN_TERMINAL_BACKENDS` makes that unreachable for a decoded record today, but adding a third backend to that frozenset without teaching the resolver would silently probe the wrong runtime. 17 new tests red-before-green across `tests/phase_executor/test_status.py`, `tests/phase_executor/test_supervisor_backend_resolver.py`, `tests/hooks/test_executor_routing.py` and `tests/hooks/test_capabilities_lib.py`, including one that drives the real `_do_status` wiring with an injected backend — added because the cross-model Step-8a review correctly found that the first five tests pinned the verdict helper while leaving the `live_fn` binding free to regress, and one drift guard pinning the new public contract in `docs/config-reference.md` after the Step-11 review found the paragraph unpinned. `test_run_status_probe_error_visible` (#471 gpt-diff A3) is deliberately superseded: it required `exited_no_sentinel` with the degradation merely legible beside it, which is the exact pairing this issue calls a defect; its intent is preserved because `probe_error` is still asserted. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5166→5183.
### v3.97.1 (2026-07-25)
- **#633 §AC1's PID-identity protocol committed as the re-runnable pre-upgrade gate for the herdr build-seat backend (#639, epic #635 C3).** herdr's `pane run <pane> exec <argv>` launch — the primitive #638's `HerdrBackend` is built on — is herdr-undocumented and herdr-unendorsed (its own guidance is the plain non-`exec` form), so a future herdr release could wrap the command in a child instead of `exec`-replacing the pane's shell and every PID the supervisor holds would silently name the wrong process. #633 qualified the primitive live (40/40 reps against pinned herdr 0.7.5) with a runner that lived in session-tmp and is now gone; this ships that protocol as committed infra so the primitive is **re-qualified rather than re-derived** before the backend is trusted on a new herdr version. New `tests/phase_executor/live/herdr_ac1_protocol.py` holds the harness AND the sentinel worker in ONE file so their on-disk contract cannot drift, with #633's thresholds in code (`GO_MAX_FAILURES = 0`, `REPS_PER_CONDITION = 20`) enforced by `decide()` rather than left to a human reading a table. The verdict is deliberately **TRI-STATE** (`GO` / `NO_GO` / `ERROR`, precedence `NO_GO > ERROR > GO`) and `RepResult` keeps `identity_failures` and `env_faults` as separate lists that are never summed: a failed split or a wedged daemon can never read as GO, and equally never as a regression — the design self-review caught the first draft doing exactly the thing #638 spent eleven review passes removing from the supervisor (an operational failure read as a definitive answer), which cost one design loop-back. `tests/phase_executor/live/test_herdr_pid_identity_live.py` is the gate itself: 20 cold-pane + 20 reused-pane reps, one rep through the PRODUCTION entry point `new_session()`, and a live `preflight` so a herdr upgrade that breaks any single verb (split/rename/run/get/process-info/list/close) is reported by the same gate. Gating is module-level `pytest.mark.live` (the existing conftest skips unless `RUN_LIVE=1`) plus a `skipif` requiring the binary, the qualified version floor, AND a resolvable calling pane — `pane split --current` cannot resolve from a pane-less process, so a herdr-installed-but-pane-less host (cron, a plain headless shell) SKIPS with a named reason instead of failing. 38 CI-safe tests in `tests/phase_executor/test_herdr_ac1_protocol.py` cover everything herdr-less CI can: a fake daemon that stops honoring `exec` drives `NO_GO`, a fake whose provisioning fails drives `ERROR`, a clean fake drives `GO`, and `launch_argv()` is pinned EQUAL to the argv `HerdrBackend.new_session` actually issues, so a change to the product code's launch form fails in CI rather than letting this check quietly qualify a form nothing uses any more. The harness reaches into none of `HerdrBackend`'s privates (identity, liveness and teardown all go through its public surface); reps provision their own pane because `new_session` always splits a fresh one and the `reused` condition needs a pane that already ran a plain non-`exec` command — a pane cannot host two sequential execs, since the first exit kills it. Two deltas from the original runner are stated in the module docstring rather than papered over: that self-provisioning, and the fact that the `new_session` rep cannot observe a pre-`exec` pid because its three calls are atomic from the caller's side. Live evidence on this host (herdr 0.7.5, from a session inside pane `w1:p1`): **GO — cold 20 reps 0 identity 0 env, reused 20 reps 0 identity 0 env**, 40 distinct pids across 40 reps, 40/40 with pre-exec == mid-run == worker-recorded pid, 0 panes left behind; with herdr stripped from PATH the pytest form exits 0 with 3 visible SKIPs, and the gate entry point exits 4 printing "The gate did NOT run — this is not a pass." The pre-upgrade gate is a dedicated entry point — `python3 tests/phase_executor/live/herdr_ac1_protocol.py --gate` — whose EXIT CODE carries the verdict (`0` GO, `2` NO_GO, `3` ERROR, `4` prerequisites unavailable, i.e. the gate did not run), because the pytest form cannot serve as the gate: pytest exits 0 when every test SKIPS, so on any herdr-less host a green exit there means "the check did not run", not "the backend is qualified" — the same no-answer-as-success trap one level up from the one the tri-state closes. That hole, plus a vacuous-check hole (`pane_pid` returns rc=0 carrying whatever herdr reports, so an unparseable `shell_pid` left `pre_pid = None` and every `is not None`-guarded identity comparison silently evaporated, letting a rep that observed NO pid at all count toward GO), were found by the cross-model Codex diff review at Step 11 and are both fixed here: unusable pids are refused as env faults and the pid-chain comparisons are now unconditional. Its third finding — a self-timed-out worker being read as a clean completion — had already been caught and fixed by the author's own review pass, an independent corroboration; its fourth (availability probing spawned herdr subprocesses at pytest COLLECTION time even with `RUN_LIVE` unset, where a wedged daemon on the runner's 30s default could stall the whole suite) is fixed by probing only under `RUN_LIVE=1` with a 5s leash. Docs name the gate as the required step in both `docs/config-reference.md` (the `executorTerminalBackend` switch) and #633's own design doc, closing that doc's named risk #1. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 5128→5166.
### v3.97.0 (2026-07-24)
- **`HerdrBackend` + build-seat config-gate — a second `TerminalBackend` on herdr's pane primitives, tmux stays the default (#638, epic #635 C2).** New `phase_executor/src/phase_executor/herdr_backend.py`: implements the #636 `TerminalBackend` protocol via `pane split --current --direction <right|down>` → `pane rename` → `pane run <pane> exec <argv>` (three herdr calls, not atomic — a failure or exception after `split` best-effort closes the orphaned pane; a successful split with an unparseable body is also treated as a failure). `--current` resolves the CALLING PROCESS's own pane context (confirmed live: an orchestrating session splits its own pane to spin up a build-seat subprocess); the new pane inherits that pane's workspace implicitly. herdr assigns its own pane id rather than accepting a caller-chosen one, so every later call resolves the caller's `name` via `pane list --workspace <ws>` filtered by `label` — confirmed live against this host's own real herdr 0.7.5 daemon that `pane get`/`process-info` do NOT accept a label directly, only `pane list` surfaces it; a FAILED/malformed list is now distinguished from a genuinely-empty successful one (a Step-11 finding — the two were conflated, which made `kill_session` falsely report a transient list failure as a clean idempotent close). herdr has no private per-run env channel like tmux's `subprocess.run(env=...)` on a per-run socket, so `pane_env` (PYTHONPATH, never credentials) threads through `pane split --env KEY=VALUE` instead — an owner-accepted risk (2026-07-24): visible via `ps` to any user on the host for the pane's lifetime, gone when the pane closes, host-local and ephemeral only. New `.rawgentic.json` `executorTerminalBackend` key (`{"version": 1, "build": "tmux"|"herdr"}`, absent = `"tmux"`, malformed fails closed) selects the backend for the `build` seat only, exposed via `capabilities_lib.py` as `executor_terminal_backend`; `select_launch_terminal_backend` is the gate decision itself, keyed on the seat's DECLARED ROLE (never its literal name) so no other seat is ever routed to herdr regardless of config.
  Design critique (Step 4) correctly rejected the first design's file-list scope as too narrow: `TmuxSupervisor` needed a genuine backend RESOLVER (`_resolve_backend`, keyed on a new additive `JobRecord.terminal_backend` field), not just a `backend=` kwarg at the three provisioning sites — `launch`/`_live`/`_kill_job`/`reap`/`kill_server`/`preflight`/`resolve_socket` all resolve per-record, fully backward-compatible. Two independent Codex Step-8a reviews of that fix each found a real, deeper flaw in the fix that preceded it. The Step-11 final review then found FOUR High-severity defects the mocked test suite could not catch because it never exercised the real binary: (1) `pane split` has no `--workspace` option at all — the shipped code could not launch anything against real herdr, only against its own mocks; (2) `reap()`'s bookkeeping tolerance for an unresolvable backend was itself unsafe — skipping only the `list_sessions` contribution left a genuinely-alive job outside `live_fresh`, and the REAL OS-level `dead_fn` check would then correctly-on-its-own-terms call it "not dead," routing it to `kill_tree` and killing a healthy process (the SAME bug was then found by inspection in `recover()` too, which the reviewer hadn't flagged); (3) an exception from `rename`/`run` (e.g. a runner timeout) bypassed orphan cleanup entirely, and a successful split with a malformed body falsely satisfied the success contract; (4) `pane_env` never reached the spawned process's environment at all. All four fixed, then verified against the REAL herdr daemon end-to-end (not mocks): a live launch, PID retrieval, liveness check, and clean kill with no leaked pane, plus a live confirmation that `pane_env` genuinely reaches the spawned process. `resume_dispatch` (site 2) and `_do_recover_run` (site 3) get `herdr_backend` configured for correct recovery of existing records but need no active gate wiring: a build-seat job is always mutating, and `resume_dispatch` already categorically refuses any mutating profile before ever launching. A SECOND, confirming Step-11 review of that fix found the "tolerate an unresolvable backend" fix was itself incomplete: it only guarded a *misconfigured* backend, not a *properly configured* one whose underlying `list`/liveness check merely transiently fails — `has_session()` was still swallowing that failure into an ordinary nonzero result, which `_live()` read as "definitively dead." Fixed by letting the failure propagate instead of being swallowed: `has_session()` now lets a genuine list-command failure raise rather than converting it to a nonzero result; `_live()` catches that and re-raises `SupervisorError`; `recover()`'s guard now wraps the liveness check itself, not just backend resolution; `reap()` excludes every record on a backend whose `list_sessions()` call *raises* (a genuine failure) — but never merely returns nonzero, since that is `TmuxBackend`'s own routine "no sessions on this socket" exit and must keep going through the ordinary OS-level dead-process sweep (a first attempt at this fix conflated the two and broke that ordinary case, caught by re-running the full suite). Also closed: `list_sessions()`'s identical malformed-shape-defaults-to-empty bug (shared a new `_list_panes` helper with `_resolve_pane_id` to fix both at once); a cleanup-time exception in `new_session`'s `finally` block could previously mask the real failure it was cleaning up after (now best-effort, never propagates); and a malformed-but-`rc=0` split response can leak an unlabeled pane with no `pane_id` available to close it — a narrow, structurally-unavoidable residual risk, documented and accepted rather than chased with a racy list-diff heuristic. Re-verified against the REAL herdr daemon end-to-end after these fixes (live split→rename→run→`pane_pid`-based PID retrieval→`has_session`→`list_sessions`→`kill_session`, confirmed no leaked pane afterward). A THIRD confirming Step-11 pass then found three more real High-severity paths to the same destructive outcome, none of them the ones already fixed: (1) `has_session()` resolved a real `pane_id` from a SUCCESSFUL list, then forwarded a nonzero return from the FOLLOW-UP `pane get` call unchanged — so a daemon hiccup on that second call still read as "definitively dead"; only a confirmed `error.code == "pane_not_found"` now returns nonzero (a genuine resolve-then-close race), and every other nonzero/malformed `get` response raises the new `_PaneGetError`. (2) `_list_panes`'s shape validation checked the top-level `result.panes` list but not its ENTRIES, so a body like `{"result":{"panes":[{}]}}` or `panes:[42]` passed and both consumers silently filtered the malformed entries — `_resolve_pane_id` reading "not found", `list_sessions` reporting a successful empty enumeration; every entry must now be a dict carrying a non-empty string `pane_id` (only `label` is ever legitimately absent, for a pane this backend doesn't manage), and a violation raises rather than being dropped. (3) `reap()`'s per-backend `list_sessions` union keyed on the backend OBJECT alone, so two records sharing one herdr backend but different endpoints (two workspaces under one run) silently shared only the FIRST endpoint seen — the second's sessions were never listed at all; now keyed on the `(backend, endpoint)` pair, with exclusion tracked per pair. A FOURTH confirming pass then found three more: (1) `resolve_endpoint` accepted any configured workspace while `split --current` ignores it, so if the calling pane's workspace differed from `HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID` the pane was created in A while every later call searched B — `kill_session` against the empty B reported *idempotent success* while the process in A stayed alive, unregistered, permit released; `new_session`/`preflight` now verify the split response's own `workspace_id` against the endpoint (confirmed present live) and fail closed, and preflight additionally asserts its probe pane is actually VISIBLE in `pane list --workspace <endpoint>` rather than just checking `rc == 0`. (2) The pass-3 entry validation checked `pane_id` but not `label`, so `{"pane_id": "w1:p9", "label": []}` passed and the malformed label compared unequal — a DEFINITIVE "not found" for a live job, sending a healthy record to quarantine/kill; a present `label` must now be a string (absent/`None` stays legitimate — that's an unmanaged pane). (3) `_live()`'s new raise propagated out of `await_job()` uncaught, so one transient probe failure aborted a healthy dispatch with its permit held; an indeterminate probe now means "not known dead" and keeps polling to the existing deadline. A FIFTH pass then found two more High plus a test gap, all fixed here: (1) the launch teardown released the quota permit UNCONDITIONALLY and ignored `kill_session`'s own result, so an unconfirmed teardown (nonzero close, or a runner timeout) freed the slot while a possibly-live, unregistered process kept running — letting the pool over-admit past its ceiling; the permit is now released only on a CONFIRMED teardown and otherwise stays held, exactly as `_finish(release_permit=False)` already does for every other death-not-verified path (a pre-spawn failure still frees it, guarded by its own test), and `HerdrBackend`'s best-effort orphan cleanup now records whether the close confirmed rather than swallowing it silently. (2) The pass-4 label validation accepted a present-but-BLANK label (`""`, `"   "`, newline-only), which then vanished exactly like the non-string case — unequal in `_resolve_pane_id` (a definitive not-found for a live job), falsey so `list_sessions` dropped it, and `reap()` strips whitespace and dropped it too; only an ABSENT/null label now denotes an unmanaged pane. (3) The pass-4 `await_job` indeterminate-probe fix had no regression test; it now has one proving the probe neither aborts the dispatch nor spins past its deadline. Each of these three was confirmed red-before-green by reverting the fix and re-running. A SIXTH pass found three more High plus two Medium and a Low, all fixed here: (1) `spawned` only flipped after a rc=0 `new_session`, so a PARTIAL spawn read as "nothing was created" — a backend's `new_session` is not atomic (herdr splits, renames, then runs before returning; tmux's own can time out after the server accepted it), so a nonzero return now means creation is INDETERMINATE and teardown is attempted regardless, freeing the permit only on confirmation. (2) The pass-5 permit hold was itself unreclaimable: `_release_permit` looks the context manager up by `record.session_name`, but on an unreadable-`pane_pid` failure no `JobRecord` was ever persisted, `reap()` only REPORTS unknown sessions, and the quota stale-reaper deliberately spares a token whose holder pid is alive — two such failures would have pinned both Claude pool slots until the process exited; a quarantined residue record (with `pane_pid`/`pane_pgid` stamped 0, which every kill path refuses as pid ≤ 1) now makes the slot reclaimable through the ordinary machinery. (3) At the deadline, `_kill_job()` can return False and a concurrently-written valid sentinel then win → `completed_with_residue`, but that `_finish` omitted `release_permit=` and took the default True, freeing the slot while residue may still have been executing. Also: `kill_session` forwarded the ordinary list-then-close disappearance race (a structured `pane_not_found` after the pane auto-closed on exit) as a teardown FAILURE, making the supervisor hold a permit for a pane that verifiably no longer exists; `CAPABILITY_REVISION` is bumped 1→2 for the `claude-opus-5` row (the file's own contract says bump on any edit, and the row flips that model from refused to supported — the `hermes-agent` additive precedent does not cover it); and `usage_capture`'s rate card gains `claude-opus-5` so its cost is not silently estimated at zero. One correction worth recording: the first draft of the deadline test passed against pre-fix code — a false green, because a sentinel present from the first poll takes the top-of-loop branch that already gated correctly; it now forces the sentinel to appear only at the deadline re-check and asserts that branch was reached. Every pass-6 fix was then confirmed red-before-green. A SEVENTH pass then found that two of the pass-6 fixes were themselves regressions, and the accumulated pattern finally named the ROOT CAUSE — so this round is a refactor of the seam rather than another patch. The structural fault: every `TerminalBackend` probe returned a `CompletedProcess` whose `returncode` conflates "I confirmed it is gone" with "I could not tell", so every call site had to guess, and each guess-site fixed in isolation created a new wrong guess adjacent to it — which is precisely why seven consecutive passes each found a fresh instance. The seam now speaks a TRI-STATE `Liveness` enum (`CONFIRMED_ALIVE` / `CONFIRMED_GONE` / `INDETERMINATE`) via `probe_session`/`close_session`/`enumerate_sessions`; each backend classifies its OWN errors (tmux parses its stderr, herdr maps its structured codes and the `_PaneListError`/`_PaneGetError` raises), and the supervisor branches on the enum instead of re-deriving meaning from a returncode. The tmux classifier is built from OBSERVED output of the pinned binary, including one trap that inference would have gotten backwards: `error connecting to <sock>` splits on its PARENTHETICAL — `(No such file or directory)` means no socket, hence no server, hence the session verifiably does not exist, while `(Permission denied)` means we could not look at all. Same prefix, opposite verdicts; anything unrecognised fails SAFE to `INDETERMINATE`. The three pass-7 findings this closes: (1) marking every attempted spawn indeterminate (the pass-6 fix) meant an ORDINARY tmux spawn refusal — whose `kill-session` exits nonzero with an absence message — read as an unconfirmed teardown and PINNED its quota permit, so two routine refusals could exhaust the pool; `CONFIRMED_GONE` now covers the already-absent case. (2) The pass-6 residue record's `pane_pid=0` made every pid test vacuously report "dead", so `_kill_job` returned True and `cancel()`/reap's `retain_worktree` released the permit for a possibly-live pane — the record now carries an EXPLICIT additive `JobRecord.identity_unknown` flag (not an inferred pid sentinel: `pane_pid=1` is a long-standing idiom in this suite for "verifies dead immediately", and keying on `<= 1` changed behaviour for ~20 unrelated tests), and for such records the ONLY acceptable death evidence is a backend-confirmed teardown. (3) tmux liveness had the same operational-nonzero-as-absence conflation already fixed on the herdr side — a LIVE server behind a socket permission error read as dead, releasing its permit without kill verification or dropping the record out of reap's `live_names` so its live process entered `kill_tree`. Also closed: the `claude-opus-5` rate-card row had no test (so deleting it would not have failed the suite) — the rate card is now pinned to the shipped routing table itself, and a stale comment claiming the revision pins expect 1 is corrected. Six unfaithful test fixtures were fixed rather than the classifier weakened: they injected a bare nonzero with EMPTY stderr (or an invented "no session" string), which real tmux never emits and which correctly classifies as INDETERMINATE. Verified against the REAL tmux binary on a private socket: no-server probe/close/enumerate all `confirmed_gone`, a live session `confirmed_alive`, a ghost `confirmed_gone`. Every pass-7 fix confirmed red-before-green with the revert itself assertion-guarded, after a pass-6 test turned out to be a false green. An EIGHTH pass then found the tri-state had fixed "couldn't query vs confirmed absent" but RELOCATED the guess one level down, to "session absent therefore process tree dead" — which is false, because the adapter starts the provider with `start_new_session` in its OWN process group, so a pane can be legitimately gone while the provider survives. Eight passes had by then yielded a flat finding count (4H / 4 / 3H / 2H+2M / 2H+1M / 3H+2M+1L / 3H+1L / 3H+2M+1L) with no convergence, and THREE of pass-8's six findings traced to a single thing invented in pass 6 — the launch-failure residue record. Owner scope decision (2026-07-24): scope this PR back to its actual ACs. **The residue record is REVERTED** (removing its recovery-relaunch erasure, its never-released `keep` tier, its lack of crash durability, and the backward-compat hazard where a record persisted by an earlier commit in this same branch would have loaded with the safety flag defaulting False). The launch-failure permit question is now a **documented KNOWN LIMITATION** in `terminal_backend.py`: the slot is released, matching pre-#638 behaviour and unchanged for tmux, with reap()'s CF-11 unknown-session reporting surfacing any pane live on the endpoint but absent from the registry. The remaining invariant — a permit may only be released on proof the process TREE is dead — is #467-era supervisor machinery that predates this issue and is filed as **#648** (which also absorbs #647), design-first, because four successive cheap substitutes each produced a new defect class. Two pass-8 findings WERE in scope and are fixed: the tmux classifier missed tmux 3.4's exact `no sessions` diagnostic (confirmed present in the installed binary via `strings`; reachable when a user's config sets `exit-empty off`), now matched with an anchored pattern that still rejects `no sessions available for the widget`; and the migration hazard vanished with the revert. Correction to an earlier claim in this entry: the unfaithful-fixture repairs number SEVEN, not six (five in `test_supervisor_lifecycle.py`, two in `test_supervisor_recover.py`), and they are fidelity repairs — they would pass against pre-refactor code too, so they are NOT red-before-green evidence and were wrong to imply otherwise. A NINTH (deliberately narrow) pass then found four more, all in scope and all mine: (1) **HIGH, a destructive regression** — pass 6's `spawned = True` placed BEFORE `new_session` meant tmux's ordinary `duplicate session: <name>` refusal (confirmed in the installed binary and reproduced live) sent the cleanup path to `kill-session` on the ALREADY-EXISTING same-name session, since session names are deterministic; it destroyed something the launch never created, and the accompanying "never blind-killed" comment was simply false. `spawned` now flips only after a CONFIRMED-successful `new_session` (origin/main's behaviour), which turned out to be both correct and sufficient — each backend already cleans up its own partial spawn (`HerdrBackend` closes the pane it created if rename/run fails; tmux's `new-session` is atomic), so the supervisor never needed to guess from a returncode at all. Verified live: the pre-existing session survives the refusal. (2) **HIGH** — the `no sessions` pattern used `re.M`, making it LINE-anchored, so `"Permission denied\nno sessions"` classified as CONFIRMED_GONE and recreated the very operational-failure-as-death class the tri-state exists to eliminate; it now takes a whole-message match, operational indicators take explicit PRECEDENCE over any co-occurring absence phrase, and each stream is examined separately (concatenating stderr+stdout had let a mixed pair match). (3) **MEDIUM** — the pass-8 revert deleted `test_await_job_indeterminate_probe_does_not_abort_and_is_bounded`, which predated the residue record and guards retained behaviour; restored. (4) **LOW** — comments still claiming an unconfirmed teardown holds the permit, and `CONFIRMED_GONE`'s "safe to treat as dead", both contradicted the documented KNOWN LIMITATION; corrected, with `CONFIRMED_GONE` now stating plainly that it is evidence about the NAMESPACE only. Also collapsed a two-armed permit release whose arms did the same thing — a self-review catch, since that shape invites a later "fix" to one arm and silently restores the p6–p8 defect chain. All six pass-9 regressions confirmed red-before-green. A TENTH pass found two more, both consequences of pass 9: (1) with `split`+`rename` succeeded and `pane run` failing, `HerdrBackend`'s own close became the ONLY cleanup — pass 9 had correctly stopped the supervisor's second, NAME-based attempt, since that path was what let a `duplicate session` refusal destroy a pre-existing session — so a single failed close leaked a labeled pane with a possibly-live payload; cleanup now RETRIES and then VERIFIES via `pane get`, addressed by `pane_id` (safe in a way retrying by name never was, because the id can only reach the pane this call created). (2) The pass-9 classifier paired substring absence-searches with a DENYLIST of operational phrases, which could never be airtight — `failed to send command` is a real tmux diagnostic that was not on it, so pairing it with an absence phrase still classified CONFIRMED_GONE; inverted to a whole-message ALLOWLIST where every non-empty stream must match a known absence diagnostic IN FULL, so an unenumerated message can only ever make the result indeterminate. Both confirmed red-before-green. Accuracy note: of the pass-9 additions, the retargeted post-spawn test and the restored `await_job` test are complementary coverage rather than red-before-green evidence (each passes against the pre-fix code) — only the refusal, duplicate-session, and classifier tests distinguish it. A self-audit of the new allowlist (run before the next reviewer's report arrived) then found a fault in the opposite direction: the variable parts used `\S+`, so an ordinary absence message whose SESSION NAME or SOCKET PATH contains a space — `can't find session: has space`, `no server running on /tmp/tm x/s p.sock`, both produced by the real binary in a live probe — failed to whole-match and classified INDETERMINATE, which holds quota permits and excludes records from the sweep indefinitely. Widened to `.*`, which still cannot whole-match a multi-line/mixed body because `.` excludes newlines; confirmed red-before-green, and pinned with the live-probed strings as the fixture data. The ELEVENTH pass's sole blocker was exactly that whitespace bug — it reviewed the parent commit, so it was already fixed, and the pass independently confirmed the reachability concern and reported **no second tmux-only regression**, which is this PR's merge bar. Its two non-blocking notes are also addressed: cleanup calls now use a 5s leash rather than the caller's 30s (3 attempts × close+verify put the worst case near 180s of cleanup on a path that had already failed with the caller blocked on it), and its exact space-bearing examples are pinned through the classifier AND all three backend surfaces. Left as a follow-up, out of scope here (filed as **#647**): the read-only run-status surface (`executor_routing_lib.py`) still probes tmux unconditionally, ignoring `record.terminal_backend`. 160 new tests. **Also in this PR — owner seat-table retune (2026-07-24):** `claude-opus-4-8` is retired from the shipped routing table in favour of `claude-opus-5` everywhere it appeared; `intake`/`analysis`/`plan` primaries move to `claude-opus-5`, `intake` and `analysis` effort rises medium→high, and the `analysis`/`plan` fallback chains normalise to `claude-fable-5 → claude-sonnet-5` (dropping `plan`'s `gpt-5.6-terra` tail). `design`/`build`/`review`/`ship`/`offload` primaries are unchanged. Downstream surfaces the table feeds, all updated in step: `model_capabilities.SUPPORTED_EFFORT` gains a `claude-opus-5` row and `CAPABILITY_REVISION` bumps 1→2 with it (the pass-6 review was right that the `hermes-agent` additive precedent does not cover this: the row flips `claude-opus-5` from unknown/refused to supported, so leaving the revision at 1 would make the old and new capability sets indistinguishable in every effort Observation that records it; the retired `claude-opus-4-8` row is KEPT because that registry is a documented superset and a persisted Observation minted against the old id must still resolve), `bakeoff_policy`'s `DESIGN_MODELS`/`BUILD_MODELS`/`INCUMBENT_MODEL` (a drift guard pins `DESIGN_MODELS` to the design seat's own model set, and `_lane_for_model` refuses any model absent from the table), the 8 driver-bench fixtures that encode expected seat models, and the generated `DATA.seatRouting` block. The 25 drift-guard failures the table edit produced were the red-before-green evidence that those guards genuinely pin the values. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV (`docs/workflow-diagram.html` changed only in its GENERATED `seatRouting` data — config digest + model lists — via `hooks/diagram_seat_data.py write`, no station or step edits). Suite 4963→5128.
### v3.96.0 (2026-07-24)
- **`WorktreeManager.park_and_reset` — the design-level loop-back park-then-reset contract, plus a `RoutingAuditLog.append_park` audit variant (#637, epic #635 C4).** New primitive in `phase_executor/src/phase_executor/worktree.py`: park a build-seat worktree's own uncommitted diff (tracked + untracked) recoverably before resetting it to `base_sha` on a WF2/WF3 design-level loop-back (Step 4→3 / Step 8→3) — never destroys uncommitted work without a named, recoverable stash first. Two Step-8a review waves (4 passes) found and fixed a concurrent-park race (verified via OID capture) and a post-park-failure audit gap (new `ParkThenResetError`, carrying the confirmed `ParkRecord` so a reset/clean failure after a successful park never loses the recovery reference). The Step-11 final review then went three rounds deeper, each an independently-confirmed real finding, not a rubber-stamp: (1) the OID-capture mechanism itself compared `refs/stash`'s tip before/after `stash push` — but `refs/stash` is a repository-GLOBAL ref shared by every linked worktree of one canonical repo (confirmed empirically with two real `git worktree add` checkouts), so a concurrent park in another worktree could misattribute a foreign OID; fixed by resolving the call's own commit via a `refs/stash` reflog MESSAGE search instead of a TIP read. (2) That fix's uniqueness key (`stash_name`, built from run_id+design_version+task_id alone) could still collide across two *different* worktrees under a competitive/bake-off dispatch running several attempts of one task concurrently; fixed by folding `handle.identity.attempt` into the actual push message. (3) `attempt` itself turned out to be an unsafe anchor — a plain caller-supplied string with no enforced uniqueness invariant, only unique by convention at its one current `uuid4()`-based minting call site; the final fix anchors on a hash of `handle.gitdir` instead, git's own linked-worktree admin-directory path, which git itself guarantees is distinct for every live linked worktree of one canonical repo. `RoutingAuditLog.append_park` (new `"park"` audit kind in `enforce.py`) additionally enforces `parked=True ⟹ non-empty stash_oid` / `parked=False ⟹ stash_oid=None` at the WRITER, not just the reader (a Step-11 completeness finding — the reader stays backward-compatible with a pre-existing record missing the key entirely). 25 new tests across `test_worktree_park.py`/`test_enforce.py`, including two-real-linked-worktree race regressions for each of the three Step-11 rounds. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4938→4963.
### v3.95.0 (2026-07-24)
- **`TerminalBackend` protocol + `TmuxBackend` — injected launch/liveness/enumerate/close seam for the build-seat supervisor, pure refactor (#636, epic #635 C1).** Extracted the tmux-specific primitives inlined in `TmuxSupervisor` (`phase_executor/src/phase_executor/supervisor.py`) into a new `phase_executor/src/phase_executor/terminal_backend.py`: a `TerminalBackend` Protocol (preflight/new_session/pane_pid/has_session/list_sessions/kill_session/resolve_endpoint/teardown_endpoint) and a default `TmuxBackend` implementing it with the EXACT current tmux invocations (byte-identical argv, confirmed against every cited call site). `TmuxSupervisor.__init__` gained an optional `backend` param; absent, it default-constructs `TmuxBackend` from the same `run`/`env`/`runtime_dir`/`state_dir` it already threaded — byte-identical to before for all 3 real construction sites in `hooks/executor_routing_lib.py`, none of which pass `backend=`. Backend-agnostic security machinery (the two-group + descendant-snapshot `os.killpg` kill, `read_sentinel`/`observation.json` status derivation, `recover`/`reap` verdict logic) is untouched. `resolve_endpoint`/`teardown_endpoint` are named backend-agnostically (not `resolve_socket`/`kill_server`) because a future backend's addressing model may not be a socket path — herdr's is workspace+pane ids, confirmed against `docs/planning/2026-07-23-633-herdr-build-seat-launch-backend-design.md`'s own primitive-mapping table, which also surfaced a Step-11 review finding (High, confirmed against that same design doc): herdr's `pane split` assigns its own opaque pane id rather than accepting a caller-chosen one, so the protocol now documents an explicit name-ownership contract — a backend whose native primitive assigns its own id must maintain its own internal name-to-native-id mapping, kept inside that backend, never added to the protocol surface or the job registry schema. Zero test file changes needed for the existing supervisor test suite — the extraction is fully transparent to the injected `run=` fakes already in use; Step 8a review (2 independent dispatches, mechanical + security lens, after an initial pair that legitimately timed out mid-suite-rerun rather than passing cleanly) found and closed one real test-coverage gap (preflight's runner raising, and launch's post-spawn-failure kill+permit-release+re-raise path were both previously untested). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4924→4938.
### v3.94.5 (2026-07-24)
- **Executor-seat dispatch is no longer denied its own project's files in multi-project workspaces (#640).** Root cause, found live during epic #635's WF2 Step 2 for #636: an executor-dispatched subprocess (`hooks/executor_routing_lib.py dispatch`, via `phase_executor`'s `claude_cli` adapter) is a fresh, unregistered `claude --print --no-session-persistence` session, never written to `claude_docs/session_registry.jsonl` — so `wal-bind-guard`'s Gate 1 treated it as unbound and denied every Read/Edit/Write under a project directory whenever more than one project was active (close to always in this workspace), burning the dispatch's full budget on guaranteed denials before failing. An independent codex review of the first draft (a bare `RAWGENTIC_DISPATCH_PROJECT=<name>` env var, trusted by string equality against the active-project list) correctly flagged it as forgeable ambient authority with no provenance check — the owner chose to harden before merging. Shipped design: `claude_cli.py`'s `run()` writes a `dispatch-claim.json` marker *inside its own capture directory* (already 0700, already rooted under the dispatching project's own `.rawgentic/runs/` tree by construction — `Capture.write_dispatch_claim`) and sets `RAWGENTIC_DISPATCH_CLAIM=<path>` on the subprocess env; `wal-bind-guard` trusts it by **path containment** (realpath-canonicalized, symlink/`../`-escape-proof, prefix-collision-proof) against each active project's own run tree — never by trusting the env value as a string — so only code that can already write into that specific project's capture tree can produce a valid claim. This anchors on `capture_root`, a parameter every dispatch mechanism already threads, rather than a new bespoke field — confirmed by reading `supervisor.py:484` (the tmux resume path threads the identical project-scoped `capture_root` into its pane spec) and `engine.py`'s bake-off `_run_candidate` (same), so the fix covers the sync, resume, and competitive dispatch paths through one shared choke point with no additional plumbing in any of them. Found and flagged, but deliberately left unfixed (out of scope, tracked separately): an unrelated pre-existing quirk where Gate 1's "workspace-level file" exception already allows an unbound session to touch a file under an *inactive* project's own directory, regardless of this fix. Re-pinned `canary.EXPECTED_REGISTRATION_DIGEST` (wal-bind-guard is a referenced enforcer script). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4908→4924.
### v3.94.4 (2026-07-23)
- **herdr build-seat launch-backend investigation — GO verdict, design doc (#633).** Live-qualified `herdr pane run <pane> exec <argv>` PID-identity preservation against pinned herdr 0.7.5: 40/40 clean (20 cold-pane + 20 reused-pane reps, fixed GO threshold of 0/20 failures per condition set before running) using a two-phase sentinel worker (closer to `pane_runner`'s real shape than `sleep`). New finding: the pane auto-closes the instant the exec'd process exits (normal or killed) — confirmed to match `supervisor.py`'s existing tmux design exactly (`new-session -- argv` already runs the worker directly with no shell, and completion status already comes from `read_sentinel()`'s disk-based `observation.json`, never from tmux/herdr state). Primitive selection: raw `pane run exec` (not `agent start`/`agent prompt` — the build-seat worker isn't a recognized agent kind, `agent_status` reads `unknown` throughout), which makes AC2 (`agent_prompt_stalled`) N/A. Executed (not paper-only) the full tmux→herdr primitive mapping for `new-session`/`display-message`/`has-session`/`list-sessions`/`kill-session` — `pane close` confirmed to actually terminate a live worker process, not just close the UI, and is idempotent on an already-gone/never-existed pane ID. Resolved the worktree-diff-contract hard precondition for the build seat's context-reuse policy: park (git stash, named + audited) then reset the isolated worktree on a design-level loop-back, never destroy-in-place. Design doc: `docs/planning/2026-07-23-633-herdr-build-seat-launch-backend-design.md` (+ `.html`). Named risks (exec-injection is herdr-undocumented, two-call create+launch vs tmux's one atomic call, host-wide singleton daemon) carried into the follow-up implementation issue. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4908→4908 (version-surface pin updates only, no test additions).

### v3.94.1 (2026-07-23)

New public `step_state.read_history()` replaces a duplicated read-loop. Design gate loop-back 1/1: the first fix draft (warning-only) was correctly rejected by both the Step-4 self-review and an opt-in adversarial review as not actually fixing timing population; the adversarial review also caught an unverified "epic #509 proves it" claim copied from the issue body, corrected against the primary source. ### v3.93.0 (2026-07-23)

New workspace top-level `defaultArchitecture` key: absent = `executor` (THE flip — no config anywhere means executor); `legacy` = the manual JOINT rollback (owner + operator together) routing every seat back to the Agent-tool path; any other value refuses fail-closed. A run is 100% executor or 100% legacy, never mixed: new `begin-run` CLI verb declares the architecture at run start in the run ledger's `initial` record (idempotent on (architecture, config_digest)); `dispatch`/`recover-run`/`close-run` CONSUME the pin (undeclared → `run_not_declared`, mixed → `mixed_architecture_run_refused`, exit 4; the lazy seeds are gone; the assertion runs inside the flock-held `append_expected`). NO runtime auto-fallback — an executor failure follows the ERROR protocol, never the Agent tool (#417 made absolute); per-project `executorRouting` seat modes no longer select the tier (a contradicting mode refuses naming the seat). Legacy kept in-tree as the rollback target (owner deviation from "removed"): bundled agents gain a first-instruction architecture SELF-CHECK; dispatch prose architecture-conditioned across WF2/WF3 with new corpus pins; run-record gains a version-gated required `architecture` field + an advisory non-primary-dispatch warn; rollback procedure documented in config-reference.
