### v3.89.0 (2026-07-22)
- **Fresh session per child in the epic auto-run — opt-in process boundary (#569).** `epic-run` currently runs "WF2 fresh per child" but all in ONE Claude process, so context accumulates across an epic's children; this adds an opt-in **fresh-session mode** (`.driver-state` `session_mode: "fresh-session"`, chosen at epic-run Step 2; absent → `single-session`, byte-identical default) where each child runs in its own `claude` process with continuity carried by durable state alone. Three pure `hooks/driver_lib.py` helpers: `fresh_session_handoff(state, *, mode)` returns an explicit disposition `{single_session|ready|complete|blocked}` — **never a `None` sentinel**, `complete` fires ONLY when every child is `merged`, and `blocked` (unmerged children remain but none ready) keeps the epic OPEN (never conflated with complete); `fresh_session_available(...)` is the pre-launch fail-open check; `handoff_claim(state, generation)` is the idempotent exactly-one-successor claim. The session ends on ANY terminal child outcome (merged/deferred/abandoned — a blocked child's context must not bleed into an independent successor); the durable `long-run-resume` launcher relaunches the successor with **NO `--resume`** (a `--resume` would reload the prior transcript and defeat the fresh context) and the successor atomically claims the pending handoff under the launcher flock; an unclaimed handoff on a later fire is takeover-failure, surfaced via escalation. Fail-open throughout: an unavailable boundary degrades to the single-session loop with a visible marker, never aborts. Contract documented in `skills/epic-run/SKILL.md` (Step 2 + Step 4) and `docs/multi-issue-driver.md`; the driver still structurally cannot weaken a WF2 gate. The `epic475-resume.sh`/`long-run-resume` launcher edit that skips `--resume` in fresh-session mode + the live cross-session transcript check are deferred owner-attended follow-ups (outside the plugin repo; #568-Phase-1 launcher-glue precedent). Red-first, +22 tests (`test_driver_lib.py`, `test_epic_run_clarity.py`, `test_multi_issue_driver.py`), incl. Step-11 handoff-hardening remediation. No WF2-spine change (epic-run is the driver, not a WF2 station) → no diagram REV. Suite 4519→4541.

### v3.88.0 (2026-07-22)
- **Hermes offload seat + numbered-option asks + unattended fallback policy — #568 Phase-2 (epic #475-adjacent).** Adds a read-only `offload` executor seat backed by the EXISTING darwin Hermes gateway's OpenAI-compatible `/v1/runs` HTTP API (new `hermes` engine adapter, `phase_executor/adapters/hermes_http.py`; provider `nousresearch`, model id `hermes-agent`), plus two owner-comms primitives on the Phase-1 bridge. The seat ships **contract-correct and CI-tested but ACTIVATION-GATED**: its adapter refuses to dispatch unless the gateway reports a confirmably sandboxed backend (fail-closed on `local`/absent), because `tool_grants:["read"]` is caller-side selection, not a gateway sandbox — so on the current unsandboxed darwin config every dispatch degrades chain-aware to the `analysis` lane (claude-sonnet-5), audited via `fallback_reason`, never silent. Identity is attested from the gateway `/health` platform field (`verify_post` verifies with no contract change); absent gateway usage → the existing `USAGE_UNAVAILABLE` status (never a fabricated zero or a new closed-schema field); the Hermes failure taxonomy maps to existing `parse_status`/`AVAILABILITY_FAILURES`. `hooks/hermes_bridge.py` gains numbered-option asks — `validate_options` fail-closed at creation (unique ids + normalized labels), strict `interpret_reply` (digit / unique-label / `N: label` both-agree → selected; else ambiguous or `unmatched_option` under `option_required`), a durable ≤1 clarification marker — fully backward-compatible (an optionless ask is byte-identical to Phase-1). New pure `hooks/hermes_policy.py` decides the unattended fallback table (gateway-down / delivery-uncertain / bb-read-down / reply / terminal-substitution) with enumerated outputs and two property-tested invariants (no `remote_reply_allowed==false` resume-from-text; no critical ask silently continues). Blind peer consult (Codex gpt-5.6-sol) + a Step-4 adversarial design gate (24 findings, one `design` loop-back r2→r3, scope narrowed to activation-gated seat + primitives). The authenticated end-to-end live cell is deferred (`@pytest.mark.live`, #138) behind the owner sandbox precondition (`docs/hermes-offload.md`). Version ×4 + phase_executor 0.8.0→0.9.0. Red-first, +61 tests (adapter/bridge/policy). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV (DATA.seatRouting digest regenerated for the new seat). Suite 4447→4519.

### v3.87.0 (2026-07-22)
- **Hermes owner-reply bridge — Phase 1, inbound capture (#568).** An unattended harness run can ask the owner a question by text and have the reply captured, correlated, and recorded in a durable inbox for the resume launcher to consume — this PR ships the inbox contract + `render_resume_prompt`, **not** an end-to-end unattended resume (the launcher wiring that feeds the inbox into `claude -p --resume` lives in a workspace launcher outside this repo and is a documented follow-up). New `hooks/hermes_bridge.py` **taps the existing BlueBubbles message store** via its read API (`POST /api/v1/message/query`, idempotent — confirmed by a live probe) rather than rebuilding the Hermes gateway; the AC1 investigation confirmed the gateway already relays inbound (webhook→skills) but not to a harness-readable sink. **Token-only EXACT correlation** (bracketed `[RG-…]`); a **two-store never-lose crash model** (`observed.jsonl` never gates delivery, `consumed.jsonl` is the sole gate, written after the durable inbox file — a crash re-delivers); ambiguity / no-token / unreachable / timeout all deliver nothing (**never wrong-act**); inbound text is untrusted **DATA, never executed**; secrets by name (password in a curl `-K` file, redacted from logs+tracebacks); write-once files via `O_EXCL` create-if-absent + `atomic_write_lib`. Outbound reuses `sentinel/bin/notify.sh` unchanged; delivery uses the existing `claude -p --resume` launcher pattern (`render_resume_prompt`, no bespoke session injection). Blind peer consult (Codex) + a Step-4 adversarial design gate (1 Critical + 3 High, one `design` loop-back) + a Step-8a wave (HIGH `parents[3]` path bug that silently disabled default outbound) + a cross-model Step-11 diff review (Codex) that caught 3 more Critical/High — an **atomic no-clobber inbox install** (a crash mid-write can no longer strand an empty file that replay treats as delivered), **token-closure** so a second reply to a closed ask is `late` not a double-action, and **bounded backlog-stop pagination** — all fixed. **Phase 2+ (offload seat) deferred to a design-doc/WF1 epic; AC4's genuine-human live round-trip deferred (owner away) — the full path is proven mechanically via a simulated reply.** Red-first, +50 tests (`tests/hooks/test_hermes_bridge.py`; fake transport/notify, no live creds in CI). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4397→4447.

### v3.86.0 (2026-07-21)
- **End-to-end proving-run hardening: recovery-dispatch chokepoint + H1/H2/H3/F1 fixes + AC1/AC2a plumbing (#559, epic #560, H6).** Seven security-hardening tasks ship test-first on the wired executor path: H1 `mark_quota_paused` gets an explicit guard order with a **post-kill sentinel re-read** (the check/kill race finalizes `completed_with_residue`, never a false `quota_paused`; permit released only on a verified kill); H2 `_relaunch` reads digest-verified spec bytes, closing the post-identity-check TOCTOU window (tamper → quarantine); H3 unknown/seatless competitive seats fail loud with no carve-out; F1 the behavioral probe parses OS-denial evidence in memory (raw ephemeral, advisory-only — never flips a verdict, no PII); the **recovery-dispatch chokepoint** `Supervisor.recover(*, dispatch_gate)` (mandatory gate, prelaunch-before-receipt, mutating-recovery refused, `RecoveryAuthorization` target/config-digest bound, recovery grouped under the original expected key); the AC1 `promote_appendix_only` traversal-hardened path policy + `work_product` audit kind + two-phase idempotent `collect-work-product`; and the AC2a `probe-account` identity observation (digest+categories, no PII) + `dispatch --resume-session-id` (claude-only, non-mutating, session-preservation asserted). A two-reviewer Step-8a wave found no Critical/High; L3 (account-digest delimiter collision) fixed by JSON-encoding the identity fields; 5 pre-existing lint errors fixed. **OPS live cells DEFERRED** (owner-gated account switch, owner away) → ships **`Part of #559` / `Part of #560`**, both issues stay OPEN for an owner-attended live pass; the proving-run report + repeatability appendix are committed (`docs/planning/2026-07-21-559-proving-run-report.md`) and published as an Artifact. Confirmed engine gaps filed as #570 (work_product reconcile binding + crash-window durability). Red-first throughout (+56 tests). phase_executor 0.7.0→0.8.0. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4340→4396.

### v3.85.0 (2026-07-21)
- **Genuine quota detection + per-call caps + classifier persistence (#558, epic #560, H5).** A claude NONZERO_EXIT collection is now classified by a new pure conjunctive classifier (`phase_executor/quota_detect.py`: engine==claude AND exit==1 AND usage-limit language AND temporal reset/retry language — "rate limit"/"upgrade" deliberately negative; per-line casefold matching, digest-pinned v1 rule table, 8 MiB bounded stderr evidence, no raw provider text persisted). `await_job` kills-then-reads (stable dead-writer evidence), gates auto-pause behind two-layer guards (verified spec → read-only → resume-policy → `CALIBRATED_CLASSIFIERS` allowlist → common → kill-verified) returning a deterministic single-reason `PauseDecision`; the allowlist **ships EMPTY (owner D-12: shadow mode)** — every verdict persists as `JobRecord.quota_classification` (additive serde, `RegistryCorrupt` on malformed, relaunch passthrough) but nothing auto-pauses until #559 calibrates a genuine capture. Per-call caps (AC-B4 as amended D-13): `bounds.max_budget_usd` on every claude-bearing seat (analysis/ship 2.0, review/plan/intake/design 5.0, build 10.0), `bounds.max_tokens` schema field + zhipu adapter boundary (default 1024 preserved), claude budget composition boundary, manifest profiles + min-timeout now threaded on the sync/competitive paths, and a MUTATING manifest on those paths rejects fail-loud (build bake-off/sync dispatch documented unavailable until a capped mutating composition exists); codex's compensating bound is the enforced timeout. Red-first throughout (9-fixture corpus + MANIFEST, 24 classifier + 20 supervisor/engine/adapter tests, +7 review-wave hardening tests — read_error short-circuit, envelope unicode safety, session-id bounds, verified-spec-first discriminator, supervisor await-deadline clamp, oversized-envelope resume-assert fallback). phase_executor 0.6.0→0.7.0. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV (seat-data block regenerated for the new bounds). Suite 4263→4340.

### v3.84.0 (2026-07-21)
- **Behavioral codex containment canary — a sandboxed-child write probe (#556, epic #560, H3).** The codex containment canary was composition-validation ONLY (`_check_codex_containment` re-checks argv/roots); it never proved the sandbox actually *confines* a write, so #472's AC2 ("confined, negative-control passed") was unproven. New `codex_behavioral` canary check + a `codex_behavioral_probe` (`hooks/executor_routing_lib.py`) that, before a real codex MUTATING launch, runs the exact mutating composition in a throwaway worktree and externally verifies an in-worktree write LANDED and an out-of-worktree sibling write was BLOCKED; `require_canary`'s `codex_mutating` policy now requires it (`[codex_containment, codex_behavioral, bare_absent]`). Wired into `supervised_dispatch` STEP 4b as an injected seam gated by `behavioral_needed` (distinct from the claude positive-deny probe stream via `probe_needed`); fail-closed — a missing seam, a probe exception, or a not-both-true result refuses (exit 6) before any spawn. AC2: fixed the broken `test_canary_live.py` (`CanaryEvidence` was missing `codex_containment_root`). AC3: a RUN_LIVE cell proves the gate end-to-end on a codex host (skip-gated where codex is absent). **Known limitation (deferred to #559):** `outside_blocked` is inferred from the sibling's absence — the positive-denial-token calibration against real codex output lands in #559's e2e proving run. Red-first: 7 canary + 4 supervised behavioral tests. phase_executor 0.5.0→0.6.0. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4248→4263.

### v3.83.0 (2026-07-21)
- **Ledger-aware dispatch chokepoint + expected-call ledger + reconcile verb (#555, epic #560, H2).** The dispatch entry point was ledger-blind — it accepted `--run-id` but never consulted an expected-call ledger nor rejected a dispatch after a "final" reconcile, so "zero uninstrumented dispatch" (#472 AC1) was not enforceable at the chokepoint. New `phase_executor/ledger.py` `ExpectedCallLedger` writes an append-only `<capture_root>/<run_id>/expected-calls.jsonl` (`initial` → per-call `expected` → terminal `run_closed`), hardened fail-closed: `O_NOFOLLOW` leaf, atomic read-check-append under an exclusive `flock`, byte + record caps, run_id equality, first-must-be-`initial`, `run_closed`-last-and-once, no post-close append, duplicate `(seat,correlation_id)` refusal (primary containment is worktree isolation; the file hardening is defense-in-depth). `_do_dispatch` now seeds the ledger, REFUSES a dispatch when the ledger is `run_closed` (exit 4, before any spawn/audit append) and appends the expected call append-before-dispatch; a keyless dispatch is refused. New `close-run` verb (terminal marker) and `reconcile --run-id --mode provisional|final` verb (`provisional` tolerates an open ledger + in-flight calls, failing only on a hard breach; `final` requires `run_closed` last AND zero anomalies; exit 0/1/2, new `EXIT_ANOMALY=1`). The ledger dir is colocated with the audit via the same `sanitize_component`. Red-before-green: parser hardening (symlink/caps/dup/post-close/run-id/first-record), chokepoint post-close refusal, and the verb modes/exit-codes; full `reconcile_run` binding rides `tests/phase_executor/test_enforce.py`. phase_executor 0.4.0→0.5.0. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4226→4248.

### v3.82.0 (2026-07-21)
- **Reconcile pause/recover model — a resumed call reconciles as one authorized attempt (#554, epic #560, H1).** `reconcile_run` had no model for a quota_paused→recovered call: the original attempt was left with an availability-only Observation (marked unsatisfied) and a fresh recovery `correlation_id` landed as an orphan, so a genuine pause→recover cycle could never reach a zero-anomaly final reconcile (`enforce.py`). Now a recovery attempt keys to its ORIGINAL expected call via an effective key `(seat, recovered_from or correlation_id)` — the orphan checks and per-key grouping use it, so an original attempt + its recoveries reconcile as ONE call: the recovery's verified Observation satisfies it, the original's pause Observation is the forgivable availability failure. `supervisor._relaunch` records recovery provenance (its own `correlation_id` `<origin>#resume<n>` + `recovered_from` = the original; a re-recovery inherits the root origin), and `registry.JobRecord` gains a serialized `recovered_from` field. The no-laundering guarantee is preserved (a breach in either attempt still forces `unverified`; a pause with no verified recovery still refuses; an unknown recovery key fails closed as an orphan; a non-string `recovered_from` is a structured anomaly, not a crash). Red-before-green at the `reconcile_run` unit level plus a tmux provenance round-trip test. **Scope:** this ships the reconcile MODEL + durable provenance; emitting `recovered_from` onto the audit receipt (`check_pre`/`PreReceipt`) and wiring a recovered attempt into the `RoutingAuditLog` is the #555 ledger chokepoint (`reconcile_run` has no live caller until #420) — the #555 PR that feeds real receipts must re-run the #554 laundering battery. phase_executor 0.3.0→0.4.0. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4217→4226.

### v3.81.0 (2026-07-21)
- **Reconcilable failure Observation for every terminal supervised state (#557, epic #560, H4).** A supervised dispatch that died without a sentinel returned `(exited_no_sentinel, None)`, leaving a receipt with no bindable Observation, so `reconcile_run` reported a missing pair — the failure that most needs auditing was the one lost (surfaced by the #472 proving run). `supervisor.await_job` now emits the supervisor's synthetic Observation (`parse_status=no_response`, `correlation_id` + `routing_config_digest` from the spec) on that path too, atomic-written to the capture dir with the same ordering and 0700 posture as the timeout path; `mark_quota_paused`'s completion guard now keys on the sentinel's SHAPE (availability-failure = recorded failure, resumable; envelope-producing = effectful completion, protected) instead of mere existence, so an `exited_no_sentinel` job with its new synthetic obs is still quota-pausable. `executor_routing_lib` STEP 6.5 comment updated (its obs consumer was already generic). Red-before-green: the synthetic-obs asserts, the `verify_post` no_response classification, and the reconcile recorded-failure-not-missing-pair pin (incl. "cannot be laundered by a sibling success") all failed on main first (`tests/phase_executor/test_supervisor_launch.py`, `test_enforce.py`). phase_executor 0.2.0→0.3.0. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 4213→4217.

### v3.71.3 (2026-07-20)
- **`step_state_post.detect_marker` recognizes inline `echo`/`printf` notes appends — the statusbar now-pointer no longer stalls on single-line markers (#533).** The `PostToolUse:Bash` hook matched a `### WFn Step X — DONE` marker only at the start of a stripped line, so a single-line `echo '…' >> claude_docs/session_notes.md` (or `printf`) append stripped to a line beginning with `echo`/`printf` and was silently dropped — the now-pointer (`claude_docs/wal/<project>.state.json`) never advanced even though the append itself landed. Recognition is now tied QUOTE-AWARE to a `>>` redirect into a session_notes path (`_tied_notes_append` in `hooks/step_state_post.py`): the marker's own command must redirect into notes (inline) or be a heredoc body line — shell metacharacters (`;`/`|`/`&`) and a `>>` sitting inside the quoted marker detail are treated as data, not command structure. The #499 read-vs-append defense stays closed (a `grep`/`cat` of marker text, and a compound read-plus-unrelated-append, both return `None`), and the per-line `_MARKER_LINE_CAP` ReDoS guard is preserved. Tests: 11 red-before-green cases in `tests/hooks/test_step_state_hook.py` (inline echo/printf incl. metachar details, the quoted-redirect-in-text false-positive, the F1 compound guard, the cap guard, own-line heredoc unchanged, and the full-hook flow). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 3863→3874.

### v3.66.4 (2026-07-19)
- **Capture dirs chmod 0700 — directory-metadata exposure closed, posture matched to specs/registry (#513).** `phase_executor`'s `create_capture` made attempt/run/seat dirs with default umask (typically 0755) while the W4 supervisor's specs/ and registry dirs are explicitly 0700 (surfaced by the #467 Step-11 R2 review; pre-existing since the #424 capture layer). The capture FILES were already 0600 via `atomic_write_text`'s `mkstemp`, so contents (`transport.stdout.txt` with the provider `session_id`) were never world-readable — what 0755 dirs leaked was listing/traversal metadata (run ids, seats, attempt ids, filenames, sizes, mtimes), plus a posture inconsistency and no defense against a future non-mkstemp writer. Now every dir a capture-tree creation CREATES is chmod 0700 via a shared `ensure_private_dir` helper (umask-proof; used by BOTH creation sites — `create_capture` and the supervisor's timeout path, which can be the tree's first writer when a child dies before `create_capture` runs), including the capture root itself when the call created it; pre-existing dirs keep their mode (posture set at creation time). Review hardening: the trailing-space needle excludes `git commit-graph`, a guard-blocked entry match classifies definitively (message prose can never fire another row's non-monotonic jump; the compound-input downstream-stamp suppression is a pinned, documented trade-off), and a conventional branch-cut REBINDS the issue from the branch name (`feature/<n>-…` / `fix/<n>-…`), so a same-session follow-up run stamps the new issue immediately. ### v3.57.0 (2026-07-18)
- **feat(executor): engine-managed git worktree lifecycle outside /tmp + orchestrator-only promotion (#466, epic #475 W3).** New extraction-clean `phase_executor/worktree.py` (no `hooks/` import): a pure planning layer (`resolve_root` enforces roots outside /tmp+$TMPDIR per spike #452, `planned_path` keyed to run/seat/attempt through `contract.canonical_contained_worktree`, `decide_disposition`, age-bounded `select_evictions`, `validate_allowlist`) plus a `WorktreeManager` (injected git runner). The security through-line: NEVER `git -C <worktree>` (a child rewrites the worktree `.git` file) — at create the trusted admin gitdir is discovered via `rev-parse --git-common-dir` + a match under `<common>/worktrees/*/gitdir` (reads only under canonical `.git`, probed on git 2.43), and every inspect/promote runs `--git-dir=<trusted> --work-tree=<wt>`. `create` compensates a post-`worktree add` failure (no orphan checkout); `populate` write-aheads each dest before copy; `finalize` routes to `_clean` (zero residue) or `_retain`, which moves the tree to `.retained/` FIRST then redacts the MOVED tree via `os.walk(followlinks=False)` + `O_NOFOLLOW` (closing the gitignored/committed-secret leak porcelain misses), re-reads+asserts each marker, and surfaces `redaction_failures`/`redaction_incomplete` fail-closed; a persistent retention-index feeds the W4 reaper and `pressure` blocks new creates. `promote` builds an immutable candidate tree (temp index in `.meta`), refuses a stale base ("base stale — rebase" — so a candidate can never silently revert peer commits), commits parented on the real base, and CAS-updates the ref. Structural guarantees hold ONLY for an OS-confined codex seat; mutating-claude stays W7-blocked (#465 contract). Design converged on the 2nd volume loop-back (iteration 3, 25-entry disposition ledger across two design passes; CF-1 silent-revert and CF-2 redaction-enumeration both live-probed this host); Step-8a on the 4 high-risk tasks (2 opus reviewers per wave) fixed a laundered `tree_differs`/gitignore comment, a wasted inspect diff, a porcelain `u`-record mis-split, and an unreadable-file teardown strand; Step-11 (3 opus reviewers) fixed a redaction-regex ReDoS + a child-planted-FIFO teardown DoS + over-redaction of benign files, made `promote` fail loud on an unreadable path, guarded the hardlink-truncation vector, and dropped a non-durable `live_identities` field from the reaper contract. Live cells are exercised in W9 #472. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 3576+10skip→3634+10skip.

### v3.40.0 (2026-07-15)
- **WF5/WF2 — settled-disposition ledger injected into pass-N adversarial reviews (#393, epic #408).** Multi-pass gates stop re-litigating settled ground: the WF2 orchestrator persists each Critical/High finding's TERMINAL disposition (`adopted | declined | dissolved` — deferrals stay in `deferrals.json`) to `claude_docs/.wf2-state/<issue>/dispositions.jsonl` at every gate close (`plan_lib` gains `append_disposition`/`read_dispositions`/`fold_dispositions`/`compute_finding_key`/`strip_reopens`; identity = the engine dedupe tuple sha256 with `category` deliberately excluded so relabeling can't dodge the backstop; tolerant per-line binary reader — one bad byte costs one line, never the ledger). Pass-N dispatches fold the ledger to a 0600 temp copy and add `--dispositions <temp> --issue <n>`; the engine re-validates, renders one escaped line per entry (newlines → literal `\n`; C0/C1 + U+2028/U+2029 stripped), caps at 20KB (most-recent kept, truncation loud on stderr), and injects a SECOND independent nonce fence plus a no-re-litigation instruction (`REOPENS <id>:` keeps legitimate reopens open; artifact text claiming "settled" is named as spoof) — both backends share the prompt; no flag → byte-identical pre-#393 prompt, pinned against a committed pre-change golden. Split fail policy: benign failures fail OPEN (`ledger: degraded (<reason>, N lines skipped)`, empty ledger honestly `ledger: empty`), `--issue` mismatch fails CLOSED exit 6 → WF2 loud-abort marker `failed (ledger integrity)`. Steps 4/6/11 wire gate-close persistence, the dispatch sequence, and the join backstop (DECLINED/DISSOLVED key match auto-dissolves as re-litigation; ADOPTED match surfaces as `possible failed remediation`, never auto-dissolved). 8a dual reviews on the 3 high-risk tasks: 1 High fixed red-first (text-mode UnicodeDecodeError dropped the whole ledger) + 5 cheap adopts. Step-11 (3 reviewers + cross-model diff pass that live-dogfooded `--dispositions` on its own diff) caught and fixed 4 more red-first: the no-re-raise instruction wrongly covered ADOPTED entries (starving the failed-remediation backstop), fold order evicted the most-recently-re-decided identity under the cap, the gate-close writer accepted malformed records the next pass would drop, and the untracked design doc (now committed as md+html pair). - **Multi-pass gate counting rule + merged-gate `reviewer_kind` precedence (#340, epic #333).** Two documented-and-drift-guarded telemetry semantics decisions close the `gates[]` honesty gap: (1) for a multi-pass gate, `findings` counts UNIQUE findings across all passes (identity = same artifact location AND same required change) and `resolved` counts findings whose FINAL disposition at gate close is terminal (applied / fixed-in-gate / refuted-with-cited-evidence / dropped-by-band — band-drops count in both), with the deduped pair computed at gate close and persisted in the gate's session-note evidence so Step 16 / WF3 Step 14 assembly reads, never re-derives; (2) a merged gate's `reviewer_kind` stays a single value recording the gate-DEFINING mechanism — the additive opt-in adversarial layer is skippable by contract and never changes it (Step 4/6 → `inline`, Step 11 → `hand_rolled_multi`; the `→ codex` mapping is scoped to a gate whose SOLE mechanism is codex). Canonical prose + worked example land in `skills/implement-feature/references/run-record.md`, WF3 `references/steps.md` §14 points there, `docs/run-records.md` gains a `### Gate counting + merged-gate reviewer_kind (#340)` subsection, and the WF14 rubric's gate-count-honesty and reviewer_kind-fidelity weak-spot checks now audit against the two rules (legacy per-pass sums = `known-limitation`). No validator change — shape untouched. Tests: 6 new red-before-green drift guards across the WF2/WF3/run-feedback clarity suites. No workflow-spine change (telemetry-assembly semantics prose only) → no diagram REV. Suite 2534+1skip→2540+1skip.
### v3.28.0 (2026-07-09)
- **Issue-keyed step-marker contract for cross-run marker attribution (#341, epic #333).** Step markers previously carried no issue-scoping, so multiple runs interleaving in the same `claude_docs/session_notes.md` (or a resumed run) couldn't tell which marker belonged to which issue. This introduces a canonical per-marker-type key contract — 5 marker classes, each keyed by its own canonical slot — captured as an AUTHORITATIVE slot table plus an emitter caution and the declared deferrals (what remains unkeyed and why) in both the WF2 and WF3 `<step-tracking>` blocks. 14 prescribed literals across the two workflows now carry the key (12 base literals plus the Step 4-discard marker and the Step 6 adversarial-review marker surfaced by the #331/8a review). `format_promotion_note` (`hooks/plan_lib.py`) gains an `issue` kwarg, TDD'd red-before-green, backward-compatible via a `None` default so existing non-keyed callers are unaffected. Consumer rules are wired run-scoped at every read site: WF2 `state-and-resume`'s `MARKERS_COMPLETE` check, WF3's `§Workflow Resumption`, and WF14 `run-feedback`'s both-shapes attribution (keyed and legacy unkeyed markers both parse). Tests: 15 new red-before-green drift guards and unit tests across the WF2/WF3 clarity suites and `test_plan_lib.py` pinning the slot table, the 14 keyed literals, and the `issue` kwarg's default behavior. No workflow-spine change (marker prose + emitter kwarg only, no station/gate/loop-back delta) → no diagram REV. Suite 2518+1skip→2533+1skip.
### v3.27.1 (2026-07-09)
- **WF3 Step 9 per-slot three-tier review-gate fallback chain + dead-return detection (#331, epic #333).** Step 9's reviewer dispatch gains a declared per-slot fallback chain — tier1 pr-review-toolkit named agent, tier2 rawgentic-reviewer substitute, tier3 generic inline reviewer — that never collapses two review slots into one, and gives both-slots-tier-2 distinct briefs so a double fallback still reviews from two angles. A vacuous subagent return (empty body, `confirmedCount: 0`) is now detected as DEAD rather than trusted: the dispatch relaunches once, and a second death raises `REVIEW_DISPATCH_FAILED` and runs the ERROR protocol; a mid-tier runtime error retries once before descending to the next tier — two new named failure modes. The WF3 resolution table is reworked so `tier1=primary`, `tier2=fallback` (the first real producer), `tier3=generic`, with descent emission split by trigger (a resolve-failure descent emits no line for the tier that never ran; a runtime-error descent adds the abandoned tier's terminal line `outcome=error` with that tier's own resolution value). The shared `:40` fallback-row block is reworded to match and re-synced. WF2 gets the same dead-return rule ported into its 8a item 7 and Step 11 item 2 reviewer dispatch sites. Tests: 6 new red-before-green drift guards across the WF2/WF3 clarity suites pinning the tier chain, the dead-return relaunch-then-`REVIEW_DISPATCH_FAILED` rule, and the reworked resolution table. WF3 diagram REV 3.27.1 (Step 9 gate delta). Suite 2512+1skip→2518+1skip.

### v3.26.0 (2026-07-09)
- **Structured `dispatches[]` run-record field + aggregate rollup (#329, epic #309).** Run-records had no structured record of the subagent dispatches inside a run — the run-feedback skill (#337) named this a standing weak spot, auditing `reviewer_kind` fidelity by eye each time. New optional `dispatches` array in the run-record schema (`hooks/work_summary.py::validate_record`), present-optional and present-is-strict per the #155/#156 precedent (an absent field costs nothing; a present-but-malformed one fails closed). Each entry carries `role` (`DISPATCH_ROLES`), `subagent_type`, `model`, `effort`, and two orthogonal enums: `outcome` (`ok`/`error`/`retried`/`dead` — `dead` names the #331 vacuous-return case explicitly, `DISPATCH_OUTCOMES`) and `resolution` (`primary`/`fallback`/`generic`, `DISPATCH_RESOLUTIONS`) — outcome and resolution are independent axes, not one collapsed enum, so "the primary reviewer died and a generic fallback covered it" is representable. `aggregate` emits a `dispatches` key **only when ≥1 loaded record carries the field** (never a zero-filled phantom section on old stores): counts `by_role`/`by_model` (null model renders `"(none)"`, never dropped), `dead_rate` by outcome, `fallback_rate` by resolution, and `runs_with_dispatches`; partitions correctly under `--group-by`. The human-readable renderer gains a `### Dispatches` section, omitted entirely under the same no-data rule. Documented in [run-records.md](docs/run-records.md) (schema table + rollup semantics + worked example). Schema and rollup only — no workflow assembles or emits `dispatches` yet; wiring WF2/WF3 Step 16 to populate it is follow-up #330. Tests: red-before-green schema validator cases (absent/valid/malformed), aggregate rollup cases (zero-record omission, null-model bucketing, mixed outcome/resolution counts), and render-section cases in `tests/hooks/test_work_summary.py`. ### v3.24.25 (2026-07-09)
- **Escape project names in the Gate-2 cross-project deny JSON to close a fail-open (#325).** `hooks/wal-bind-guard`'s Gate-2 cross-project deny built its response with a raw heredoc that interpolated `${BOUND_PROJECT}`/`${VIOLATION}` unescaped, so a `"` or `\` in a project name malformed the deny JSON, the runtime dropped the hook output, and a cross-project write Gate 2 meant to deny silently fail-opened; `VIOLATION` is read unvalidated from the workspace `.name` field, so a crafted/hand-edited name reached the builder. The deny is now built with `jq -cn --arg` (the exact construction the Gate-1 deny already uses at `wal-bind-guard:94`, shipped in #318); the allow/deny decision and the documented jq-missing fail-open are byte-for-byte unchanged (AC2/AC4) — only the response construction differs. Guard core deny logic unchanged; adversarial-review (cross-model, codex) folded 4 spec-clarity findings into the fix. Tests: red-before-green `test_multi_active_deny_message_names_tool_and_path` + an empty-path deny regression in `tests/hooks/test_wal_bind_guard.py`. ### v3.24.19 (2026-07-08)
- **wal-guard deny() no longer fails open on huge commands (#310).** `deny()` passed the full blocked command as a single jq exec argument; over Linux `MAX_ARG_STRLEN` (~128KiB per argument) the jq exec failed (E2BIG, rc 126), no deny JSON reached stdout, and the deliberately fail-closed guard silently ALLOWED the command (pre-existing; found by the #267 R2 review, reproduced red-first with a 300KB command). The embedded command is now bounded at deny() entry via pure bash parameter expansion — a first-cut `printf|head` pipe died of SIGPIPE under the script's pipefail and failed open again, caught red-first — bounding both the decision JSON and the headless GUARD_BLOCK audit line; truncation is visible in the reason (`[truncated: total N chars]`), never silent. Review hardening (cross-model adversarial High + silent-failure hunter): the decision jq call gains a static printf-builtin **fallback deny** — ANY serializer failure (not just E2BIG) previously meant empty stdout = allow — and the audit path's `ts=$(date …)` is guarded so no audit step can abort the deny under `set -e`. 5 tests red-before-green. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2340+1skip→2345+1skip.

No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2358→2363.

### v3.24.11 (2026-07-08)
- **session-start: python3 spawns bounded, dead archive path removed (#269, epic #278).** Startup spawned python3 once per session-notes file plus a dead two-spawn archive-context section whose helper script was deleted back in #57 (C12+C13); consolidations: the notes-size handler accepts many files in ONE invocation (per-file failures isolated inside — red-before-green delta test pins that 1 vs 6 notes files spawn python3 identically); the dead section is gone (structural pin); the four inline workspace parses are ONE python3 emitting a single parseable state line, with the registry project name passed via argv instead of interpolated into code (removes a latent injection surface); the two post_update_reconcile invocations are ONE `--session-start` combined pass (each sub-pass isolated, fail-open, behavior pinned); the jq-fallback stdin parse is one spawn instead of three, with values transported as shlex-quoted assignments (a Codex diff-review catch: the first draft's newline-delimited transport let a newline inside `cwd` silently shift `session_id` — now pinned by test); and the migration registry-merge snippet now routes through `atomic_write_lib.atomic_write_text`, closing the docstring's documented exclusion (structural no-mkstemp pin added). Folded #268 follow-up: wal-context absolutizes its in-process `WAL_PROJECT_PATH` to match the resolver's shape (registry format unchanged). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2350→2358.

### v3.24.10 (2026-07-08)
- **wal-bind-guard: bound-session jq cost 6→5 (#268, epic #278).** Two consolidations: `wal_resolve_project` reads the registry's `project`+`project_path` in ONE jq (the #266 `@sh` pattern), and the guard's own-project check + cross-project violation scan run in ONE jq. The first draft fast-pathed on the registry's `project_path` — a Step 11 reviewer refuted it live (Medium): a stale or hand-edited registry entry (`project: alpha, project_path: ./projects/beta`) flipped the old deny into an allow, since `project_path` is unvalidated registry text (#265 validates only the name). The shipped scan derives the bound project's path from the WORKSPACE entry for the validated name — the registry's `project_path` decides nothing. Red-before-green: jq-spawn-count (6→5), cross-project deny with `project_path` present, and the inconsistent-registry deny. wal-lib also strips a leading `./` when absolutizing `project_path` (cosmetic normalization; one test had pinned the accidental `/./` form — pin updated). Named delta: nested project dirs (unsupported, none exist) allow via the own-check where the plain scan denied. No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2347→2350.

### v3.24.9 (2026-07-07)
- **wal-guard: one combined pattern pass on the hot allow-path (#267, epic #278).** `_check_patterns` ran 12 per-pattern greps per tool call (review finding C9); a precomputed union of all 12 EREs (shell-expansion join, no subprocess) now runs as ONE `grep -qiE` pre-filter — no match means allow, done. A match falls through to the unchanged per-pattern loop, which alone decides (active-set filter, ansible exclusion, per-rule reason), so deny/allow decisions and reasons are byte-identical — sound because every pattern shares `-i` and the union of ALL patterns is a superset of any active subset's. Clean-command grep spawns 13→2 (red-before-green PATH-shim count test); 26 parametrized allow/deny cases pin decision parity, and a 600-iteration reviewer fuzz found 0 superset breaks. Step 11 catch shipped with it: only a clean no-match (rc 1) takes the fast path — a grep ERROR (rc ≥ 2, e.g. a future malformed pattern edit) falls through to the per-pattern loop instead of silently allowing, keeping the guard fail-closed (red-before-green shim test). No workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2345→2347.

### v3.24.8 (2026-07-07)
- **One jq parse of hook stdin per WAL event (#266, epic #278).** `wal_parse_fields()` spawned four separate jq processes per tool call (the WAL hot path's dominant cost, review finding C8+C11); it now runs ONE jq that emits all four field assignments through jq's `@sh` filter, applied with `eval` — every value arrives single-quoted, so quotes, newlines, and command substitution in field values stay inert (test-enforced, including a marker-file injection probe). The filter binds to exactly one input document (a Codex adversarial diff-review catch: a multi-document stdin previously let a trailing document's assignment group win via eval — now rejected like malformed input), empty stdin stays rc-0 tolerated, defaults are pre-set so malformed stdin yields the same sentinels as missing fields, and `set -e` exit semantics match the old code path (pinned by test from the convergent 8a review note). All 7 WAL hooks route through the one function; red-before-green spawn-count (4→1), malformed-defaults, and multi-document tests. Step 11 also surfaced two wal-guard hardening fixes shipped here: parse failure no longer aborts the fail-closed guard (`|| true` + sentinels keep pattern-matching at strict), and `deny()` builds its decision with `jq -n` (piping stdin through the constant filter emitted duplicate deny objects on multi-document input — unparseable = allow). End-to-end guard test pins multi-document stdin → deny. Suite 2336→2345.

### v3.22.1 (2026-07-06)
- **Refuted the security-guidance "double-block loop" claim; relaxed the recommendation (#119, epic #247).** `security-guard-check.sh` claimed the official `security-guidance` plugin "uses a broken blocking mechanism that auto-retries, causing a double-block loop" (never verified — flagged in PR #117's review). **Investigation (primary-source, dispositive):** the official plugin v2.0.6 registers `SessionStart`/`UserPromptSubmit`/`PostToolUse`/`Stop` — **no PreToolUse hook**; rawgentic's guard IS PreToolUse (`permissionDecision: deny`). They hook different lifecycle events, so a PreToolUse double-block is structurally impossible; the official plugin's `exit(2)` is a PostToolUse/Stop **supplementary** asyncRewake review (its own `CONTINUATION_SUFFIX` says so), not a tool-call retry. **Verdict: REFUTED.** The SessionStart notice is **relaxed** — disabling security-guidance is now framed as **optional (redundant, not conflicting)**, not required; record-once + "keep both" opt-out unchanged. Findings report committed (`docs/reviews/119-…md` + rendered `.html`); drift guard pins the corrected wording (`tests/hooks/test_security_guard_check.py` +1). Allowed ops log an stderr warning; Gate 1 (unbound) unchanged. `tests/hooks/test_wal_bind_guard.py` +10, `conftest.make_workspace` gains `workspace_fields`, `docs/config-reference.md` documents the field. ### v3.21.0 (2026-07-06)
- **Multi-store / workspace fleet aggregation for run-records (#115, epic #247).** `work_summary.py aggregate` read exactly one `--store`, so a workspace's telemetry (fragmented across N per-project stores) had no pooled view — the N-starvation keeping #94's metrics "directional." Now: **repeatable `--store a --store b`** and a **`--workspace <path>`** mode that resolves every ACTIVE project's default store from `.rawgentic_workspace.json` and pools them. Records are **origin-tagged at load** (`_source`, read-side — no schema change) so the new **`--group-by source`** slices the fleet by project. **Cross-store fail-closed:** a missing/unreadable store among several (or in `--workspace` mode) is skipped with a visible stderr warning + a `missing_stores` count in `--json`, still aggregating the rest — while a single explicitly-named missing `--store` still exits 2 (single-store parity). Builds on #116 (canonical gate names) so the pooled `names`/`scanner_skip_freq` columns read clean. `hooks/work_summary.py` gains `load_stores` + `stores_from_workspace`; `tests/hooks/test_work_summary.py` +14; `docs/run-records.md` documents the fleet flags. Step-11 review hardened it: an active project with a malformed `path` is surfaced (not silently dropped), and `--workspace` + `--store` together errors rather than ignoring `--store`. Not a workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2239→2253.

### v3.20.2 (2026-07-06)
- **pylint CI lane for production Python (#40, epic #247).** New `.github/workflows/lint.yml` runs pylint on `hooks/*.py` + `tests/` (push to `main` + PRs to `main`, Python 3.12, standard actions — no OAuth). A hook runs on every prompt/tool-call, so a bug there could silently break security enforcement across all projects. The gate is scoped to the **ERROR class** plus two targeted warnings — `--disable=all --enable=E,unreachable,f-string-without-interpolation` with `--disable=import-error` (hook runtime deps aren't installed in CI) — so it catches real bugs (undefined variables, f-string errors, type mismatches, unreachable code) but passes on the current tree with **no code churn** (the issue's bare `--disable=import-error` alone would drown in convention/refactor noise and never go green — a documented deviation; `--errors-only` is avoided because it would render the two extra warning checks inert). Verified red-before-green (a broken file trips it, exit 6). Drift-guarded by `tests/test_lint_workflow.py` (+6). Not a workflow-spine change → no diagram REV. Suite 2233→2239.
